FILE cex-cm02-nonunique-flow-for-a-continuous-force
Consider the framework's equation of motion with one label of unit mass
($m=1$, [[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)), time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$, and a force map
$F:\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3}\times\mathbb R^{3}\to\mathbb R^{3}$ that is
**merely continuous** — that is, the standing hypotheses of clause (iii) of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with the state-local-Lipschitz requirement
dropped. Then for every initial datum $(t_{0},x_{0},v_{0})$ there is at most
one motion of the model through that datum, on every interval containing
$t_{0}$.

FILE cex-cm02-singular-collision-finite-time
---
id: cex-cm02-singular-collision-finite-time
kind: counterexample
title: "A singular collision in finite time: the inverse-square external model has a motion whose maximal interval is bounded above"
status: draft

domain: physics
dependency_level: 13

  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "A refutation, inside the model, of the claim that every motion of the inverse-square external-potential model exists for all real times: for one label of mass $m>0$ in the external potential $U(x)=-k/|x|$ on the collision-excluded domain, an explicit radial motion with the stated initial data solves the model on $(-\\infty,T)$ and has no extension past the finite time $T$, at which the position reaches the excluded center and the speed is unbounded. The item refutes only the all-time-existence claim for this model, illustrates the escape alternative of the breakdown criterion, and asserts no motion of any physical body, no measurement and no empirical claim."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 1.2, printed pages 2-5 (PDF pages 8-11): the equation of motion and the role of the force law's domain; the two-body reduction with the attractive potential -kappa/|r| discussed in Section 2.3-2.4"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Section 1.2, chapter page 4 (PDF page 8), and Section 3.4: the inverse-square gravity law on the collision-free domain and its radial consequences, including the singularity of the potential at the center"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "G. Teschl, Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical Systems (AMS Graduate Studies in Mathematics 140, online edition), Section 2.6, printed pages 50-53: a maximal solution with finite right endpoint leaves every compact subset of its domain"
      url: "https://schecter.math.ncsu.edu/ma_732_sp13/teschl_ode.pdf"

---
Let $m>0$ and $k>0$. Consider the force model of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] for one label with time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, collision-excluded configuration domain
$Q=\mathbb R^{3}\setminus\{0\}$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]] clause (a)), mass
$m$, and the external potential
[L4] If a maximal motion has finite right endpoint $b$ and its state stays in
a compact subset of the open state domain near $b$, it extends past $b$
([[pthm-cm02-continuation]] clause 2); in particular, a motion that reaches a
configuration excluded from $Q$ at a finite time cannot be extended beyond
it, because a motion's values must lie in $Q$ at every time of its interval
([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]] clause (c)).
4.1 Escape consistent with the breakdown criterion: as $t\uparrow T$ one has $\lvert\dot r(t)\rvert=\frac{2r_{0}}{3T}(1-t/T)^{-1/3}\to+\infty$ while $r(t)\to0$; the state $(x(t),\dot x(t))$ therefore leaves every compact subset of $U=Q\times\mathbb R^{3}$ near $T$, in the two ways predicted by [[pthm-cm02-continuation]] clause 4: the velocity is unbounded and the position approaches the excluded configuration. The refuted all-time-existence statement is false for this model. [step 3.1, L4, algebra] ∎
- **Singular collision.** The motion falls radially into the force center and
  reaches it at the finite time $T$; the model, which excludes the
  configuration $x=0$ from its domain, has no dynamics past that event. This
  is a computation inside the model, not a claim about the fate of a physical
  body near a point mass: an actual system would require a different model
  (for instance a finite-size body, or a modified short-distance force) across
  the regime where the idealization fails.

FILE cex-cm03-centrality-is-not-necessary-for-angular-momentum-conservation
1.1 The model. Take $N=3$, masses $m_{1}=m_{2}=m_{3}:=1\,\mathrm{kg}$, time window $\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q:=\mathbb R^{9}$ (no exclusion is needed), no external forces, and the constant pair force maps $$F_{12}\equiv w,\qquad F_{13}\equiv-w,\qquad F_{23}\equiv w,\qquad w:=1\,\mathrm N\,e_{3},$$ with $F_{ji}:=-F_{ij}$ for every ordered pair. Constant maps are continuous and state-Lipschitz with constant $0$, the antisymmetry $F_{ji}=-F_{ij}$ holds by declaration, and the presentation is $F_{i}=0+\sum_{j\ne i}F_{ij}$; hence the model satisfies the hypotheses of the force branch and of [[post-cm02-interactions]] clause (i) with the antisymmetry clause (ii)1. [given, L1]
2.2 The internal torque sum vanishes identically. Using bilinearity and $u\times w=-(w\times u)$ [L4], $$I=\sum_{i<j}(x_{i}-x_{j})\times F_{ij}=(x_{1}-x_{2})\times w+(x_{1}-x_{3})\times(-w)+(x_{2}-x_{3})\times w=-w\times\bigl[(x_{1}-x_{2})-(x_{1}-x_{3})+(x_{2}-x_{3})\bigr],$$ and the bracket telescopes: $(x_{1}-x_{2})-(x_{1}-x_{3})+(x_{2}-x_{3})=x_{1}-x_{2}-x_{1}+x_{3}+x_{2}-x_{3}=0$. Hence $I\equiv0$ on the whole configuration domain, without any centrality. The individual pair torques are nevertheless nonzero: at $t=0$, $(x_{1}-x_{2})\times F_{12}=e_{2}$, $(x_{1}-x_{3})\times F_{13}=e_{1}$ and $(x_{2}-x_{3})\times F_{23}=-(e_{1}+e_{2})$, whose sum is zero; so the vanishing is by cancellation among the pairs. [step 1.1, L4, algebra]
3.1 The pair forces are not central. The pair force $F_{12}=w=e_{3}$ is parallel to $x_{1}-x_{2}$ only if $(x_{1}-x_{2})\times e_{3}=0$; but $$(x_{1}-x_{2})\times F_{12}=(te_{2}-e_{1})\times e_{3}=t\,(e_{2}\times e_{3})-(e_{1}\times e_{3})=t\,e_{1}+e_{2}\neq0$$ for every $t$, since the two components do not vanish simultaneously; the same computation at one time instant shows the failure for the constant model at generic configurations. Hence centrality fails along the whole motion (and $F_{12}$ fails it for every configuration with $(x_{1}-x_{2})\times e_{3}\neq0$). [step 2.1, L4, algebra]
- **The cancellation is structural.** The telescoping identity of step 2.2 uses only the constancy of the declared pair forces and the assignment $F_{12}\equiv w$, $F_{13}\equiv-w$, $F_{23}\equiv w$; it holds for every configuration, not only along the exhibited motion. The motion is used to exhibit nonzero individual pair torques and a nonzero conserved angular momentum.

In the force branch of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with the potential
presentation of [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]] clause (a):
**if a model's force field is time- and velocity-independent and closed
($\operatorname{curl} F=0$, equivalently $\partial_{j}F_{k}=\partial_{k}F_{j}$)
on its configuration domain, then the model is a global potential model; in
particular there is a global potential energy $U$ on the domain with
$F=-\nabla U$, the mechanical energy $K+U$ exists for every motion, and it is
conserved.**
[L1] Framework notions: a motion of the model satisfies
$m\ddot x=F(t,x,\dot x)$ ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]); a field
$\varphi:Q\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ is closed when
$\partial_{k}\varphi_{j}=\partial_{j}\varphi_{k}$ and exact with potential
energy $U$ when $\varphi=-\nabla U$; on a star-shaped open domain every closed
$C^{1}$ field is exact with the radial potential of the Poincaré lemma
([[def-closed-and-exact-c1-vector-fields]],
[[thm-poincare-lemma-for-star-shaped-domains]],
[[def-star-shaped-open-subset-of-rn]],
[[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]); dimensions are [[def-cm01-units]].
1.1 The model. Take $N=1$, a mass $m:=1\,\mathrm{kg}$, time window $\mathbb R$, the open **annular** configuration domain $$Q:=\Bigl\{x\in\mathbb R^{3}:\ \tfrac12\,\mathrm m<\sqrt{x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}}<2\,\mathrm m\Bigr\},$$ and the declared parameter $\beta:=1\,\mathrm J$; the force map is the time- and velocity-independent external contribution $$F(t,x,v)=\beta\,\Phi(x),\qquad \Phi(x):=\frac{(-x_{2},\,x_{1},\,0)}{x_{1}^{2}+x_{2}^{2}},$$ of force dimension $\mu\ell\tau^{-2}$ ([[def-cm01-units]]). $Q$ is open and does not meet the axis, and $F$ is smooth on $Q$. [given, L1]
4.1 Local potentials and local energy. Let $p\in Q$ and let $$\varepsilon:=\frac12\min\bigl\{r(p)-\tfrac12,\ 2-r(p)\bigr\}>0,\qquad B:=\{z:\lvert z-p\rvert<\varepsilon\}$$ (in metres); for $z\in B$ one has $r(z)<r(p)+\varepsilon<2$ and $r(z)>r(p)-\varepsilon>\frac12$, so $B\subseteq Q$. $B$ is open and star-shaped with respect to its centre ([[def-star-shaped-open-subset-of-rn]]), so the Poincaré lemma of [L1] provides a $C^{2}$ potential $U_{B}:B\to\mathbb R$ with $F|_{B}=-\nabla U_{B}$. The restriction of the model's force map to the configuration domain $B$ is then a global potential model with a time-independent potential and no residual forces, so clause 2 of [[pthm-cm03-energy-balance]] gives that $K+U_{B}$ is constant along every motion of the restricted model, hence along every subinterval of a motion of the annular model on which the position remains in $B$. [step 3.1, L1, L4, algebra]

FILE cex-cm15-deflection-need-not-decrease
[L1] Rule consistency and existence of the broken straight motion ([[post-cm15-impulse]] clauses (i)-(ii) for the contact-law format, [[def-cm15-asymptotic-data]] clauses (ii)-(iii) for the deflection vocabulary, [[def-cross-product-in-r3]], [[def-euclidean-inner-product]]): with the tangential velocity component and the energy preserved at the interface, the inside velocity obtains by Snell's law $\sin\alpha=n\sin\beta$ at the entry and, by the symmetry of the chord, the exit is the mirror configuration; the inside radial kinetic energy at $r=a$ is $\frac\mu2(v_{\mathrm{in}}^2-v_\infty^2b^2/a^2)=\frac\mu2v_\infty^2(n^2-b^2/a^2)>0$ for $b<a$. The outgoing radial energy is $\frac\mu2v_\infty^2(1-b^2/a^2)>0$, so the label reaches the second intersection and transmits without internal reflection.

FILE cex-cm15-momentum-alone-does-not-fix-the-impact
**Given:** The collinear isolated-impact model with masses $m_1=2\ \mathrm{kg}$, $m_2=6\ \mathrm{kg}$ and incoming velocities $v_1^-=3n\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-1}}$, $v_2^-=-1n\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-1}}$ along the contact direction; the two candidate outcomes of the Counterexample below.
2.2 Outcome (b), elastic: $v_1^+=-3n$, $v_2^+=+1n$. Then $m_1(v_1^+-v_1^-)=2(-6)n=-12n$ and $m_2(v_2^+-v_2^-)=6(+2)n=+12n$; the impulse is $J=-12n\ \mathrm{N\,s}$ and the total momentum is $0=P^-$. [step 1.1, L1, algebra]
3.1 Both outcomes satisfy the reciprocal impulse equations of [L1], with different impulses ($-6n$ versus $-12n$), and the same given incoming data; their post-impact velocities differ ($0$ versus $-3n$ and $+1n$). Hence the impulse equations alone do not fix the post-impact velocities, and the false statement fails. [step 2.1, step 2.2, L1, algebra]

physical_scope: "A refutation of the claim that every real coefficient of restitution is admissible in the adopted isolated-impact model: with the collinear restitution law and $e=2$ on declared data, the kinetic energy rises by $3\\,\\mathrm{J}$ across an impact while the configuration is instantaneously unchanged, so the total mechanical energy of a conservative two-label model is not conserved; values $e>1$ therefore signal an omitted internal energy reservoir and are excluded from the conservative contact model of this page, which restricts $e$ to $[0,1]$. The witness uses declared model data and asserts no measurement."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "Jacob Moore and Contributors, Mechanics Map (Pennsylvania State University, 2nd ed.), Engineering LibreTexts, §11.4: the coefficient of restitution between 0 and 1, with the remark that values above 1 would describe a superelastic collision in which additional stored energy is released"

[L2] Configurational continuity ([[post-cm05-central-model]] clause (i), [[def-cm01-units]], [[def-interval]]): the impact is instantaneous and the positions are continuous, so $\lvert x_1-x_2\rvert$ and hence $U=u(\lvert x_1-x_2\rvert)$ are unchanged across the impact; the masses and $u$ are model data.
4.1 Potential energy: by [L2] the configuration, hence $U$, is unchanged across the instant of the impact: $U(t_*^+)=U(t_*^-)$. Therefore the total mechanical energy jumps by $\Delta(K+U)=\Delta K=+3\ \mathrm{J}>0$ across the impact. [step 3.1, L2, algebra]
- **What the witness isolates.** The obstruction is not the momentum account — the exhibited outcome conserves total momentum — but the energy account: an instantaneous contact leaves the configuration, hence $U$, unchanged, so any kinetic-energy gain is a total-energy gain. The same computation shows that the page's model cannot represent superelastic contacts without extending the model by an internal-energy term.

FILE cex-cm17-traction-symmetry-without-stress-matrix
---
id: cex-cm17-traction-symmetry-without-stress-matrix
kind: counterexample
title: "FALSE: the reversed-normal convention alone makes a continuous traction field a stress matrix. The field t(n)=|n_1|n refutes it"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: mathematics
dependency_level: 1
deps: [def-cm-traction-and-stress, def-matrix-space, def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix, def-euclidean-spheres-and-closed-balls, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-continuity-real, thm-algebra-of-limits, lem-every-norm-on-rn-is-continuous-for-the-euclidean-metric]
dependency_roles:
  def-cm-traction-and-stress: mathematical-premise
  def-matrix-space: mathematical-premise
  def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-spheres-and-closed-balls: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-inner-product: mathematical-premise
  def-continuity-real: mathematical-premise
  thm-algebra-of-limits: mathematical-premise
  lem-every-norm-on-rn-is-continuous-for-the-euclidean-metric: mathematical-premise
proof_strategy: direct
provenance:
  statement: ai-generated
  proof: ai-generated
generation:
  role: counterexample
sources:
  references:
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Section 4.3 (Cauchy's hypothesis and the need for force balance in the tetrahedron argument)"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
    - title: "MIT OpenCourseWare 12.005, Applications of Continuum Mechanics, Lecture 3 (Cauchy tetrahedron and Cauchy's formula t = sigma n)"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/12-005-applications-of-continuum-mechanics-to-earth-atmospheric-and-planetary-sciences-spring-2006/5005ac6aec16d031bffbd361c792ca70_lec3.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
The false statement is: *every continuous traction field
$t:S^2\to\mathbb R^3$ satisfying the reversed-normal convention
$t(-n)=-t(n)$ for every $n\in S^2$ is represented by a matrix
$\sigma\in M_3(\mathbb R)$ with $t(n)=\sigma n$ for every $n$.*
The field constructed below is continuous and satisfies the convention, and
inherits Cauchy's hypothesis (it depends on the surface only through the unit
normal), but no matrix represents it. The refutation shows that the balance
hypothesis of the Cauchy-tetrahedron theorem is essential: symmetry of the
traction in the normal alone is not enough.
[F1] Traction and representation ([[def-cm-traction-and-stress]], [[def-matrix-space]], [[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]]): a matrix $\sigma$ represents $t$ when $t(n)=\sigma n$ for every $n\in S^2$; the columns of $\sigma$ are then determined by $\sigma e_j=t(e_j)$; and $t(-n)=-t(n)$ is the reversed-normal convention, which is a property and not a definition.
- The counterexample isolates the missing hypothesis: the Cauchy-tetrahedron
  theorem derives the matrix from the **balance** of contact forces on
  shrinking regions, not from the symmetry of the traction in the normal.
  The field above is not balanced on all admissible regions; by the cited
  theorem, the balance condition must fail for it, and continuity plus the
  reversed-normal convention are insufficient on their own.
- The physical reading is the corresponding warning for the model page: the
  representation of contact forces by a stress matrix is a consequence of the
  adopted balance principles, not of the format of the traction field, and a
  model whose contact forces violate the balance hypotheses lies outside the
  Cauchy continuum framework.


FILE cex-cm18-finite-sample-may-miss-the-signal


FILE def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric
---
id: def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric
kind: definition
title: "Constrained configurations, the mass matrix and the mass metric"
status: draft
origin: pipeline
domain: mathematics
dependency_level: 0
deps: [def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart, def-derivation-at-a-point-and-tangent-space, def-differential-of-a-smooth-map, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix, def-bilinear-symmetric-skew-and-alternating-forms, def-smooth-manifold]
dependency_roles:
  def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart: mathematical-premise
  def-derivation-at-a-point-and-tangent-space: mathematical-premise
  def-differential-of-a-smooth-map: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-inner-product: mathematical-premise
  def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix: mathematical-premise
  def-bilinear-symmetric-skew-and-alternating-forms: mathematical-premise
  def-smooth-manifold: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, 2015), Section 2.1 (constraints, generalized coordinates) and Section 3.1 (configuration space)"
      url: "https://www.damtp.cam.ac.uk/user/tong/dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Chapter 3"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
Let $N\ge1$ and let $n=3N$. Identify $\mathbb R^{3N}$ with the $N$-fold product $(\mathbb R^3)^N$ and write a point as $x=(x_1,\dots,x_N)$ with $x_i\in\mathbb R^3$; the Euclidean inner product on $\mathbb R^{3N}$ is
$$\langle x,y\rangle_{3N}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\langle x_i,y_i\rangle_2,$$
$$X:Q\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{3N}$$
be a smooth map. The manifold $Q$ is the **configuration space** and $X$ is the **configuration map**; the pair $(Q,X)$ is called a **particle configuration** when $Q$ is an embedded submanifold of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ and $X$ is the inclusion ([[def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart]]), and a **parametrized configuration** otherwise. For $q\in Q$ the differential $dX_q:T_qQ\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ is the differential of [[def-differential-of-a-smooth-map]] ([[def-derivation-at-a-point-and-tangent-space]] for the tangent space).
Let $m_1,\dots,m_N>0$ be **masses**. The **mass matrix** is the linear map $M:\mathbb R^{3N}\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ acting on each block by $M(x_1,\dots,x_N)=(m_1x_1,\dots,m_Nx_N)$; its matrix in the standard basis is block diagonal with blocks $m_iI_3$ ([[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]]).
The **mass metric** of $(Q,X,m_1,\dots,m_N)$ is the family of maps
$$G_q:T_qQ\times T_qQ\longrightarrow\mathbb R,\qquad G_q(u,v):=\langle dX_q(u),\,M\,dX_q(v)\rangle_{3N},$$
- In a local chart with coordinate vector fields $\partial_1,\dots,\partial_n$ the matrix of $dX_q$ is the Jacobian $X_q$ with entries $\partial_jX^k(q)$, and the matrix of $G_q$ is $X_q^{\mathsf T}MX_q$; this finite computation is [[lem-cm-mass-metric-positivity]]. When $X$ is the inclusion of an embedded $Q$, the differential is the inclusion $T_qQ\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^{3N}$ and the formula reads $G_q(u,v)=\langle u,Mv\rangle_{3N}$.
- The mass metric is a mathematical construction from the data $(Q,X,m_i)$: no physical law, no identification of $m_i$ with any measured inertial or gravitational mass, and no assertion that the motion of any physical system minimizes anything is made here.
- The kinetic energy $T_q$ is not a function on $Q$ but a function on tangent vectors depending on the base point; this dependence is the reason the mass metric is recorded as a family indexed by $q$.


FILE def-cm-traction-and-stress
---
id: def-cm-traction-and-stress
kind: definition
title: "Traction fields and stress matrices representing them"
status: draft
origin: pipeline
domain: mathematics
dependency_level: 0
deps: [def-euclidean-spheres-and-closed-balls, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix, def-matrix-space]
dependency_roles:
  def-euclidean-spheres-and-closed-balls: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-inner-product: mathematical-premise
  def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix: mathematical-premise
  def-matrix-space: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
sources:
  references:
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Section 4.3 (force, Cauchy's hypothesis, Remarks (b)-(e)), printed pages 105-110"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
    - title: "MIT OpenCourseWare 12.005, Applications of Continuum Mechanics, Lecture 3: tensors, stress, Cauchy tetrahedron and Cauchy's formula"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/12-005-applications-of-continuum-mechanics-to-earth-atmospheric-and-planetary-sciences-spring-2006/5005ac6aec16d031bffbd361c792ca70_lec3.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
A **traction field** is a map
read with the following convention: $t(n)$ is the force per unit area exerted **by the material on the side towards which $n$ points upon the material on the other side**, at the point of a surface whose unit normal is $n$. The dependence of $t$ on position and time is suppressed in this notation; a traction **field on a set** $\Omega\times I$ is a map $t:\Omega\times I\times S^2\to\mathbb R^3$, and all statements below apply to each fixed $(y,s)$.
A matrix $\sigma\in M_3(\mathbb R)$ ([[def-matrix-space]]) **represents** the traction field $t$ when
the product being matrix-vector multiplication ([[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]]). A matrix representing a traction field is called a **stress matrix** for it.
The **reversed normal convention** is the identity $t(-n)=-t(n)$; it is a property some traction fields have and is not part of the definition. A traction field is **normal** when $t(n)$ is a real multiple of $n$ for every $n$, and **purely tangential** when $\langle t(n),n\rangle_2=0$ for every $n$ ([[def-euclidean-inner-product]]).
- Stress positivity is not asserted: $-\lambda I_3$ with $\lambda>0$ represents a purely normal traction field with $t(n)=-\lambda n$, i.e. a compressive traction with positive scalar pressure $\lambda$; the sign convention is the one recorded, so positive $\lambda$ gives compression in the direction $-n$.
- The notation $t(n)$ suppresses the point of application. A traction field depending only on $(y,s)$ cannot distinguish two surfaces through the same point with the same normal, which is exactly the restriction Cauchy's hypothesis imposes; the failure of that hypothesis for general contact interactions is not a defect of this definition.


FILE def-cm01-configuration
---
id: def-cm01-configuration
kind: definition
title: "Configuration domain of $N$ labeled particles: open admissible sets, regular holonomic constraints and the mass operator"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 6
deps: [post-cm01-worldlines, def-cm01-units, def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric, thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold, def-regular-and-critical-points-and-values, def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart]
dependency_roles:
  post-cm01-worldlines: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric: mathematical-premise
  thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold: mathematical-premise
  def-regular-and-critical-points-and-values: mathematical-premise
  def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "Definition of the configuration stage of the framework: for $N$ labeled particles of the model, the admissible position tuples in one inertial frame as an open set or as the regular zero set of holonomic constraint functions, the collision exclusion of a singular force model, the degree-of-freedom count, and the mass operator assembled from the fixed masses. The item defines vocabulary only; it adopts no force law, no equation of motion and no constitutive assumption, and it asserts no measured value."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "G. J. Sussman and J. Wisdom, Structure and Interpretation of Classical Mechanics, second edition (MIT Press, 2014/2015), Section 1.1 'Configuration Spaces', extracted chapter pages 1-2: the configuration of a system of point particles, the configuration space, its dimension as the number of degrees of freedom, and the worked example that two particles at fixed distance have a five-dimensional configuration space"
      url: "https://mitp-content-server.mit.edu/books/content/sectbyfn/books_pres_0/9579/sicm_edition_2.zip"
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.1, printed pages 10-13 (PDF pages 16-19): constraints and generalized coordinates, and Section 3.1, printed pages 46-48: configuration space of a mechanical system"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "J. M. Lee, Introduction to Smooth Manifolds (2003 first edition), Level Sets, printed pages 104-105: the regular level set theorem (used here through its established statement)"
      url: "https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/palestine/lee-smooth-manifolds.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
Let $N\ge1$ particle labels with fixed masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$ be given as
in [[post-cm01-worldlines]], and fix one inertial frame of the model with
spatial coordinates $x_{i}\in\mathbb R^{3}$ for each label $i$. Identify the
space of all position tuples with $\mathbb R^{3N}=(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$ and
write its points as $x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})$; each component $x_{i}$ has
dimension $\ell$ ([[def-cm01-units]]).
**(a) Unconstrained branch.** The **unconstrained configuration domain** is an
open set $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$, chosen as part of the model. A tuple
$x\in Q$ is an **admissible configuration**: an assignment of one position to
each label at one time. A **singular-force exclusion** is the removal of the
**collision set**
$$\Delta:=\Bigl\{x\in\mathbb R^{3N}:\ \text{there are }i\ne j\text{ with }x_{i}=x_{j}\Bigr\}$$
from the domain, which is done when the model's force law is not defined at
coincident positions: $\Delta$ is closed (it is the union of the finitely many
closed sets $\{x_{i}=x_{j}\}$, each the zero set of the continuous map
$x\mapsto x_{i}-x_{j}$), so $Q:=\mathbb R^{3N}\setminus\Delta$ is open and is a
legitimate configuration domain. Whether the exclusion is part of the model is
a modelling decision recorded with the force law; the framework itself only
requires $Q$ to be open and stated.
**(b) Holonomic constraint branch.** Let $O\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ be an open
**ambient set**, let $k\in\mathbb N$ with $1\le k\le3N-1$, and let
1. **Nonempty level set:** $G^{-1}(0)\ne\varnothing$;
2. **Rank condition:** the differential $DG(x):\mathbb R^{3N}\to\mathbb R^{k}$
   is surjective, equivalently has rank $k$, at every $x\in G^{-1}(0)$.
Then $0$ is a regular value of $G$ in the sense of
[[def-regular-and-critical-points-and-values]], and the **constrained
configuration space** is $Q:=G^{-1}(0)$. By
[[thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold]] — applied to the smooth
map $G$ from the open subset $O$ of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ to $\mathbb R^{k}$, whose
regular value $0$ has nonempty fibre — $Q$ is an embedded submanifold of
$\mathbb R^{3N}$ of dimension $3N-k$ ([[def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart]]).
The **degrees of freedom** of this branch of the model is the number
$$\dim Q=3N-k.$$ In the sense of [[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]], $Q$ together with the inclusion $X:Q\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^{3N}$ is then a particle configuration, and the mass metric of that definition is available with the masses $m_{i}$; the configuration is constrained to $Q$ for all times in this branch.
**(c) Time-dependent holonomic constraints.** If the constraint map depends on
time,
with $G$ smooth and $D_{x}G(t_{0},x)$ of rank $k$ at every $x$ of the nonempty
set $G(t_{0},\cdot)^{-1}(0)$, then for each fixed $t_{0}\in\mathbb R$ the
**instantaneous configuration space** $Q_{t_{0}}:=G(t_{0},\cdot)^{-1}(0)$ is an
embedded submanifold of dimension $3N-k$ by the same theorem applied to
$G(t_{0},\cdot)$. The family $\{Q_{t_{0}}\}$ varies with $t_{0}$; a curve
$t\mapsto q(t)$ with $G(t,q(t))=0$ is not thereby a curve in one fixed
manifold, and the kinematic treatment of such constraints (velocities, the
reaction forces that maintain them and the distinction between rheonomic and
scleronomic constraints) belongs to the framework's constraint development and
is not part of this definition.
**(d) Mass operator.** In the ambient coordinates define the **mass operator**
$\mathsf M:\mathbb R^{3N}\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ by block multiplication,
its matrix in the standard basis is block diagonal with blocks $m_{i}I_{3}$,
the **mass matrix** of [[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]].
Its dimension is $\mu$: it multiplies positions of dimension $\ell$ by masses
of dimension $\mu$. On the constrained branch the mass metric restricts the
Euclidean inner product to $Q$ as in that item.
**(e) Velocity constraints are a different object.** A **velocity-map
constraint** of the model is a pair $(A,b)$ with
$A:\mathbb R\times O\to\mathbb R^{k'}$ a smooth map of rows and
$b:\mathbb R\times O\to\mathbb R^{k'}$, imposing $A(t,x)\,\dot x+b(t,x)=0$ on
velocities. Such a constraint is called **nonholonomic** when its admissible
velocities are not the tangent spaces of position-constraint submanifolds. The
rank of $A(t,x)$ is a count of independent instantaneous velocity restrictions
and is not a dimension of a configuration manifold; no relation between that
rank and the number $\dim Q$ of clause (b) is asserted by this definition, and
no accessibility dimension is defined here. The framework's treatment of
nonholonomic models, and the distinction between their equations and those of
positional constraints, is developed separately.
- **Choice of coordinates.** The definition uses the redundant particle
  positions $x\in\mathbb R^{3N}$ as coordinates, not a set of generalized
  coordinates of $Q$; describing a constrained configuration by $3N-k$
  coordinates requires charts of the embedded submanifold, and the
  transformation between such charts is part of the differential geometry of
  $Q$. The distinction between the redundant description and a chart is not
  erased by the count $\dim Q$.

FILE def-cm02-newtonian-flow
---
id: def-cm02-newtonian-flow
kind: definition
title: "The associated first-order system, motions, maximal motions and the Newtonian flow of a force model"
status: draft
origin: session

$Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ open ([[def-cm01-configuration]] clause (a)),

hypotheses of clause (iii) of that postulate.
$$D:=I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N},$$
$$\mathsf M^{-1}:\mathbb R^{3N}\to\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad \mathsf M^{-1}(u_{1},\dots,u_{N}):=\Bigl(\frac{u_{1}}{m_{1}},\dots,\frac{u_{N}}{m_{N}}\Bigr),$$
the inverse of the mass operator $\mathsf M$ of clause (d) of
[[def-cm01-configuration]].
$$\mathcal F:D\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad \mathcal F(t,x,v):=\Bigl(v,\ \mathsf M^{-1}F(t,x,v)\Bigr),$$
$$\Phi:\Omega\longrightarrow Q\times\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad \Omega:=\bigl\{(t,t_{0},z_{0}):t_{0}\in I_{0},\ z_{0}\in Q\times\mathbb R^{3N},\ t\in J_{\max}(t_{0},z_{0})\bigr\},$$

Fix a model of the force branch [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: $N\ge1$
particle labels with fixed masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$
([[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)), a time window $I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb
R$, an open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ with its collision
set removed when the model's force law is singular there
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), and a force map
$$F:I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}\longrightarrow(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$$
with the standing continuity and state-local-Lipschitz hypotheses of clause
(iii) of that postulate. Fix one inertial frame of the model with adapted
coordinates $(t,x)$ ([[post-cm01-frames]]), and let $J\subseteq I_{0}$ be an
interval with nonempty interior ([[def-interval]]). All sums below range over
$i=1,\dots,N$ ([[lem-finite-sum-laws]]), and $u\times v$, $\langle u,v\rangle$
and $\lVert u\rVert_{2}$ are the cross product, the Euclidean inner product and
the Euclidean norm on $\mathbb R^{3}$ ([[def-cross-product-in-r3]],
[[def-euclidean-inner-product]]).

FILE def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy
Adopt the force model and one inertial frame of
[[def-cm03-centre-of-mass-and-momenta]], with configuration domain $Q$, time
window $I_{0}$ and force map $F$. Dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]]
and gradients are Euclidean ([[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]); points of
$Q$ are written $x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})$ with $x_{i}\in\mathbb R^{3}$.
**(d) Closed and exact force fields.** A **time- and velocity-independent
force field** is an external single-label contribution of the special form
$F_{i}^{\mathrm{ext}}(t,x,v)=\varphi_{i}(x)$, read as the map
$\varphi=(\varphi_{1},\dots,\varphi_{N}):Q\to\mathbb R^{3N}$. Such a field is
**closed** when $\partial_{k}\varphi_{j}=\partial_{j}\varphi_{k}$ at every
point of $Q$ (all partial derivatives continuous), and **exact with potential
energy $U$** when $\varphi=-\nabla U$ for a $C^{2}$ function $U:Q\to\mathbb
R$; equivalently, the field $-\varphi$ is exact in the sense of
[[def-closed-and-exact-c1-vector-fields]] with potential $U$. The following
mathematical facts are used, with their proofs at the cited items:
Consequently the existence of a global potential energy on the model's
configuration domain is a property of the field on that domain, and a model
whose potential part is a merely closed field on a non-star-shaped domain is
not thereby a global potential model. The distinction is sharp: the
companion examples page carries a model on an annular domain whose force
field is smooth and closed, has nonzero circulation around a closed loop, and
therefore admits no global potential and no global conserved energy of the
form $K+U$, while item 2 provides a local potential and a locally conserved
energy on every star-shaped subpart of that domain.
**(e) Local potentials.** Let $V\subseteq Q$ be open and star-shaped with
respect to one of its points ([[def-star-shaped-open-subset-of-rn]]) and let
$\varphi$ be closed on $V$. Then item 2 of (d) provides a $C^{2}$ potential
$U_{V}:V\to\mathbb R$ with $\varphi|_{V}=-\nabla U_{V}$: a **local potential**
of the field on $V$. On the restricted configuration domain $V$ the model
with force $-\nabla U_{V}$ is a global potential model, so the mechanical
energy $K+U_{V}$ is available along curve families in $V$; this is how a
potential is recovered from a merely closed field on a sufficiently small
star-shaped piece of the domain, with no claim of a global representative.
**(f) Equipotential level sets.** Let $U:Q\to\mathbb R$ be $C^{2}$, let
$c\in\mathbb R$ and suppose $U^{-1}(c)\neq\varnothing$ and $\nabla
U(x)\neq0$ for every $x\in U^{-1}(c)$; equivalently $c$ is a regular value of
$U$ and every point of the level set is a regular point
([[def-regular-and-critical-points-and-values]]). Then $U^{-1}(c)$ is an
embedded submanifold of $Q$ of dimension $3N-1$
([[thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold]],
[[def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart]]). No such conclusion is made at a
level where $\nabla U$ vanishes somewhere on the level set, where the level
set may have singular points.

The item fixes vocabulary, domains, regularity and dimensions for the conservative and dissipative models of this page; it adopts no force law of nature, asserts no existence, uniqueness, conservation or stability statement, and records no measured value."

---
**(a) Data of a one-dimensional model.** A **one-dimensional model** is
declared by the following data: a fixed positive **mass** $m>0$, of dimension
$\mu$ read in kilograms ([[def-cm01-units]], [[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause
(v)); a **time window** $I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb R$, a nondegenerate open
interval read in seconds ([[def-interval]]); an open **configuration
domain** $Q\subseteq\mathbb R$, a nonempty open interval of the real line
read in metres; and a **force law**
$$F:I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R\longrightarrow\mathbb R,\qquad (t,x,v)\longmapsto F(t,x,v),$$
of dimension $\mu\ell\tau^{-2}$, read in newtons, subject to the **standing
regularity** of the framework: $F$ is continuous on $I_{0}\times Q\times
\mathbb R$ and locally Lipschitz in the state variable $(x,v)$ locally
uniformly in time ([[def-locally-lipschitz-in-the-state-variable]]). The
framework's three-dimensional force branch is
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]; the class declared here is a **restricted
model class** adopted at the same primitive level, in which the physical
position of a single label along one coordinate is the whole state. A model
whose force law fails the standing regularity is not a one-dimensional model
of this class. When a statement differentiates the force law or the potential,
the additional smoothness it needs is stated in that statement.
- **Why a separate restricted class.** The three-dimensional branch
  [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] is formulated for $N$ labels in
  $\mathbb R^{3N}$; the models of this page are declared in one scalar
  coordinate, as the framework's one-dimensional class, because every claim
  below is a statement about that scalar equation. Reading a model of this
  class as the straight-line motion of a three-dimensional label, or as the
  effective reduction of a constrained system (for example the angle
  coordinate of the pendulum), is a modelling identification that is stated
  and used only where a consumer item declares it, together with the extra
  assumptions it needs; it is not a premise of this definition.

FILE def-cm04-phase-plane-and-energy-level-sets
- **The phase plane is two-dimensional, and the model is not the pendulum.**
  The coordinates $(x,v)$ are the position and velocity of the declared
  scalar model; a model with an angle coordinate would carry a quotient of
  the line by the periodicity of the physical configuration, which is not
  part of this definition. The pendulum model of the companion page declares
  its own coordinate interval.

FILE def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model
---
id: def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model
kind: definition
title: "Holonomic-constraint models of the force branch: constraint data, the constrained equation, virtual displacements and the scleronomic/rheonomic distinction"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 9
deps: [post-cm02-force-superposition, post-cm01-frames, def-cm01-configuration, def-cm01-velocity-acceleration, def-cm01-units, def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions, thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold, def-regular-and-critical-points-and-values, def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart, cor-matrix-rank-nullity, def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms, def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient, def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral, thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives, def-interval]

  post-cm01-frames: formulation-prerequisite

  def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions: mathematical-premise
  thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold: mathematical-premise
  def-regular-and-critical-points-and-values: mathematical-premise

physical_scope: "Definition of the holonomic-constraint branch of the force model: the declared regular constraint map on an ambient set, the constraint locus and its instantaneous configuration manifolds, constrained curve families and permitted velocities, the virtual displacements, the reaction field and the constrained equation of motion, the consistent initial data, and the scleronomic/rheonomic classification of the constraint locus. The item declares a model class; it adopts no ideal-reaction law, no force law beyond the force branch and no measured value, and it does not assert that any real mechanism is described by the class."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.3.1 'Holonomic Constraints', Eqs. (2.29)-(2.33), printed pages 17-19 (PDF pages 23-25): holonomic constraints f_alpha(x_A,t)=0, the time-dependent form, the multipliers and the constraint-force term lambda_alpha d f_alpha/d x_A"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"

Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Sections 2.1-2.2, chapter pages 1-3 and Fig. 2.1: generalized coordinates defining a configuration together with constraints, and the bead on a rotating ring as a system with one degree of freedom"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Sections 1.4-1.5, printed pages 19-25 (PDF pages 22-28): configurations constrained by maps f:U x I -> R^k of full spatial rank, admissible virtual displacements as the kernel of the spatial derivative at fixed time, and ideal constraint reactions as covectors annihilating them"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"

  precheck: pass
---
Adopt the force branch of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: $N\ge1$ particle
labels with fixed masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, a nondegenerate open time window
$I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb R$, an open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$
([[def-cm01-configuration]] clause (a)), and a force map
$$F:I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}\longrightarrow(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$$
with the standing continuity and state-local-Lipschitz hypotheses of clause
(iii) of that postulate. Fix one inertial frame of the model with adapted
coordinates $(t,x)$ ([[post-cm01-frames]]), identify
$x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})\in(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}=\mathbb R^{3N}$ with the position
tuple of the labels ([[def-cm01-configuration]]), and let $\mathsf M$ be the
mass operator of clause (d) of that definition, $\mathsf M(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})
=(m_{1}x_{1},\dots,m_{N}x_{N})$, a symmetric positive definite linear map with
block-diagonal matrix and blocks $m_{i}I_{3}$. All components below are read in
this frame, and dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]].
**(a) Constraint datum.** A **holonomic-constraint datum** on this data is a
triple $(O,k,G)$ with:
1. an **ambient set** $O\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, open and
   nonempty;
2. an integer $k$ with $1\le k\le 3N-1$;
3. a map $G=(G_{1},\dots,G_{k}):O\to\mathbb R^{k}$ of class $C^{2}$
   ([[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]), each component carrying a
   declared dimension $[G_{\alpha}]=\gamma_{\alpha}$ fixed by the model (the
   model may rescale a component by a nonzero constant, which changes
   $\gamma_{\alpha}$ and the component but not its zero set);
4. **nonempty admissible locus:** the **constraint locus**
   $$\Sigma:=\bigl\{(t,x)\in O:\ G(t,x)=0\bigr\}$$
   is nonempty and contained in $I_{0}\times Q$: every constrained
   configuration is an admissible configuration of the force-branch model and
   every constrained time lies in its time window;
5. **regularity:** for every $(t,x)\in\Sigma$ the Jacobian $D_{x}G(t,x)$
   ([[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]) is surjective, equivalently has rank
   $k$.
For $t\in\mathbb R$ write $\Sigma_{t}:=\{x\in\mathbb R^{3N}:(t,x)\in\Sigma\}$
for the **instantaneous constraint locus**. If $\Sigma_{t}\neq\varnothing$,
then $x\mapsto G(t,x)$ has the regular value $0$ with nonempty fibre
$\Sigma_{t}$ in the sense of [[def-regular-and-critical-points-and-values]]
(on the open set $\{x:(t,x)\in O\}$), so by
[[thm-a-regular-level-set-is-an-embedded-submanifold]] the set $\Sigma_{t}$ is
an embedded submanifold of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ of dimension $3N-k$
([[def-embedded-submanifold-and-slice-chart]]); when $k=3N-1$ the
instantaneous locus is locally a curve, and the number
$$\dim\Sigma_{t}=3N-k$$
is the **instantaneous degrees of freedom** of the constrained branch. The
set $V(t,x):=\ker D_{x}G(t,x)\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ has dimension $3N-k$ by
the rank-nullity theorem for matrices ([[cor-matrix-rank-nullity]]) and is called the space of **virtual displacements** of the datum
at $(t,x)$; its elements are the first-order differences of constrained
configurations at fixed time, in the sense of the mathematical definition
[[def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions]]. A vector
$v\in\mathbb R^{3N}$ is a **permitted velocity** at $(t,x)\in O$ when
$$G_{t}(t,x)+D_{x}G(t,x)\,v=0 .$$
**(b) Constrained curve families.** Let $J\subseteq I_{0}$ be an interval with
nonempty interior ([[def-interval]]). A **constrained curve family** of the
datum is a map $x:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ of class $C^{2}$ with
$(t,x(t))\in O$ and $G(t,x(t))=0$ for every $t\in J$; writing
$x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})$ with coordinate curves $x_{i}:J\to\mathbb R^{3}$ and
$w_{i}:=x_{i}'$, the componentwise derivatives of
[[def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral]] ([[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]];
$w_{i}$ is the coordinate velocity of label $i$), the chain rule
([[thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives]]) applied to $t\mapsto G(t,x(t))$
gives
$$G_{t}\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)+D_{x}G\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)\,w(t)=0\qquad(t\in J),$$
so the velocity of a $C^{1}$ constrained curve family is permitted at every
time. A tuple $(t_{0},x_{0},v_{0})\in O\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is **consistent
data** of the datum when $G(t_{0},x_{0})=0$ and
$G_{t}(t_{0},x_{0})+D_{x}G(t_{0},x_{0})v_{0}=0$; these are the initial data at
which constrained motions are posed below.
**(c) Reaction field and the constrained equation.** A **reaction field along
a curve family** $x:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ is a continuous map
$R:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$, of force dimension $\mu\ell\tau^{-2}$ in each
component. The **equation of the constrained datum along $x$** is
$$\mathsf M\,x''(t)=F\bigl(t,x(t),w(t)\bigr)+R(t)\qquad(t\in J),$$
componentwise $m_{i}x_{i}''(t)=F_{i}(t,x(t),w(t))+R_{i}(t)$. A
**holonomic-constraint model** consists of the force-branch data, a
holonomic-constraint datum $(O,k,G)$, and the declaration that the position
tuple of the labels satisfies $G(t,x(t))=0$ for all times while a reaction
field acts: a **constrained motion** of the model (with reaction $R$) is a
constrained curve family $x:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ together with a reaction field
$R$ satisfying the displayed equation. The force map $F$ is the model's
declared interaction force of the force branch, and $R$ is the additional
force contribution exerted by the mechanism that realizes the constraint; the
total force along a constrained motion is the pointwise sum $F+R$ in the sense
of clause (v) of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], read along the curve. The
reaction is **not** part of the force map: the constrained branch is the branch
excluded by clause (i) of that postulate, and the equation above is the
adopted equation of the constrained model, with $R$ fixed by an additional
assumption. As declared so far the model leaves $R$ free; the assumption that
selects it is the ideal-reaction law of the postulate of this page. The
reaction field of a constrained motion is not determined by the curve alone
until that law is imposed.
**(d) Scleronomic and rheonomic constraints.** The datum is **scleronomic**
when the constraint locus is time-independent, in the precise sense that there
is a set $N\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ with
$$\Sigma=\bigl\{(t,x)\in O:\ x\in N\bigr\};$$
equivalently, membership in the locus is a condition on $x$ alone within $O$:
if $(t,x)\in\Sigma$ and $(t',x)\in O$, then $(t',x)\in\Sigma$ (the two
formulations are exchanged by passing between $N$ and the projection of
$\Sigma$ to $\mathbb R^{3N}$). For the standard product ambient set
$O=I_{0}\times Q$ the condition reads $\Sigma=I_{0}\times(N\cap Q)$. The datum
is **rheonomic** otherwise.
The identity is a consequence of time-independence and **not** a criterion for
it. The presentation $G(t,x):=(x_{1}-t)^{2}$ on
$O=\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ has the moving zero set $\{x_{1}=t\}$ while
$G_{t}(t,x)=-2(x_{1}-t)$ vanishes at every point of that set; this presentation
violates the rank hypothesis, but the identity can also hold for a
rank-regular presentation: on
$O=\bigl((0,1)\cup(2,3)\bigr)\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ the $C^{2}$ map equal to
$x_{1}$ on $(0,1)\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ and to $1$ on
$(2,3)\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ has the rank-$1$ zero set
$\Sigma=(0,1)\times\{x\in\mathbb R^{3N}:x_{1}=0\}$ with $G_{t}=0$ on $\Sigma$,
yet $\Sigma$ is not of the form $\{(t,x)\in O:x\in N\}$ for any $N$: the point
$(1/2,0)\in\Sigma$ would force $0\in N$, and then $(5/2,0)\in O$ would lie in
$\Sigma$. Hence the classification is a property of the locus, and the
identity is used only in the direction proved above.
**(e) Status.** This item defines a model class: it fixes the constraint
datum, the constrained curves, the virtual displacements, the reaction field
and the equation of the constrained branch. It adopts no ideal-reaction law
(that is the content of the ideal-reaction postulate of this page), no particular force
law, and no measured value. Whether a concrete apparatus — a frictionless
joint, a smooth track, a taut rod or string, a no-slip contact — is described
by a holonomic-constraint model is an application-level judgement recorded with
the apparatus, together with the regime in which the rigidity, frictionlessness
or contact assumptions are adequate; the framework itself attaches no empirical
claim to the class.
- **Presentation of the constraint.** The components $G_{\alpha}$ are
  redundant descriptions of the locus and are fixed only up to multiplication
  by an invertible matrix-valued function; the dimension count $3N-k$ and the
  virtual displacements at the locus are unchanged under such a
  re-presentation, whereas the multiplier of a later motion is tied to the
  declared components. This is why the multiplier is never a measured
  quantity in this framework; only the reaction enters the equation.

---
id: def-cm06-nonholonomic-model
kind: definition
title: "Velocity-constraint (nonholonomic) models of the force branch: constant-rank constraint laws, the d'Alembert reaction and holonomic equivalence"
status: draft
origin: session

dependency_level: 9
deps: [post-cm02-force-superposition, def-cm01-configuration, def-cm01-velocity-acceleration, def-cm01-units, def-cm-nonholonomic-velocity-constraint-law, cex-cm-dalembert-and-vakonomic-evolutions-differ, prop-cm-nonholonomic-distribution-is-not-tangent-to-level-sets, lem-linear-functional-annihilating-kernel-of-a-surjection-is-a-transpose-multiple, cor-matrix-rank-nullity, def-linear-map, def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms, def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient, def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral, thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-invertible-matrix-and-general-linear-group, def-interval]

physical_scope: "Definition of the velocity-constraint branch of the force model: a declared constant-rank affine velocity law, its admissible velocities and Chetaev virtual displacements at frozen time and configuration, kinematically admissible curve families, the adopted d'Alembert ideal-reaction law and its multiplier representation, the equation of the branch, the classification of holonomic equivalence and nonholonomic models, and the exclusion of rank changes, contact changes, impacts, friction and driving. The item defines a model class; it asserts no measured value and does not claim that any particular mechanism is described by it."

Adopt the force branch of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: $N\ge1$ labels
with masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, a nondegenerate open time window
$I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb R$, an open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$
and a force map $F$ with the standing regularity
([[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]]). Fix one inertial frame with
adapted coordinates $(t,x)$ and write $x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})\in\mathbb R^{3N}$
with coordinate velocity $w=x'$ ([[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]],
[[def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral]]).
1. an **ambient set** $O_{v}\subseteq I_{0}\times Q$, open and nonempty;
2. an integer $k'$ with $1\le k'\le 3N-1$;
3. maps $A:O_{v}\to M_{k'\times 3N}(\mathbb R)$ and
   $b:O_{v}\to\mathbb R^{k'}$ of class $C^{2}$
   ([[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]), of declared dimensions
   chosen so that every row satisfies $[A_{\alpha j}w_{j}]=[b_{\alpha}]$ —
   the output of each row of the law below is a single declared quantity;
4. the **constant-rank hypothesis**: $A(t,x)$ has rank $k'$ for every
   $(t,x)\in O_{v}$, equivalently the linear map
   $A(t,x):\mathbb R^{3N}\to\mathbb R^{k'}$ is surjective
   ([[def-linear-map]], [[cor-matrix-rank-nullity]]).
**(b) Admissible velocities, virtual displacements, admissible curves.** For
$(t,x)\in O_{v}$ the **admissible velocities** are the vectors
$v\in\mathbb R^{3N}$ with $A(t,x)v+b(t,x)=0$. They form the nonempty affine
subspace $v_{0}+\ker A(t,x)$ (nonempty because $A(t,x)$ is surjective), with
direction the linear space
$$V(t,x):=\ker A(t,x)\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad \dim V(t,x)=3N-k'$$
by rank-nullity ([[cor-matrix-rank-nullity]]). The elements of $V(t,x)$ are
the **virtual displacements** of the datum at $(t,x)$: the admissible
velocity differences at frozen time and configuration, in the Chetaev
prescription of [[def-cm-nonholonomic-velocity-constraint-law]]. A
**kinematically admissible curve family** is a map $x:J\to Q$ of class $C^{2}$
on an interval $J\subseteq I_{0}$ with nonempty interior ([[def-interval]])
such that $(t,x(t))\in O_{v}$ and
$A(t,x(t))x'(t)+b(t,x(t))=0$ for every $t\in J$; its velocity at each time is
admissible by definition.
**(c) d'Alembert velocity-constraint model.** A **velocity-constraint model**
consists of the force-branch data, a velocity-constraint datum $(O_{v},k',A,b)$
and the declaration that the labels' motion satisfies the constraint law
while a reaction field acts: a **motion** of the model is a kinematically
admissible curve family $x:J\to Q$ together with a continuous reaction field
$R:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ of force dimension satisfying
$$\mathsf M\,x''(t)=F\bigl(t,x(t),x'(t)\bigr)+R(t)\qquad(t\in J),$$
with $\mathsf M$ the mass operator of [[def-cm01-configuration]] clause (d).
The model is a **d'Alembert model** when, in addition, the reaction is
**ideal**: along every motion,
$$R(t)\cdot\delta=0\qquad\text{for every }\delta\in V\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)=\ker A\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr),$$
the pairing being the Euclidean inner product ([[def-euclidean-inner-product]]).
This is the **adopted idealization** of the mechanism that realizes the
velocity constraint — the analogue for velocity constraints of the ideal
reaction law of the holonomic branch — and it is not a consequence of the
constraint being imposed. By the multiplier lemma
([[lem-linear-functional-annihilating-kernel-of-a-surjection-is-a-transpose-multiple]]),
applied at each time to the surjective $A(t,x(t))$, the ideal condition holds
at time $t$ if and only if there is a unique $\mu(t)\in\mathbb R^{k'}$ with
$$R(t)=A\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)^{\mathsf T}\mu(t);$$
the representation is a mathematical consequence of the condition and the
rank hypothesis, while the ideal condition itself is the adopted part. The
equation of the d'Alembert model in multiplier form is therefore
$\mathsf Mx''=F+A^{\mathsf T}\mu$ along an ideal motion.
**(d) Holonomic equivalence and nonholonomic models.** The datum is
**holonomic-equivalent** on $O_{v}$ when there exist a $C^{2}$ map
$\Phi:O_{v}\to\mathbb R^{k'}$ with $\Phi_{x}$ of rank $k'$ on $O_{v}$ and a
$C^{2}$ map $\varrho:O_{v}\to GL(k',\mathbb R)$
([[def-invertible-matrix-and-general-linear-group]],
[[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]) with
$$A(t,x)=\varrho(t,x)\,\Phi_{x}(t,x),\qquad b(t,x)=\varrho(t,x)\,\Phi_{t}(t,x)\qquad\text{on }O_{v};$$
the datum is **nonholonomic** when no such pair $(\Phi,\varrho)$ exists. In
the holonomic-equivalent case the law is the differentiated family of
position constraints $\Phi(t,x)=\text{constant}$: for a $C^{1}$ curve with
$(t,x(t))\in O_{v}$, the chain rule
([[thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives]]) gives
$$\frac{d}{dt}\Phi\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)=\Phi_{t}+\Phi_{x}x'=\varrho^{-1}\bigl(b+Ax'\bigr),$$
so the constraint law holds exactly when $\Phi(t,x(t))$ is constant; a
holonomic-equivalent model is thus one whose constraint is equivalent to a
family of $k'$ independent position constraints with regular differentials,
presented through the nowhere-vanishing factor $\varrho$. For $k'=1$ the
existence of $(\Phi,\varrho)$ is the existence of an integrating factor for
the one-form $b\,dt+A\cdot dx$; the class of nonholonomic models is exactly
the class of constant-rank velocity laws for which no such integrating factor
exists. The planar no-slip law of a disk rolling in a straight line is
holonomic-equivalent with $A=(1,-a)$, $b=0$, $\Phi(x,\theta)=x-a\theta$ and
$\varrho=1$; the velocity constraint $z'=xy'$ of
[[def-cm-nonholonomic-velocity-constraint-law]] is not: an integrating factor
would make the distribution $\ker A$ tangent to the level sets of $\Phi$, and
[[prop-cm-nonholonomic-distribution-is-not-tangent-to-level-sets]] proves
that this distribution is not tangent to the level sets of any $C^{2}$
function with nonvanishing differential. The sources report the general
rolling-coin constraints of Tong Eq. (2.41) as a further example of the
nonholonomic class without a local proof being given here.
**(e) Status and scope.** The d'Alembert law of clause (c) is a prescription
for the reaction at frozen time and configuration, adopted as the physical
idealization of the mechanism; it is **not** identified with the vakonomic
(stationary augmented action) prescription of
[[def-cm-nonholonomic-velocity-constraint-law]], and for the model $z'=xy'$
the two prescriptions produce different motions from the same initial data
([[cex-cm-dalembert-and-vakonomic-evolutions-differ]]); the closing remark of
this page records the distinction and its scope. The framework excludes from
this branch: points where the rank of $A$ drops (the multiplier system
degenerates there, and no continuation is asserted); unilateral constraints
and contact changes (a skate leaving the ground, a wheel beginning to slip),
which require their own mode-change models; impacts; dry friction along
admissible directions, which must be declared as a separate force
contribution; and active drives, whose driving is an additional force of the
model. Whether a concrete mechanism is described by this class, and whether
its reaction can supply the computed multiplier, are application-level
statements recorded with the apparatus; the definition asserts no measured
value.
- **Position versus velocity constraints.** A holonomic model constrains
  configurations and its constraint locus is a submanifold of position space;
  a velocity-constraint model constrains velocities, its configuration
  remains in the open set $Q$, and its virtual displacements are the frozen
  kernel of $A$. A holonomic model can be written as a velocity-constraint
  model by differentiating its constraint, but not conversely; the
  classification of clause (d) is the precise sense of the converse
  obstruction.
- **The multiplier is not measured.** As in the holonomic branch, only the
  reaction $R=A^{\mathsf T}\mu$ enters the equation, and $\mu$ is tied to the
  declared rows of the law: replacing $(A,b)$ by $(\varrho A,\varrho b)$ with
  an invertible $\varrho$ leaves the admissible velocities and the reaction
  unchanged and changes $\mu$ to $\varrho^{-\mathsf T}\mu$.
- **Rolling is several models.** Planar straight rolling, no-slip rolling
  whose contact can supply the required friction, slip with kinetic friction,
  and prescribed motion of the contact are different models with different
  predictions; the planar ideal no-slip case is holonomic-equivalent, general
  three-dimensional rolling is reported nonholonomic, and rank or contact
  changes are outside this definition. The companion page compares the
  planar ideal no-slip and frictionless sliding models explicitly.

## Definition
Adopt one force model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: $N\ge1$ particle labels
with fixed masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$ (clause (i)), an open nondegenerate time
window $I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb R$, an open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$
and a force map $F$ with the standing continuity and state-local-Lipschitz
hypotheses of clause (iii) of that postulate. Fix one inertial frame with adapted
coordinates $(t,x)$ and identify position tuples with points of
$\mathbb R^{3N}=(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]]); dimensions are
read by [[def-cm01-units]], inner products and norms are Euclidean
([[def-euclidean-inner-product]]), and differentiability classes are those of
[[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]. Let $\mathsf M$ be the mass
operator of clause (d) of [[def-cm01-configuration]],
$\mathsf M(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})=(m_{1}x_{1},\dots,m_{N}x_{N})$, a symmetric
positive definite linear map of $\mathbb R^{3N}$.
**(a) Kinetic energy.** The **kinetic energy** of the labels at coordinate
velocity $v\in(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}=\mathbb R^{3N}$ is
$$L:I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\longrightarrow\mathbb R,\qquad L(t,x,v):=K(v)-U(t,x).$$
**(c) Action, variations, stationarity.** Let $a<b$ be reals with
$[a,b]\subseteq I_{0}$ and put $O:=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$. The set
$O$ is open and contains $[a,b]\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, so with $n:=3N$
the pair $(O,L)$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over $[a,b]\times Q$ in
the sense of [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]]. **Admissible
curves** (the $C^{2}$ curves $x:[a,b]\to Q$ with the extension convention of
that definition), **variations** $\eta$ with $\eta(a)=\eta(b)=0$, the
**action**
the **first variation** $\delta S(x)[\eta]$ and **stationarity** of $x$ are the
notions of [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]] instantiated at this
chart; the derivative $x'$ is the coordinate velocity ([[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]]).
The action has dimension $\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-1}$ (energy multiplied by time).
Write $E_{L}(x):[a,b]\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ for the Euler-Lagrange expression of
the instantiated chart.
- **Velocity and configuration slots.** The three arguments of $L$ are
  $(t,x,v)$ with $x\in Q$ the configuration and $v$ the coordinate velocity in
  the fixed inertial frame; the kinetic energy uses the fixed masses and the
  coordinate velocity, not any generalized velocity. Generalized coordinates
  enter only through the substitution construction of this page.

## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]).
Dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]]; gradients and Jacobians are those of
[[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]], transposes those of
[[def-transpose-of-a-matrix]], and $C^{r}$ classes those of
[[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]].
**(a) Generalized-coordinate map.** Let $r\ge1$ and let
$\Omega\subseteq\mathbb R^{r}$ be open and nonempty. A **generalized-coordinate
map of the model** is a map $X:\Omega\to Q$ of class $C^{3}$ whose derivative
$DX(q)$ is injective for every $q\in\Omega$
([[def-euclidean-submersions-and-immersions]]: an immersion); the coordinates
$q=(q_{1},\dots,q_{r})$ each carry a declared dimension $\gamma_{i}$ and unit
fixed as part of the model declaration. The two intended cases are:
$r=3N$ with $DX(q)$ invertible for every $q$ — a **chart** of the configuration
domain — and $r=3N-k$ for an integer $k$ with $1\le k\le3N-1$ with $X$ a
**parametrization** of a regular holonomic-constraint locus as in clause (d).
The **admissible coordinate curves** are the $C^{2}$ maps $q:J\to\Omega$ on an
interval $J\subseteq I_{0}$ with nonempty interior ([[def-interval]]); each such
curve induces the configuration curve $x:=X\circ q:J\to Q$.
**(c) Generalized forces.** Let $f:I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\to\mathbb R^{3N}$
be a force field of the model's standing regularity
([[post-cm02-force-superposition]] clause (iii)) — for instance the residual
force $d$ of the presentation, or the model's full force map $F$. Its
**generalized force** in the coordinates $X$ is
**(d) Constraint parametrizations.** Let a regular holonomic-constraint datum
$(O,k,G)$ of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]] be given, with
time-independent locus $N\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$, i.e.
$\Sigma=\{(t,x)\in O:x\in N\}$ (the scleronomic case). A
**parametrization of the constrained configurations** is a generalized-coordinate
map $X:\Omega\to Q$ with $r=3N-k$, $X(\Omega)=N\cap Q$, and
$(t,X(q))\in O$ for all $(t,q)\in I_{0}\times\Omega$; such a map presents every
constrained configuration exactly once and every admissible coordinate curve
induces a constrained curve family with permitted velocity (proved by the
reduction theorem of this page, which also identifies the virtual
displacements with the image of $DX$).

Adopt one force model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: $N\ge1$ particle
labels with fixed masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$ (clause (i)), an open
nondegenerate time window $I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb R$, an open configuration
domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ and a force map $F$ with the standing
continuity and state-local-Lipschitz hypotheses of clause (iii) of that
postulate, and fix one inertial frame with adapted coordinates $(t,x)$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]). Adopt in addition a potential presentation
$(U,d)$ of [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]: a function
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ of class $C^{2}$ of dimension
$\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-2}$ with $F_{i}(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U(t,x)+d_{i}(t,x,v)$,
with residual forces $d=(d_{1},\dots,d_{N})$ of the standing regularity. Let
$L=K-U$, $O:=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ and $\mathsf M$ be the
mechanical Lagrangian, the chart domain and the mass operator of
[[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], so that $L$ is of class $C^{2}$
on the open set $O$ and $(O,L)$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over
every $[a,b]\times Q$ with $[a,b]\subseteq I_{0}$
([[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]]). Dimensions are read by
[[def-cm01-units]], inner products and norms are Euclidean
([[def-euclidean-inner-product]]), and differentiability classes are those of
[[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]. For a $C^{1}$ function
$\varphi$ on an open subset of $\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ and
$(t,x,v)\in O$ write
$$\tau:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R,\qquad \xi:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad B:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R,$$
**(b) Strict data and point symmetries.** A symmetry datum is **strict**
when $\tau\equiv0$; its identity reads
$L_{x}\cdot\xi+L_{v}\cdot D_{t}\xi=D_{t}B$. If a strict datum has $\xi$
independent of $t$, then the pair $(\xi,B)$ is an **infinitesimal point
symmetry of the chart $(O,L)$** in the sense of
[[def-cm-infinitesimal-point-symmetry]]: that definition's identity,
instantiated with $n=3N$ in the configuration slot, is verbatim
$L_{x}(t,x,v)\cdot\xi(x)+L_{v}(t,x,v)\cdot D\xi(x)v=B_{t}(t,x)+B_{x}(t,x)\cdot v$.
Conversely every infinitesimal point symmetry of the chart $(O,L)$ whose
boundary function is defined on $I_{0}\times Q$ is a strict datum with $\xi$
independent of $t$. A general datum is a **point symmetry with time
reparametrisation**: the family

FILE def-cm09-coupled-quadratic-model

id: def-cm09-coupled-quadratic-model
kind: definition
title: "The coupled quadratic oscillator model: mass metric, stiffness, generalized force, modal vocabulary and the small-oscillation model at a mechanical equilibrium"

physical_scope: "The restricted model class used on this page: a constant mass metric matrix M (symmetric positive definite) and a constant symmetric stiffness matrix K in a chart of n coordinate directions, with an optional prescribed generalized force J(t), and the model equation M q'' + K q = J(t); together with the quadratic kinetic and potential energies, the phase system, the modal vocabulary of the decoupling transform, and the small-oscillation model assigned to a mechanical equilibrium q_0 of a chart mechanical system, whose mass metric is M(q_0) and whose stiffness is the potential Hessian D^2 U(q_0). The item declares vocabulary, domains, regularity and dimensions only; it asserts no existence, uniqueness, conservation, stability, resonance or approximation statement, adopts no force law of nature, records no measured value, and states no empirical applicability. In particular the mass metric and the stiffness are model parameters of the declared chart, not measured quantities, and the quadratic model is an idealization whose accuracy near an equilibrium is quantified only by the approximation theorems of this page."

      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Chapter 3, Sections 3.1-3.2, printed pages 52-59 (PDF pages 55-62): the quadratic natural Lagrangian, the generalized symmetric eigenproblem and the decoupled normal modes"

**(a) Data of a coupled quadratic model.** A **coupled quadratic model** (a
**quadratic oscillator model**) is declared by the following data: an integer
$n\ge1$, the number of **degrees of freedom**; a **mass metric matrix**
$M\in M_{n}(\mathbb R)$, symmetric and positive definite, that is
$M=M^{\mathsf T}$ and $v^{\mathsf T}Mv>0$ for every nonzero
$v\in\mathbb R^{n}$ ([[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]],
[[def-euclidean-inner-product]]); a **stiffness matrix**
$K\in M_{n}(\mathbb R)$, symmetric, $K=K^{\mathsf T}$; and, when the model is
read as forced, an interval $I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb R$ with nonempty interior
([[def-interval]]) together with a continuous **generalized force**
$J:I_{0}\to\mathbb R^{n}$ ([[def-vector-valued-functions-limits-and-continuity]]).
The coordinates $q_{1},\dots,q_{n}$ of $\mathbb R^{n}$ are read in the chart
in which the model is declared and carry declared dimensions
$\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{n}$ and units, and time is read in seconds and mass
in kilograms ([[def-cm01-units]]). The data carry the dimensions
**(c) Quadratic energies and the mass metric.** The **kinetic energy**, the
**potential energy of the stiffness** and the **mechanical energy** of the
model are
all of energy dimension. $U_{K}$ is defined for every symmetric $K$; the name
records that $K$ is the matrix through which the model enters, and when $K$ is
indefinite $U_{K}$ is not non-negative, the model is not presented by a
potential with a local minimum at the origin, and no stability vocabulary is
attached to it by this item. The quadratic form $v\mapsto v^{\mathsf T}Mv$ is
positive definite and is called the **mass metric** of the model; the word
"metric" records only this positive-definiteness, and no distance on
configuration space is defined here.
**(d) Small-oscillation model at an equilibrium.** Let a mechanical system in
a chart be given by a $C^{2}$ field $q\mapsto G_{\mathrm{kin}}(q)$ of
symmetric positive definite matrices on an open set $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb
R^{n}$ and a $C^{2}$ potential $U:\Omega\to\mathbb R$
([[def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart]]), and let
$q_{0}\in\Omega$ be a **mechanical equilibrium**, $\nabla U(q_{0})=0$. Its
**small-oscillation model at $q_{0}$** is the coupled quadratic model with
degrees of freedom $n$, mass metric matrix $M:=G_{\mathrm{kin}}(q_{0})$,
**stiffness matrix** $K_{0}:=D^{2}U(q_{0})$ — the Hessian of the potential
([[def-hessian-and-euclidean-critical-point]], symmetric by
[[cor-hessian-is-symmetric]]) — and zero generalized force, read in the
displacement coordinates $\xi:=q-q_{0}$. When the chart arises from the
generalized-coordinate dictionary of
[[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]], the matrix
$G_{\mathrm{kin}}(q_{0})=DX(q_{0})^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q_{0})$ is the
value at $q_{0}$ of that dictionary's kinetic metric. This clause assigns
model data to the equilibrium; the identification of the assigned model with
the derivative of the mechanical field, and every statement about its motions,
are separate results and are not asserted here.
**(e) Modal vocabulary of the decoupling data.** By
[[thm-cm-quadratic-mode-decomposition]] applied to the symmetric positive
definite $M$ and the symmetric matrix $K$ there are a symmetric positive
definite square root $M^{1/2}$, its inverse $M^{-1/2}$, an orthogonal matrix
$O$ with $O^{\mathsf T}=O^{-1}$, and a diagonal matrix
$\Lambda=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n})$ with
$M^{-1/2}KM^{-1/2}=O\Lambda O^{\mathsf T}$. The **modal coordinates** of the
model are $z:=O^{\mathsf T}M^{1/2}q$, a linear bijection of $\mathbb R^{n}$,
and the **modal vectors** are the columns $u_{i}:=M^{-1/2}Oe_{i}$; the numbers
$\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{n}$ are the **modal stiffnesses**. The identities
$Ku_{i}=\lambda_{i}Mu_{i}$ and $u_{i}^{\mathsf T}Mu_{j}=\delta_{ij}$ are part
of the modal theorem and are used by name on this page. An index $i$ (or the
mode it labels) is **oscillatory** when $\lambda_{i}>0$, with **mode
frequency** $\omega_{i}:=\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}>0$ (the nonnegative square root;
dimension $\tau^{-1}$, read in radians per second under the angle convention
of [[def-cm01-units]]); it is a **zero mode** when $\lambda_{i}=0$; and it is
an **unstable (exponential) direction** when $\lambda_{i}<0$. Two distinct
indices $i\ne j$ are **degenerate** when $\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{j}$; for a real
number $\lambda$ the set $E_{\lambda}:=\{u\in\mathbb R^{n}:(K-\lambda M)u=0\}$
is the **modal eigenspace** of $\lambda$, a linear subspace whose dimension is
the number of indices with $\lambda_{i}=\lambda$. The model is **oscillatory**
when $K$ is non-negative, $q^{\mathsf T}Kq\ge0$ for all $q$; then every modal
stiffness is $\ge0$, the zero modes are the indices with $\lambda_{i}=0$, and
the eigenvalue $0$ has multiplicity $\dim\ker K$.
- **Why "mass metric" and not "mass matrix".** The matrix $M$ is the value of
the chart's kinetic metric at the equilibrium, and the quadratic form it
defines is the velocity dependence of the kinetic energy. In the
Cartesian-particle case $G_{\mathrm{kin}}=\mathsf M$ is the constant mass
operator of [[def-cm01-configuration]] and the name reduces to the familiar
one. With arbitrary coordinate dimensions the entries of $M$ are not masses
in kilograms; only the pairing $v^{\mathsf T}Mv$ and the products with
coordinate variations have the stated invariant dimensions.

The item defines model-derived objects only: it asserts no equation of motion, no conservation law, no inverse-Legendre branch and no measured value, and it does not claim that any apparatus is described by the potential branch."

## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], in the
fixed inertial frame with adapted coordinates $(t,x)$ used there: $N\ge1$
labels with masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain
$Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$, time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential $U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$
of dimension $\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-2}$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing
regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]],
the Euclidean inner product of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ is [[def-euclidean-inner-product]],
and gradients and Jacobians are those of [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]
and [[def-directional-and-partial-derivatives]].
**(a) Cartesian momentum.** For a state $(t,x,v)\in I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$
define the **momentum of the labels** by
whose label components are $p_{i}=m_{i}v_{i}$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]]
clause (d)); its dimension is $\mu\ell\tau^{-1}$ (read in
$\mathrm{kg}\,\mathrm m\,\mathrm s^{-1}$). The space $\mathbb R^{3N}$ is
identified with its dual by the Euclidean inner product, so that $p$ is a
**covector** acting on a velocity $w$ by the dot product $p\cdot w$. The pair
$(x,p)$ is the **cotangent datum** of the state, and $I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$
with coordinates $(t,x,p)$ is the **momentum phase chart** of the
presentation.
- **Why a covector.** The physical content of the definition is the choice of
  the **momentum** as the fibre derivative $\mathcal L_{v}$ rather than the
  kinematic velocity: in an inertial Cartesian chart the two differ only by
  the positive definite mass operator, $p=\mathsf Mv$, whereas in generalized
  coordinates the difference is the configuration-dependent kinetic metric
  $G(q)$, and $p_{a}$ has the dimension of action per unit of $q_{a}$. The
  covector $p$ pairs with velocities by the Euclidean product in coordinates;
  no metric on momentum space is introduced here.
