## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential $U$ and residual forces $d$ of
the standing regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]); dimensions are read by
[[def-cm01-units]] and the Euclidean pairing is
[[def-euclidean-inner-product]].
**(a) Cartesian Hamiltonian.** Assume the presentation is hyperregular on the
Cartesian chart, with inverse Legendre branch
$v:I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\to\mathbb R^{3N}$
([[def-cm12-hyperregularity]], [[def-cm-legendre-map-in-a-time-dependent-chart]]).
The **Hamiltonian of the presentation on the Cartesian chart** is the function
$$H:I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\longrightarrow\mathbb R,\qquad H(t,x,p):=p\cdot v(t,x,p)-L\bigl(t,x,v(t,x,p)\bigr),$$
where $p\cdot v(t,x,p)$ is the Euclidean pairing of the momentum covector
with the inverse-branch velocity ([[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]],
[[def-euclidean-inner-product]]). The momentum phase chart
$I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ with coordinates $(t,x,p)$ is the
**Hamiltonian phase chart** of the presentation on the Cartesian chart.

## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential $U$ and residual forces $d$ of
the standing regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]); dimensions are read by
[[def-cm01-units]] and differentiability classes are those of
[[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]].
$$\mathbb F_{t,x}:\mathbb R^{3N}\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad \mathbb F_{t,x}(v):=L_{v}(t,x,v)=\mathsf Mv,$$
is a bijection of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ onto $\mathbb R^{3N}$ and the inverse
assignment
$$v:I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad L_{v}\bigl(t,x,v(t,x,p)\bigr)=p,$$
is defined on the whole momentum phase chart $I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$
and is of class $C^{1}$. In that case $v$ is the **inverse Legendre branch**
of the presentation on the Cartesian chart. Hyperregularity of a chart is
exactly hyperregularity of its fibre-full Lagrangian in the sense of
[[def-cm-legendre-map-in-a-time-dependent-chart]], read through the
identification of the fibre derivative with the momentum of
[[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]].

## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] in the
fixed inertial frame with adapted coordinates $(t,x)$: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential $U$ and residual forces $d$ of
the standing regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Dimensions are read by
[[def-cm01-units]], and the Euclidean gradients and second partial derivatives
in the velocity slot are those of [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]] and
[[def-directional-and-partial-derivatives]].
**(a) Velocity Hessian.** Let $(O,L)$ be the fibre-full time-dependent
Lagrangian chart of clause (c) of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]
and let $\widetilde L$ be the substituted Lagrangian of a generalized-coordinate
map $X:\Omega\to Q$ ([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]). The
**velocity Hessian** of the presentation in Cartesian data is the matrix
$L_{vv}(t,x,v)\in M_{3N}(\mathbb R)$ of second partial derivatives of $L$ in
its third slot at $(t,x,v)$; in the coordinates $X$ it is the matrix
$\widetilde L_{vv}(t,q,v)$ of second partial derivatives of $\widetilde L$ in
its third slot. Invertibility of the Hessian is invertibility of a linear map
in the sense of [[def-invertible-matrix-and-general-linear-group]], read by
[[cor-square-matrix-invertible-iff-determinant-is-a-unit]] through the
determinant, and matrices act on vectors by
[[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]] and [[def-linear-map]];
transposes are those of [[def-transpose-of-a-matrix]].

## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
momentum charts, the phase domains and the two-form datum of
[[def-cm13-two-form]]: the Cartesian phase domain $W=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$
with $n=3N$, its two-form $\omega$, and for each generalized-coordinate map
$X:\Omega\to Q$ with $r$ coordinates the chart map $\Phi_{X}$ and the form
$\omega_{X}$ ([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]],
[[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]). All
differentiability classes are those of
[[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]].
**(b) The model's own charts are canonical.** The Cartesian coordinates
$(x,p)$ on $W$ are canonical coordinates: by clause 1 of
[[pthm-cm13-two-form-is-symplectic]] the two-form is
$\omega=\sum_{a}dx^{a}\wedge dp_{a}$. For a generalized-coordinate map, the
coordinates $(q,P)$ on $\Omega\times\mathbb R^{r}$ are canonical coordinates
for the pulled-back form $\omega_{X}$: by clause 2 of
[[pthm-cm13-two-form-is-symplectic]] one has
$\omega_{X}=\sum_{a}dq^{a}\wedge dP_{a}$, and when the map is a genuine chart
($r=3N$) the chart transition is a change of canonical coordinates on
overlaps.

$$X_{f}=\bigl(f_{p},\,-f_{x}\bigr)=J\nabla f,\qquad J=\begin{pmatrix}0_{3N}&I_{3N}\\-I_{3N}&0_{3N}\end{pmatrix};$$

## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$ and open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$,
a $C^{2}$ potential $U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ of dimension
$\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-2}$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing regularity
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Dimensions are read by
[[def-cm01-units]], the Euclidean product on $\mathbb R^{3N}$ is
[[def-euclidean-inner-product]], and differentiability classes are those of
[[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]. Write $n:=3N\ge1$. The
Cartesian momentum phase chart of the presentation is
$I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ with the momentum $p=\mathsf Mv$ of
[[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]], and the momentum phase chart of a
generalized-coordinate map $X:\Omega\to Q$ with $r$ coordinates is
$I_{0}\times\Omega\times\mathbb R^{r}$ with the generalized momentum
$P=G(q)v$, $G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$
([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]],
[[def-cm12-hyperregularity]]).
$$W:=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\subseteq\mathbb R^{2n},\qquad z=(x,p),$$
a phase domain in the sense of [[def-cm-phase-domain-and-standard-symplectic-two-form]]
(openness is clause (a) of [[def-cm01-configuration]]). On $W$ the
**canonical one-form of the presentation in the Cartesian chart** is the smooth
one-form
$$\lambda_{(x,p)}(\delta x,\delta p):=p\cdot\delta x\qquad\bigl((x,p)\in W,\ (\delta x,\delta p)\in\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N}\bigr),$$
([[def-smooth-differential-k-form]]). In coordinates, reading
$x=(x^{1},\dots,x^{3N})$ and $p=(p_{1},\dots,p_{3N})$ as the standard
coordinates of $W$,
$$\lambda=\sum_{a=1}^{3N}p_{a}\,dx^{a},\qquad \omega=\sum_{a=1}^{3N}dx^{a}\wedge dp_{a},\qquad \omega_{(x,p)}\bigl((\delta x,\delta p),(\delta x',\delta p')\bigr)=\delta x\cdot\delta p'-\delta p\cdot\delta x',$$
$$\Phi_{X}:\Omega\times\mathbb R^{r}\longrightarrow Q\times\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad \Phi_{X}(q,P):=\Bigl(X(q),\ \mathsf M\,DX(q)\,G(q)^{-1}P\Bigr).$$
Here $DX(q)$ is the $(3N)\times r$ Jacobian of $X$ at $q$
([[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]), $G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$
is symmetric positive definite ([[def-transpose-of-a-matrix]],
[[def-invertible-matrix-and-general-linear-group]]), and $P$ is a
generalized momentum. The image of $\Phi_{X}$ is
the momentum chart of the presentation in the coordinates $X$; indeed
$P\mapsto v=G(q)^{-1}P$ is a linear bijection of $\mathbb R^{r}$. The map
$\Phi_{X}$ is $C^{1}$ by the displayed algebra, and it is a $C^{1}$ immersion:
its derivative is injective at every point, because $DX(q)$ is injective
([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]],
[[def-euclidean-submersions-and-immersions]]) and $P\mapsto G(q)^{-1}P$ is a
linear bijection; when $r=3N$ its derivative is invertible and it is a local
diffeomorphism onto $W$
(equivalently $\mathsf M\,DX(q)G(q)^{-1}=DX(q)^{-\mathsf T}$: see the
recorded identity below). The **forms of the presentation in the
coordinates $X$** are the pullbacks
moreover, when $r=3N$ and $DX(q)$ is invertible,
$\mathsf M\,DX(q)G(q)^{-1}=DX(q)^{-\mathsf T}$, so that on an open
generalized-coordinate chart the chart map reads
$\Phi_{X}(q,P)=\bigl(X(q),DX(q)^{-\mathsf T}P\bigr)$ and the chart
transition between the two descriptions of the same momentum datum is a
$C^{1}$ local diffeomorphism.

## Definition
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential $U$ and residual forces $d$
of the standing regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Assume the presentation is hyperregular
on its charts, with the Hamiltonians $H$ and $\widetilde H$ and inverse
Legendre branches of [[def-cm12-hamiltonian]],
[[def-cm12-hyperregularity]],
[[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]]. The momentum charts, their forms
and their canonical coordinates are those of [[def-cm13-two-form]] and
[[def-cm13-canonical-form]]; dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]] and
differentiability classes are those of
[[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]].
**(a) The chart datum.** Fix one momentum chart of the presentation: either
the Cartesian chart with the coordinates $(t,x,p)$ on
$I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ and the Hamiltonian $H$ of
[[def-cm12-hamiltonian]] clause (a), or the chart of a
generalized-coordinate map $X:\Omega\to Q$ with $r$ coordinates, the
coordinates $(t,q,p)$ on $I_{0}\times\Omega\times\mathbb R^{r}$ and the
Hamiltonian $\widetilde H$ of clause (b). Write $m\in\{3N,r\}$ for the
number of momentum pairs, let $V\subseteq\mathbb R^{m}$ denote the
configuration-coordinate domain of the chart ($Q$ in the Cartesian case,
$\Omega$ in the other), and write $H$ for the corresponding Hamiltonian,

**(i) The scattering model.** A **central scattering model** is a model of
class (i) of [[post-cm05-central-model]] with $N=2$ labels, masses
$m_1,m_2>0$ ([[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)), no external forces, and a
static central pair potential $u\in C^2(J_0)$
([[def-cm-scattering-data]], [[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]])
whose collision-excluded interval satisfies $(0,\infty)\subseteq J_0$, so
that the only forbidden configuration is the collision $r=0$. Its **reduced
problem** is the one-label problem of class (ii) with reduced mass
$\mu:=m_1m_2/(m_1+m_2)$ ([[def-cm01-units]]: $[\mu]=\mathsf M$) and the same
potential, presented through the relative coordinate $r=x_1-x_2$; by
[[pthm-cm05-relative-reduction]] the relative coordinate of every motion of
the model is a central-force trajectory for $(\mu,u)$ and every pair of an
affine centre-of-mass motion and a solution of the reduced equation on a
common interval reconstructs a motion of the model.

      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"

      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"

**(i) The model class.** A **stability model** is a force model of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N\ge1$ labels, fixed masses
$m_1,\dots,m_N>0$ ([[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)), a time window
$I_0\subseteq\mathbb R$ with nonempty interior, an open configuration domain
$Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]]), and a force map
$F:D\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ on an open set
$D\subseteq I_0\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, continuous and locally Lipschitz
in the state variable, whose **motions** are the $C^2$ curves $x:J\to Q$ of
[[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]] (that is, $m_ix_i''=F_i(t,x,x')$ on $J$ and
$(t,x(t),x'(t))\in D$). The model is **autonomous** when $I_0=\mathbb R$ and
$D=\mathbb R\times S_0$ for an open **state domain**
$S_0\subseteq Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ with $F(x,v)$ independent of $t$, and
**$P$-periodic** for a period $P>0$ when $I_0=\mathbb R$ and
$F(t+P,x,v)=F(t,x,v)$ for all $(t,x,v)\in D$. All time intervals use the
frame-time parameter fixed by [[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]] with
$[t]=\tau$ ([[def-cm01-units]]).
**(ii) Associated field and flow.** Write $n:=3N$,
$\mathsf M:=\operatorname{diag}(m_1I_3,\dots,m_NI_3)$, and let
$$Z(t,x,v):=\bigl(v,\ \mathsf M^{-1}F(t,x,v)\bigr)$$
be the **associated first-order field** of the model, on the same domain $D$;
the state curve $z:=(x,x')$ of a motion is a solution of $z'=Z(t,z)$ and
conversely ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]] clause (c)). For an autonomous model
write $G:S_0\to\mathbb R^{6N}$, $G(x,v):=(v,\mathsf M^{-1}F(x,v))$; by the
standing regularity of $F$, the field $G$ is continuous and locally Lipschitz
in the state, so it has a **local flow**
$$\Phi:\Omega\longrightarrow S_0,\qquad \Phi(t,z_0):=z(t;z_0),$$
with maximal solutions $z(\,\cdot\,;z_0):I_{z_0}\to S_0$ and flow domain
$\Omega=\{(t,z_0):t\in I_{z_0}\}$, in the sense of
[[def-cm-local-flow-of-an-autonomous-system]]. The model's motions with state
curve through $z_0$ at time $0$ are exactly the curves $t\mapsto\Phi(t,z_0)$
restricted to their maximal intervals, and
$\Phi(s+t,z_0)=\Phi(t,\Phi(s,z_0))$ wherever both sides are defined
([[lem-cm-local-flow-composition-law]]). If in addition $F$ is of class $C^1$
in the state, then $\Omega$ is open and $\Phi$ is $C^1$ on it
([[cor-cm-flow-domain-open-and-flow-is-c1]]). No completeness of $\Phi$ is
asserted: the maximal interval $I_{z_0}$ may be bounded
([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]).
**(iii) Equilibria and periodic orbits.** A state $z_*=(x_*,v_*)\in S_0$ is an
**equilibrium state** of the autonomous model when $G(z_*)=0$, that is
$v_*=0$ and $F(x_*,0)=0$; under that condition the constant curve
$x(t)=x_*$ is a motion. A **periodic orbit** of the model is a motion
$x:\mathbb R\to Q$ that is not constant and satisfies $x(t+T)=x(t)$ for all
$t\in\mathbb R$ and some $T>0$; the infimum of all such $T$ is its **least
period**. A **limit cycle** (also **closed orbit**) is a periodic orbit whose
image is isolated among images of periodic orbits: there is an open set
$U\supseteq x(\mathbb R)$ such that no other periodic orbit has image meeting
$U$. A limit cycle's attraction is a separate property and is not part of this
definition.

The item records the distinctions between these notions and asserts no stability, instability or attraction of any particular model."
sources:

      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"

**(ii) Orbital stability of an invariant set.** Let $S\subseteq S_0$ be a
nonempty compact invariant set ([[def-cm16-flow-equilibria-and-invariant-sets]]
clause (iv)). Then $S$ is **orbitally stable** when for every open set
$U\supseteq S$ there is an open set $V$ with $S\subseteq V\subseteq U$ such
that every maximal solution $z$ with $z(0)\in V$ is defined for all $t\ge0$
and satisfies $z(t)\in U$ for all $t\ge0$. The distance used implicitly is
the orbital distance $d(z,S)$ of clause (iv) of the cited definition. Orbital
stability is a statement about the set, not about one motion, and it does not
assert attraction.

FILE def-cm17-traction-and-stress
---
id: def-cm17-traction-and-stress
kind: definition
title: "Traction fields and stress matrices of the continuum model, with units and sign conventions"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 10
deps: [def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units, post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model, def-cm-traction-and-stress, def-cm01-units, def-cm-continuum-motion]
dependency_roles:
  def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm-traction-and-stress: mathematical-premise
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm-continuum-motion: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "The model's contact-force quantity: a traction field assigning to each point and unit normal the force per unit area exerted across a surface, its dimensions, and the stress matrix representing it when one exists; the sign convention identifying compressive normal traction with negative scalar stress, the component reading, and the distinction from body-force densities. Existence of a representing stress matrix, its continuity and its symmetry are not asserted here; they are conditional conclusions of the balance items of this page. No measured force is asserted."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Section 4.3 (force, Cauchy's hypothesis for contact forces, traction and the stress tensor)"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
    - title: "K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Classical Mechanics (September 1, 2026 complete volume), Section 7.2 (the stress tensor and the force on a surface element)"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
A **traction field of the model** is a map
read with the convention of [[def-cm-traction-and-stress]]: $t(s,x,n)$ is the
force per unit area exerted **by the material on the side towards which $n$
points upon the material on the other side**, at the point $x$ of a surface
whose unit normal is $n$, at time $s$. Its dimension is that of force per
area, $[t]=\mu\ell^{-1}\tau^{-2}$, read in $\mathrm{N\,m^{-2}}$
([[def-cm01-units]]).
A $C^1$ field of matrices $\sigma:U\to M_3(\mathbb R)$ **represents** the
traction field when
the product being matrix-vector multiplication. The existence of such a
$\sigma$ for a given traction field is **not asserted here**: it is a
conditional conclusion of the balance items of this page. When it exists,
$\sigma(s,x)$ is the **stress matrix** of the model at that event, and its
entries read $\sigma_{ij}=\bigl(t(s,x,e_j)\bigr)_i$: the $i$-th component of
the traction across the surface with normal $e_j$.
The isotropic matrix $-\lambda I_3$ with $\lambda\in\mathbb R$ represents the
**normal** traction $t(n)=-\lambda n$: under the convention above, positive
$\lambda$ is a compressive normal traction and $\lambda$ is called the
**pressure** in this sign convention, while $\sigma_{11}=\sigma_{22}
=\sigma_{33}=0$ and $\sigma_{12}=\sigma_{13}=\sigma_{23}=0$ describe a
pressure-free state. The **body-force density** is a separate object: a
continuous field $b$ with the dimension of acceleration, $[b]=\ell\tau^{-2}$,
whose force per unit volume is $\rho b$. A traction field need not satisfy
$t(-n)=-t(n)$ and its representing matrix, when one exists, need not be
symmetric; both properties are separate conditional conclusions.
- **Cauchy's hypothesis is a modelling assumption.** The definition lets the
  traction depend on the surface only through its unit normal at the point.
  Contact interactions that depend on the curvature of the surface, on a
  surface couple distribution, or on a thin boundary layer would require a
  richer object; the counterexample item of the companion page shows that the
  global symmetry $t(-n)=-t(n)$ alone, without a balance law, does not even
  force the existence of a representing matrix.
- **Units and interpretation.** The values of $t$ and $\sigma$ are model
  quantities in the fixed frame of [[def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units]]. A
  measured force per area is related to them only through a measurement model
  and is not asserted by this definition; no measured stress is recorded on
  this page.
- **Sign convention.** The convention is the one of [[def-cm-traction-and-stress]]:
  $t(n)$ acts on the material towards which $n$ points. With this convention
  a fluid at positive pressure $p$ has $\sigma=-pI_3$, so positive $p$ gives
  compression in the direction $-n$.

FILE def-cm18-observable-model
---
id: def-cm18-observable-model
kind: definition
title: "Operational observable model of the force branch: parameters, preparations, observation times, readout, units and additive error"
status: draft
origin: pipeline
domain: physics
deps: [post-cm01-frames, post-cm01-worldlines, def-cm01-configuration, def-cm01-units, post-cm02-force-superposition, def-cm02-newtonian-flow, pthm-cm02-ivp, def-cm-measurement-model, def-probability-measure, def-random-element-and-real-random-variable]
dependency_roles:
  post-cm01-frames: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm01-worldlines: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm01-configuration: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm02-force-superposition: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm02-newtonian-flow: formulation-prerequisite
  pthm-cm02-ivp: physical-result
  def-cm-measurement-model: mathematical-premise
  def-probability-measure: mathematical-premise
  def-random-element-and-real-random-variable: mathematical-premise
dependency_level: 11
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "Operational observable models of an adopted force model: a parameter set indexing force maps, a preparation map assigning initial data, a finite family of observation times lying in the maximal motions, a declared readout of the model states, declared units for the readout coordinates, a probability space and an additive error random element with values in a declared open error domain. The item fixes the interface between the force branch and the measurement-model machinery of the companion mathematics page; it asserts no distributional, moment, independence or calibration hypothesis, no apparatus realization and no measurement, and it does not claim that any physical situation satisfies the protocol."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "JCGM 100:2008, Evaluation of measurement data - Guide to the expression of uncertainty in measurement (BIPM, 2008 corrected version), clauses 4.1.1-4.1.4 and equation (1): the measurand function Y = f(X1, ..., XN), the input quantities and the correction quantities; clause 4.1.3 on values brought in from calibrated standards"
      url: "https://www.bipm.org/documents/20126/2071204/JCGM_100_2008_E.pdf"
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Section 1.2, chapter pages 3-4 (PDF pages 7-8): the equation of motion m a = F as model input with the force law and its parameters supplied by the model, and the empirical status of the law itself"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 1.3, printed pages 5-9 (PDF pages 11-15): external forces as declared model contributions and the open-system warning that a force whose source is not modelled must be declared as external"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
Fix a force model of the force branch [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: a
nondegenerate open time window $I_{0}\subseteq\mathbb R$, an open configuration
domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ for $N\ge1$ particle labels, with the
collision set removed when the model's force law is singular there
([[def-cm01-configuration]] clause (a)), fixed masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$
([[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)), and a force map
$$F:I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}\longrightarrow(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$$
with the standing continuity and state-local-Lipschitz hypotheses of clause
(iii) of that postulate. Fix one inertial frame of the model with adapted
coordinates $(t,x)$ ([[post-cm01-frames]]) and read all times and positions
below in this frame; dimensions and units are those of [[def-cm01-units]].
1. **Parameters.** A nonempty set $\Theta$, the **model parameters**, with a
   family $(F_{\theta})_{\theta\in\Theta}$ of force maps on
   $I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$, each satisfying the standing
   hypotheses of the force branch, so that $\bigl(I_{0},Q,(m_{i}),F_{\theta}\bigr)$
   is a force model of that branch for every $\theta\in\Theta$.
2. **Preparations.** A nonempty set $Z$, the **apparatus and preparation
   settings**, and a map $\zeta:Z\to I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$,
   written $\zeta(z)=\bigl(t_{0}(z),x_{0}(z),v_{0}(z)\bigr)$, assigning to
   each setting an initial datum of the model.
3. **Observation times.** A finite strictly increasing family
   $t_{1}<\dots<t_{k}$ in $I_{0}$ with $k\ge1$, the **observation times**.
4. **Definedness.** For every $(\theta,z)\in\Theta\times Z$ the model with
   force map $F_{\theta}$ and initial datum $\zeta(z)$ has a unique maximal
   motion $x_{\theta,z}:J_{\theta,z}\to Q$, with $J_{\theta,z}\subseteq
   I_{0}$ an open interval containing $t_{0}(z)$
   ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]], [[pthm-cm02-ivp]]), and the protocol requires
   $[t_{1},t_{k}]\subseteq J_{\theta,z}$. Its **state** at $t_{j}$ is
   $$s_{\theta,z}(t_{j}):=\bigl(x_{\theta,z}(t_{j}),\,w_{\theta,z}(t_{j})\bigr) \in Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N},$$
   where $w_{\theta,z}:=x_{\theta,z}'$ is the coordinate velocity.
5. **Readout.** An integer $d\ge1$ and a map
   $$R:\Theta\times Z\times\bigl(\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N}\bigr)^{k} \longrightarrow\mathbb R^{d},$$
   the **readout**, whose value at $(\theta,z)$ is the tuple of the $k$
   states of clause 4 in order; the **prediction map** is
   $$\Phi:\Theta\times Z\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{d},\qquad \Phi(\theta,z):=R\bigl(\theta,z,s_{\theta,z}(t_{1}),\dots, s_{\theta,z}(t_{k})\bigr).$$
   No regularity of $R$ in the parameters is assumed; continuity,
   differentiability or measurability in $\theta$ or in the states is an
   additional hypothesis, declared where it is used.
6. **Units.** For each $j\le d$ a dimension $[\Phi_{j}]$ from
   [[def-cm01-units]] is declared, and the coordinate $\Phi_{j}(\theta,z)$ is
   read in the corresponding unit.
7. **Error and observation.** A probability space $(\Omega,\mathfrak F,
   \mathbb P)$ ([[def-probability-measure]]), an open **error domain**
   $V\subseteq\mathbb R^{d}$ with $0\in V$, and a random element
   $\varepsilon:\Omega\to\mathbb R^{d}$ with $\varepsilon(\omega)\in V$ for
   every $\omega$ ([[def-random-element-and-real-random-variable]]). The
   **observation** at $(\theta,z)$ is the random element
   $$Y_{\theta,z}:=\Phi(\theta,z)+\varepsilon:\Omega\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{d},$$
   the sum being read coefficientwise in the declared units, so that the
   coordinate $\varepsilon_{j}$ carries the dimension $[\Phi_{j}]$ for every
   $j$ (rule (b) of [[def-cm01-units]]).

FILE ex-cm-mass-metric-on-a-sphere
---
id: ex-cm-mass-metric-on-a-sphere
kind: example
title: "The mass metric of a particle constrained to the unit sphere in spherical coordinates"
status: draft
origin: pipeline
domain: mathematics
dependency_level: 2
deps: [def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric, lem-cm-mass-metric-positivity, def-euclidean-spheres-and-closed-balls, def-total-derivative-in-euclidean-space, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-sine-and-cosine-by-power-series, def-immersion-submersion-and-constant-rank-map]

  lem-cm-mass-metric-positivity: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-spheres-and-closed-balls: mathematical-premise
  def-total-derivative-in-euclidean-space: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-inner-product: mathematical-premise
  def-sine-and-cosine-by-power-series: mathematical-premise
  def-immersion-submersion-and-constant-rank-map: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, 2015), Section 3.1 (configuration space) and Section 2.1 (generalized coordinates)"
      url: "https://www.damtp.cam.ac.uk/user/tong/dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Appendix C (curvilinear coordinates), printed pages 160-162"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
Let $Q:=S^2=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:\|x\|_2=1\}$ be the unit sphere ([[def-euclidean-spheres-and-closed-balls]]), let $X:Q\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^3$ be the inclusion, and let $m>0$ be the mass of one particle, so that the mass matrix of [[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]] is $M=mI_3$.
The mass metric of the inclusion is $G_q(u,v)=m\langle u,v\rangle_2$ for $u,v\in T_qS^2$, hence positive definite at every point by [[lem-cm-mass-metric-positivity]]. On the chart domain $0<\theta<\pi$, $0<\varphi<2\pi$ consider the parametrization
Its differential is injective on the chart domain, and in the coordinate basis $(\partial_\theta,\partial_\varphi)$ the mass metric has the classical spherical form
[L1] The mass metric of $(Q,X,m)$ is $G_q(u,v)=m\langle u,v\rangle_2$ for the inclusion and tangent vectors $u,v$, and it is positive definite ([[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]], [[lem-cm-mass-metric-positivity]]).
3.1 Since $\Psi$ is the parametrization of the inclusion chart, the mass metric in these coordinates is $G_{ij}=m\langle\partial_i\Psi,\partial_j\Psi\rangle_2$; by step 2.1 its matrix is $m\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\0&\sin^2\theta\end{pmatrix}$, so for a coordinate velocity $(\dot\theta,\dot\varphi)$ the kinetic energy is $T=\tfrac12 m(\dot\theta^2+\sin^2\theta\,\dot\varphi^2)$, and the metric is positive definite on the chart domain because $\sin\theta\ne0$. [step 1.1, step 2.1, L1, algebra] ∎
- The coordinate expression degenerates at $\theta=0$ and $\theta=\pi$, but this is a property of the chart, not of the mass metric: the chart domain excludes the poles, where the parametrization is not an immersion. The metric of the inclusion is positive definite on the whole sphere, as recorded above, and any consumer working at a pole must use a different chart.

[L3] The mass operator multiplies the spatial components of a position tuple by the masses $m_{i}$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]]).
5.1 Finally the constraint function of the two-particle example has dimension $\ell^{2}$; the level equation $G(x)=0$ relates a quantity of dimension $\ell^{2}$ to the number $0$, which is admissible because $0$ is the neutral element of addition in every dimension, and multiplying $G$ by a nonzero constant of any fixed dimension produces the same zero set with the same ranks of $DG$ on it, as recorded with the definition of the configuration stage; all quantities and laws of this page are therefore dimensionally consistent under the stated conventions. [step 4.1, algebra] ∎
- The dimension of the generalized coordinates of the constrained branch is fixed by the model of the constraint, not by the ambient positions: on a configuration manifold a generalized coordinate may carry any declared dimension, and the dimensions of its velocity and momentum then follow by the same rules; the conventions of [[def-cm01-units]] record this together with the rule that only equal dimensions are added.

      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"

      url: "https://math.mit.edu/~hrm/palestine/lee-smooth-manifolds.pdf"

  precheck: pass

of [[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]], where
$\langle\,\cdot\,,\cdot\,\rangle_{2}$ is the Euclidean inner product of
[[def-euclidean-inner-product]].
with the ambient set $O=\mathbb R^{6}$ and $k=1$ in the notation of
[[def-cm01-configuration]]. The zero set $Q:=G^{-1}(0)$ is the configuration
space of two particles whose mutual distance is the fixed length $\ell$; the
case is a holonomic constraint branch of the definition.
[L1] Configuration domains: the unconstrained domain of the model is a stated open set $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$, the collision exclusion removes the set $\Delta$ when the force law is undefined there, and the holonomic branch takes $Q=G^{-1}(0)$ for a smooth $G$ with nonempty zero set and $DG$ of rank $k$ on it; the degrees of freedom are $\dim Q=3N-k$, where $N$ is the number of labels and $k$ the number of independent constraint components ([[def-cm01-configuration]]).
[L3] The particle configuration $(Q,X)$ with inclusion $X:Q\hookrightarrow\mathbb R^{6}$, the mass matrix $M(x_{1},x_{2})=(m_{1}x_{1},m_{2}x_{2})$ and the mass metric $G_{q}(u,v)=\langle dX_{q}(u),M\,dX_{q}(v)\rangle_{6}$ ([[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]]).
3.1 By the regular level set theorem [L2], applied to the smooth map $G:\mathbb R^{6}\to\mathbb R$ with the regular value $0$, the set $Q=G^{-1}(0)$ is an embedded submanifold of $\mathbb R^{6}$ of dimension $6-1=5$; by [L1] the rigid-rod branch therefore has $5=3\cdot2-1$ configuration degrees of freedom, in agreement with the identification of the same constraint in the cited treatment. With the masses $m_{1},m_{2}$, the pair consisting of $Q$ and the inclusion is a particle configuration whose mass matrix is $M=\operatorname{diag}(m_{1}I_{3},m_{2}I_{3})$ and whose mass metric is that of [L3]; the constraint function $G$ has dimension $\ell^{2}$, and rescaling it by any nonzero constant leaves both its zero set and the rank condition on that set unchanged, as recorded in [L1]. [step 2.1, L1, L2, L3, algebra] ∎
- The example exhibits both branches of [[def-cm01-configuration]] for the same two-particle model: without the rod the model uses the open domain $Q_{0}$ with the collision configurations excluded; with the rod it uses the five-dimensional embedded submanifold $Q$. The two are alternatives of one model, not descriptions of one system at one time, and each would be paired with its own force law when the dynamics is adopted.

- **The inverse-square constant is not uniform on the whole domain.** The
  Lipschitz constant $k(r^{-3}+3R^{3}r^{-6})$ blows up as the annulus
  approaches the excluded center, which is exactly the statement that the
  definition's local Lipschitz property is local, not global, and that the
  cylinder sizes in [[pthm-cm02-ivp]] shrink for data near the collision
  configuration.

FILE ex-cm02-uniform-field
Work in the force model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N$ labels,
time window $I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3N}$, fixed
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, and fix a vector
$\mathbf g\in\mathbb R^{3}$ with dimension $[\mathbf g]=\ell\tau^{-2}$,
read in metres per second squared ([[def-cm01-units]]); $\mathbf g$ is a
**declared parameter** of the model, not a measured value. The **uniform-force
model** is the force map
1.1 The map $F_{i}(t,x,v)=m_{i}\mathbf g$ is constant on $D=\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, hence continuous there; and for every pair of states $(t,z),(t,z')$ one has $\lVert F(t,z)-F(t,z')\rVert_{2}=0\le 0\cdot\lVert z-z'\rVert_{2}$, so the local-Lipschitz requirement of clause (iii) of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] holds on every compact cylinder with constant $L=0$, in particular with a constant not depending on time; this proves claim 1. [given, L1, algebra]
1.2 Differentiate the displayed curve: by [L2] applied to the three components, $\dot x(t)=v_{0}+\mathbf g\,t$ and $\ddot x(t)=\mathbf g$ for every $t\in\mathbb R$; hence $m_{i}\ddot x_{i}(t)=m_{i}\mathbf g=F_{i}(t,x(t),\dot x(t))$ for every $i$, and $x(t)\in Q=\mathbb R^{3N}$, $t\in I_{0}=\mathbb R$; the initial values $x(0)=x_{0}$ and $\dot x(0)=v_{0}$ are immediate. By the equivalence of clause (c) of [[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]], $x$ is a motion of the model on $\mathbb R$. [given, L1, L2, algebra]

FILE ex-cm03-driven-mechanical-energy-balance
Work in the force branch of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with one label
$N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window $I_{0}=\mathbb R$ and configuration domain
$Q=\mathbb R$, and declare the **potential presentation** of
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]] clause (a)
$$U(t,x):=\frac12\lambda\,t\,x^{2},\qquad d(t,x,v):=2ma+\lambda a\,t^{3},$$
where $a>0$ is a constant of dimension $\ell\tau^{-2}$ and $\lambda>0$ a
constant of dimension $\mu\tau^{-3}$, so that $U$ has dimension
$\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-2}$ and $d$ has the dimension of a force
([[def-cm01-units]]). Both maps are smooth; the force map of the model is
$F(t,x,v)=-\partial_{x}U(t,x)+d(t,x,v)=-\lambda tx+2ma+\lambda at^{3}$, and
the model is a potential presentation with residual force $d$ in the sense of
that definition.

FILE ex-cm03-two-body-hooke-model-check
Fix the two-body model of [[post-cm02-interactions]] clause (iv) (the general
pair-potential example) with $N=2$, masses $m_{1}=m_{2}=m>0$, time window
$\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{6}$ (the potential below is
smooth everywhere, so no collision exclusion is needed), and the **Hooke pair
potential**
$$U(x_{1},x_{2}):=\frac12k\,\lVert x_{1}-x_{2}\rVert_{2}^{2},$$
with a declared **spring constant** $k>0$ of dimension $\mu\tau^{-2}$
([[def-cm01-units]]), so that $U$ has energy dimension. The force map is
$$F_{1}(x)=-k\,(x_{1}-x_{2}),\qquad F_{2}(x)=k\,(x_{1}-x_{2}),$$
the two negative gradients of $U$; it is linear in the positions and
independent of time and velocities, with
$\lVert F(x)-F(x')\rVert_{2}\le2k\lVert x-x'\rVert_{2}$, hence continuous and
state-locally Lipschitz with the uniform constant $2k$. The model therefore
satisfies the standing hypotheses of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] clause
(iii), it has no external forces ($F_{i}^{\mathrm{ext}}\equiv0$), and its
pair force is central: $F_{12}=-k(x_{1}-x_{2})$ is parallel to
$x_{1}-x_{2}$. It presents its force map through the global potential $U$ in
the sense of [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]] clause (a).

Declare a one-label model of the force branch of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$ and force map
$$F(t,x,y,z):=(0,\,0,\,-mg)$$
with a constant $g>0$ of dimension $\ell\tau^{-2}$ (the uniform downward
field; dimension conventions of [[def-cm01-units]]). Constrain the label,
without friction, to the straight wire through the origin that rotates in the
plane $z=0$ about the $z$-axis with constant angular speed $\Omega>0$, and to
that plane, by the regular holonomic-constraint datum
$$O:=\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3},\quad k:=2,\qquad G(t,x,y,z):=\bigl(-x\sin\Omega t+y\cos\Omega t,\ z\bigr)$$
of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]], and adopt the ideal-reaction law
of [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]].
[F1] The force branch and the mass operator: $F$ is constant, hence continuous and state-Lipschitz, and of class $C^{1}$; the mass operator is $\mathsf M=mI_{3}$, symmetric positive definite ([[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]).
[F2] The holonomic-constraint branch ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]): $G$ is of class $C^{2}$ with $D_{x}G$ of rank $k$ on $\Sigma$; virtual displacements are $\ker D_{x}G(t,x)$; the datum is scleronomic exactly when there is $N\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ with $\Sigma=\{(t,x)\in O:x\in N\}$, equivalently when $(t,x)\in\Sigma$ and $(t',x)\in O$ always imply $(t',x)\in\Sigma$, and then $G_{t}=0$ on $\Sigma$; a constrained motion satisfies $\mathsf Mx''=F+R$ with a continuous $R$.
1.1 The datum is a regular holonomic-constraint datum with $k=2=3N-1$: $G$ is smooth, and its Jacobian is the constant-rank matrix $$D_{x}G(t,x,y,z)=\begin{pmatrix}-\sin\Omega t&\cos\Omega t&0\\ 0&0&1\end{pmatrix};$$ the two rows are independent for every $t$ because a combination $a(-\sin\Omega t,\cos\Omega t,0)+b(0,0,1)=(0,0,0)$ has $b=0$ from the third entry and then $a=0$ from the first two entries, since $(-\sin\Omega t,\cos\Omega t)\ne(0,0)$ by [F5]. The locus is $\Sigma=\{(t,r\cos\Omega t,r\sin\Omega t,0):t,r\in\mathbb R\}$, nonempty and contained in $I_{0}\times Q$, and the virtual displacements at $(t,x)$ are the one-dimensional space $\mathbb R(\cos\Omega t,\sin\Omega t,0)$ because $\delta\in\ker D_{x}G$ means $\delta_{z}=0$ and $-\delta_{x}\sin\Omega t+\delta_{y}\cos\Omega t=0$. Finally the datum is not scleronomic: $(0,(1,0,0))\in\Sigma$ because $G(0,1,0,0)=(0,0)$, while for $t_{1}:=\pi/(2\Omega)$ one has $G_{1}(t_{1},1,0,0)=-\sin(\pi/2)=-1\ne0$, so $(t_{1},(1,0,0))\in O=\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3}$ but $(t_{1},(1,0,0))\notin\Sigma$; by the membership form of [F2] the datum is rheonomic. [F1, F2, F5, algebra]

[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window

with a constant $g>0$ of dimension $\ell\tau^{-2}$. Constrain the label,
without friction, to the circle of radius $l>0$ about the origin in the plane
$z=0$ --- the ideal pendulum realized as a bead threaded on a smooth circular
wire in a vertical plane --- by the regular holonomic-constraint datum
$$O:=\{(t,x,y,z)\in\mathbb R^{4}:(x,y)\ne(0,0)\},\qquad k:=2,\qquad G(t,x,y,z):=\bigl(x^{2}+y^{2}-l^{2},\ z\bigr)$$
of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]], and adopt the ideal-reaction law
of [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]]. The locus is
$\Sigma=\{(t,l\sin\theta,-l\cos\theta,0):t,\theta\in\mathbb R\}$; write
$$X(\theta):=\bigl(l\sin\theta,\,-l\cos\theta,\,0\bigr),\qquad u(\theta):=\bigl(\sin\theta,\,-\cos\theta,\,0\bigr),\qquad X(\theta)=l\,u(\theta).$$
[F1] The force branch and mass operator: $F$ is constant, hence of the standing regularity and of class $C^{1}$, with potential $U(t,x,y,z)=mgy$ and residual forces zero, $F=-\nabla U$ ([[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]] clause (a), [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]).
[F2] The holonomic-constraint branch ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]): $G$ is smooth with $D_{x}G=\begin{pmatrix}2x&2y&0\\0&0&1\end{pmatrix}$ of rank $2$ on $O$; virtual displacements are $\ker D_{x}G$; the datum is scleronomic exactly when there is $N\subseteq\mathbb R^{3}$ with $\Sigma=\{(t,x)\in O:x\in N\}$ (equivalently when $(t,x)\in\Sigma$ and $(t',x)\in O$ imply $(t',x)\in\Sigma$), and then $G_{t}=0$ on $\Sigma$; a constrained motion satisfies $\mathsf Mx''=F+R$ with a continuous ideal reaction.
[F3] The ideal-reaction law and the balance theorem ([[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]], [[pthm-cm06-multiplier-balance]], [[def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions]]): $R=D_{x}G^{\mathsf T}\lambda$ with the unique continuous multiplier $\lambda=\Lambda(t,x,x')\in\mathbb R^{2}$; hypotheses (h1)-(h2) of the balance theorem hold here because $G$ is smooth with $D_{x}G$ of rank $2$ on all of $O$ by [F2] and $F$ is constant; with consistent data the constrained motions are the solutions of the first-order system and have a unique maximal one through each datum; the power identity $R\cdot x'=-\lambda\cdot G_{t}$ holds.
5.1 Clauses 1-5 of the Example are proved: the datum is a regular scleronomic holonomic constraint realizing the circle $x^{2}+y^{2}=l^{2}$ in the plane $z=0$, the multiplier has the displayed two components with the second vanishing along every constrained motion, the reaction is the displayed tension, the pendulum equation is the tangential projection of the constrained equation, the ideal reaction performs no work, the mechanical energy is conserved, and the finite data give a positive tension on the reachable arc. [step 1.1, step 1.2, step 2.1, step 2.2, step 3.1, step 4.1] ∎

title: "Rolling as distinct models: the planar ideal no-slip disk against frictionless sliding, and the contact capability condition"

**(M1) ideal no-slip model.** The holonomic constraint
$$\mathcal G(x,\theta):=x-a\theta,\qquad \mathcal G_{q}=(1,-a),$$
imposed as $\mathcal G=0$; the Jacobian has rank one. With the d'Alembert
ideal-reaction law of
[[def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions]] and virtual displacements
$\delta\in\ker\mathcal G_{q}=\mathbb R(a,1)$: the generalized reaction is
$\mathcal G_{q}^{\mathsf T}\lambda$ for a multiplier $\lambda$, and the
motions satisfy $Mq''=\Phi+\mathcal G_{q}^{\mathsf T}\lambda$.
1. in **M1** the ideal motions satisfy
   $$\Bigl(m+\frac{I}{a^{2}}\Bigr)x''=f,\qquad x''=\frac{fa^{2}}{I+ma^{2}},\qquad \theta''=\frac{af}{I+ma^{2}},$$
   with the unique multiplier $\lambda=-\dfrac{fI}{I+ma^{2}}$, reaction
   $\mathcal G_{q}^{\mathsf T}\lambda=(\lambda,-a\lambda)$ and reaction power
   identically zero; the planar straight-line no-slip constraint is
   holonomic-equivalent, since $x-a\theta$ is constant along every admissible
   curve;
2. in **M2** the motions satisfy $x''=f/m$ and $\theta''=0$;
3. the two models make different predictions for the same applied force: for
   $f\ne0$ and $a^{2}m>0$,
   $$\frac{x''_{\mathrm{M1}}}{x''_{\mathrm{M2}}}=\frac{ma^{2}}{I+ma^{2}}=\frac{1}{1+I/(ma^{2})}<1,$$
   equal to $\tfrac23$ for a uniform thin disk $I=\tfrac12ma^{2}$ and to
   $\tfrac12$ for a hoop $I=ma^{2}$;
4. the ideal no-slip model requires the contact to supply the generalized
   reaction $(\lambda,-a\lambda)$, that is a contact force of magnitude
   $|\lambda|=|f|I/(I+ma^{2})$ along the line of rolling together with its
   torque; if the normal component of the contact force is $N=mg$ (horizontal
   plane, weight only) and the static-friction coefficient of the declared
   contact model is $\mu_{s}$, the no-slip idealization is consistent with the
   declared contact model only while $|\lambda|\le\mu_{s}N$; for the uniform
   disk this is $|f|\le3\mu_{s}mg$. Where the required force exceeds the
   contact capability, the no-slip constraint cannot be maintained and a
   slipping model (with kinetic friction as a separately declared force)
   applies instead;
5. on the explicit model data $m=1\,\mathrm{kg}$, $a=1\,\mathrm m$,
   $I=\tfrac12\,\mathrm{kg}\,\mathrm m^{2}$, $g=10\,\mathrm m\,\mathrm s^{-2}$
   and $f=3\,\mathrm N$: $x''_{\mathrm{M1}}=2\,\mathrm m\,\mathrm s^{-2}$,
   $x''_{\mathrm{M2}}=3\,\mathrm m\,\mathrm s^{-2}$,
   $\lambda=-1\,\mathrm N$ and $|\lambda|=1\,\mathrm N$, within
   $\mu_{s}N=5\,\mathrm N$ for the declared $\mu_{s}=\tfrac12$; with
   $f=18\,\mathrm N$ instead, M1 requires $|\lambda|=6\,\mathrm N>5\,\mathrm N$,
   so under those parameters the declared contact model cannot maintain the
   no-slip constraint and M1's premises fail.
[F1] The ideal-constraint mathematics ([[def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions]], [[lem-cm-constraint-multipliers-and-accelerations]], [[prop-cm-ideal-holonomic-system-in-a-time-dependent-chart]]): for a $C^{2}$ regular constraint $\mathcal G$ with $\mathcal G_{q}$ of rank $k$, $M$ symmetric positive definite, $C=\mathcal G_{q}M^{-1}\mathcal G_{q}^{\mathsf T}$ is symmetric positive definite and invertible, the ideal-constraint motions with permitted velocity are exactly the solutions of the first-order system with multiplier $\Lambda=-C^{-1}[\mathcal G_{q}M^{-1}\Phi+\mathcal G_{tt}+2\mathcal G_{tq}[v]+\mathcal G_{qq}[v,v]]$ and acceleration $a=M^{-1}(\Phi+\mathcal G_{q}^{\mathsf T}\Lambda)$, and the reaction power satisfies $R\cdot q'=-\Lambda\cdot\mathcal G_{t}$; the multiplier is unique, and a vector orthogonal to $\ker\mathcal G_{q}$ is $\mathcal G_{q}^{\mathsf T}\lambda$ with $\lambda=C^{-1}\mathcal G_{q}M^{-1}R$ ([[lem-cm-constraint-multipliers-and-accelerations]]).
[F5] The nonholonomic classification, read for this declared reduced model in the coordinates $(x,\theta)$ ([[def-cm06-nonholonomic-model]] clause (d): a constant-rank affine law is holonomic-equivalent when it has the form $A=\varrho\Phi_{q}$, $b=\varrho\Phi_{t}$ for a $C^{2}$ map $\Phi$ and a nowhere-vanishing $C^{2}$ factor $\varrho$, so that it integrates to the position constraint $\Phi=$ constant along admissible curves): step 1.1 exhibits the pair $\Phi=x-a\theta$, $\varrho=1$ for the planar straight-line no-slip law; general three-dimensional rolling is reported nonholonomic; rank and contact changes are outside the comparison.
1.1 The M1 constraint data and its holonomic-equivalence: $\mathcal G_{q}=(1,-a)$ has rank one, $\ker\mathcal G_{q}=\{(a\delta,\delta):\delta\in\mathbb R\}$ as a set of virtual displacements, and along any admissible curve $x'-a\theta'=0$; hence $\mathcal G(q(t))$ is constant by [F4], so the constraint is the differentiated position constraint $\mathcal G=$ constant and is holonomic-equivalent in the sense of [F5]. [F2, F4, F5, algebra]

FILE ex-cm06-rotating-chart-inertial-terms
Declare a one-label model of the force branch of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m=1\,\mathrm{kg}$, time
window $I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$ and the
**zero force map** $F\equiv0$ (admissible: constant, hence of the standing
regularity), in a fixed inertial frame $F$ with adapted coordinates $(t,x)$
([[post-cm01-frames]], [[def-cm01-units]]). For real parameters
$\omega_{0},\beta,\gamma$, vectors $a_{0},v_{c}\in\mathbb R^{3}$ and
[[def-cm06-moving-frame]] data
$$\theta(t):=\omega_{0}t+\tfrac12\beta t^{2},\qquad R(t):=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\theta(t)&-\sin\theta(t)&0\\ \sin\theta(t)&\cos\theta(t)&0\\ 0&0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad c(t):=a_{0}+v_{c}t+\tfrac12\gamma t^{2}e_{1},$$
consider the motion $x(t):=x_{0}+v\,t$ of the zero-force model with
$x_{0}=(1,0,0)\,\mathrm m$, $v=(0,1,0)\,\mathrm{m}\,\mathrm{s}^{-1}$, its
moving-chart coordinate curve $y(t)=R(t)^{\mathsf T}(x(t)-c(t))$, and the
inertial-terms field
$\tau(t,y,\dot y)=-\bigl(2\omega(t)\times\dot y+\omega'(t)\times y+\omega(t)\times(\omega(t)\times y)+R(t)^{\mathsf T}c''(t)\bigr)$.

id: ex-cm07-nonholonomic-unrestricted-action-fails

title: "The unrestricted action principle fails for the nonholonomic constraint z' = x y': the d'Alembert motion is not vakonomic"

  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "A worked check on the nonholonomic constraint z' = x y' with unit mass and zero applied force: the d'Alembert motion from the stated initial data has strictly positive y' and nonzero y'' on the positive half-line, so it cannot satisfy the vakonomic (unrestricted-action) equations of the same constraint, which force y'' to vanish wherever x'' does. Consequently the unrestricted action principle is not a substitute for the d'Alembert prescription on this branch. The example reports exact model computations, not measurements."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "J. Cortes, M. de Leon, D. Martin de Diego and S. Martinez, Geometric description of vakonomic and nonholonomic dynamics. Comparison of solutions (SIAM J. Control Optim., author PDF), Sections 1, 2 and 5"
      url: "http://terrano.ucsd.edu/jorge/publications/data/2003_CoLeMaMa-sicon.pdf"
    - title: "D. Cline, Variational Principles in Classical Mechanics (2nd edition, University of Rochester River Campus Libraries, open textbook, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0), Sections 5.7 and 6.9: constrained variational systems and the virtual-work treatment of non-holonomic constraints"
      url: "https://downloads.libretexts.org/api/v1/download/phys-9557/pdf"

## Example
Adopt the constraint model of [[def-cm-nonholonomic-velocity-constraint-law]]:
unit mass in $\mathbb R^{3}$ with coordinates $(x,y,z)$, configuration domain
$\mathbb R^{3}$, the velocity constraint law $z'=xy'$, the d'Alembert
prescription $x''=0$, $y''=-\lambda x$, $z''=\lambda$ with
$\lambda=x'y'/(1+x^{2})$ along motions, and the vakonomic prescription
$x''=-\eta y'$, $y''=\eta'x+\eta x'$, $z''=-\eta'$ for the multiplier $\eta$
of the augmented action. Consider the initial data
$$(x,y,z)(0)=(0,0,0),\qquad(x',y',z')(0)=(1,1,0).$$
1. the curve
   $$x(t):=t,\qquad y(t):=\int_{0}^{t}\frac{ds}{\sqrt{1+s^{2}}},\qquad z(t):=\int_{0}^{t}s\,y'(s)\,ds=\sqrt{1+t^{2}}-1$$
   is a d'Alembert motion with these data, and it satisfies
   $y'(t)=(1+t^{2})^{-1/2}>0$ and
   $y''(t)=-t\,(1+t^{2})^{-3/2}$ for every $t\ge0$;
2. $y''(1/2)=-4/(5\sqrt{5})\ne0$;
3. consequently this d'Alembert motion is not a vakonomic motion of the same
   constraint with the same data: a vakonomic motion with $x(t)=t$ on an
   interval where $y'>0$ would have $\eta\equiv0$ there and hence $y''\equiv0$,
   contradicting clause 2;
4. hence the unrestricted action procedure — minimising the substituted or
   augmented action — does not reproduce the model's d'Alembert evolution on
   this nonholonomic branch.
[F1] The constraint model ([[def-cm-nonholonomic-velocity-constraint-law]]): the allowed velocities and virtual displacements are $D(q)=\{(w_{x},w_{y},w_{z}):w_{z}=xw_{y}\}$; the d'Alembert equations with zero applied force and the vakonomic equations are those displayed in the Example; the model's sample Lagrangian is $\tfrac12\lvert v\rvert^{2}$.
[F4] The model setting ([[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]], [[def-euclidean-inner-product]]): the constraint model is posed for a one-label force model with vanishing force map; the quantities are read in the stated coordinates with the framework's dimensions.
4.1 Clause 4: since the d'Alembert motion with the stated data is not vakonomic, the two prescriptions differ on this branch, and the unrestricted action procedure cannot replace the d'Alembert equation of the model. This conclusion is confined to the displayed constraint and data; the holonomic reduction of this page concerns the separate holonomic branch. [F1, F2, step 3.1] ∎

## Example
Declare a one-label model of the force branch of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R$ and force map
$F(t,x,v)=-kx$ with constant $k>0$; this is the global potential model with
$U(x)=\tfrac12kx^{2}$ and no residual forces
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]), with mechanical Lagrangian
$$L(t,x,v)=\tfrac12 m v^{2}-\tfrac12 k x^{2}$$
([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]). Write
$\omega:=\sqrt{k/m}>0$. Let $T>0$ and consider curves on $[0,T]$.

## Example
Declare a one-label model of the force branch of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$ and force map
$$F(t,x,y,z):=(0,-mg,0)$$
with constant $g>0$: the global potential model of potential
$U(x,y,z)=mgy$ and no residual forces
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]). Fix $l>0$ and constrain the
label to the circle of radius $l$ in the plane $z=0$ by the regular
holonomic-constraint datum of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]
$$O:=\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3},\qquad k:=2,\qquad G(x,y,z):=\bigl(x^{2}+y^{2}-l^{2},\ z\bigr),$$
and adopt the ideal-reaction law of [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]]. The
locus is the time-independent set
$N=\{(x,y,z):x^{2}+y^{2}=l^{2},\ z=0\}$, so the datum is scleronomic, and the
map
$$X:\mathbb R\longrightarrow Q,\qquad X(\theta):=\bigl(l\sin\theta,\ -l\cos\theta,\ 0\bigr)$$
is a global parametrization of the constrained configurations in the sense of
[[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]].
Then, with $r=3N-k=1$, kinetic metric $G(\theta)=ml^{2}$, substituted
potential $\widetilde U(\theta)=-mgl\cos\theta$ and substituted Lagrangian
$$\widetilde L(\theta,\dot\theta)=\tfrac12ml^{2}\dot\theta^{2}+mgl\cos\theta,$$
the following hold:
[F1] The reduction theorem ([[pthm-cm07-lagrangian-reduction-of-ideal-constraints]]): for a scleronomic regular holonomic datum with a global parametrization $X(\Omega)=N\cap Q$ and a potential presentation $(U,d)$, a $C^{2}$ coordinate curve solves the Lagrange equations of $\widetilde L$ with residual generalized force if and only if $x=X\circ\theta$ is a constrained motion with ideal reaction; the virtual displacements at $x$ equal $DX(\theta)(\mathbb R)$, and the reaction is unique with a unique multiplier.
[F2] The datum and its locus ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]], [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]], [[cor-matrix-rank-nullity]], [[def-euclidean-submersions-and-immersions]], [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]): $G$ is smooth with $D_{x}G(x,y,z)=\begin{pmatrix}2x&2y&0\\ 0&0&1\end{pmatrix}$ of rank $2$ on $N$ (the last row is independent; on $x^{2}+y^{2}=l^{2}$ not both $2x,2y$ vanish), the locus is nonempty and time-independent, and the virtual displacements at $X(\theta)$ are $\ker D_{x}G=\mathbb R(\cos\theta,\sin\theta,0)$.
1.1 Hypotheses of the reduction theorem: $G$ is smooth and $D_{x}G$ has rank $2$ on $N$ by [F2], so $(O,2,G)$ is a regular holonomic-constraint datum with nonempty locus, and it is scleronomic with $N=\{x^{2}+y^{2}=l^{2},z=0\}$. The map $X$ is smooth with $DX(\theta)=(l\cos\theta,l\sin\theta,0)$ injective for all $\theta$ (its norm is $l>0$), and $X(\mathbb R)=\{(l\sin\theta,-l\cos\theta,0):\theta\in\mathbb R\}=N\cap Q$; moreover $(t,X(\theta))\in O$ for all $t,\theta$. Hence $X$ is a global parametrization with $r=3N-k=1$, and the presentation $(U,d)=(mgy,0)$ has no residual forces. [F2, F3, given, algebra]

## Example
Declare a one-label model of the force branch of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$ and a global
potential presentation $F(t,x,v)=-\nabla U(t,x)$ with $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ and no residual forces
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]); the mechanical Lagrangian is
$L(t,x,v)=\tfrac12m\lvert v\rvert_{2}^{2}-U(t,x)$
([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]). Let
$\Omega:=\{(r,\theta,z)\in\mathbb R^{3}:r>0\}$ and define the cylindrical
coordinate map
$$X(r,\theta,z):=\bigl(r\cos\theta,\ r\sin\theta,\ z\bigr),\qquad X:\Omega\longrightarrow Q ,$$
with substituted Lagrangian and kinetic metric
$$\widetilde L(t,r,\theta,z,\dot r,\dot\theta,\dot z)=\tfrac12m\bigl(\dot r^{2}+r^{2}\dot\theta^{2}+\dot z^{2}\bigr)-\widetilde U(t,r,\theta,z),\qquad \widetilde U:=U\circ X,$$
$$G(r,\theta,z)=DX(r,\theta,z)^{\mathsf T}\,(mI_{3})\,DX(r,\theta,z)=m\begin{pmatrix}1&0&0\\ 0&r^{2}&0\\ 0&0&1\end{pmatrix}.$$

Work in the one-label force model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with
$N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window $I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain
$Q=\mathbb R^{3}$, and let $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R^{3}$ be of class $C^{2}$,
of dimension $\mu\ell\tau^{-2}$ (newtons), be a prescribed external drive.
Adopt the **time-dependent potential**
[F1] Model and presentation ([[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]]): $U$ is $C^{2}$ with $\nabla_{x}U=kx-f(t)$ and $\partial_{t}U=-f'(t)\cdot x$; $F=-\nabla_{x}U$ has the standing regularity; the model is a global potential model with mechanical energy $E=\tfrac{m}{2}\lvert v\rvert^{2}+U(t,x)$; motions satisfy $mx''=F(t,x,x')=-kx+f(t)$ ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]).
1.1 Clause 1: differentiating $U(t,x)=\tfrac{k}{2}\lvert x\rvert^{2}-f(t)\cdot x$ in the configuration slot gives $\nabla_{x}U(t,x)=kx-f(t)$, hence $F=-\nabla_{x}U=-kx+f(t)$, which is continuous and locally Lipschitz in $(x,v)$ locally uniformly in $t$ because $f$ is continuous and the map is affine in the state; differentiating in $t$ gives $\partial_{t}U(t,x)=-f'(t)\cdot x$, which vanishes identically exactly when $f'\equiv0$; by [F2] the time-translation datum satisfies the model identity exactly then. [F1, F2, F3, algebra]

Work in the one-label uniform-force model of [[ex-cm02-uniform-field]]: the
force branch of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$,
time window $I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$ and
force map $F(t,x,v)=m\,\mathbf g$ for a declared constant parameter
$\mathbf g\in\mathbb R^{3}$ of dimension $\ell\tau^{-2}$
([[def-cm01-units]]). Adopt the **potential presentation**
[F5] Calculus ([[thm-algebra-of-derivatives]], [[lem-derivative-of-a-power]], [[def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral]], [[def-euclidean-inner-product]], [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]], [[def-directional-and-partial-derivatives]], [[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]], [[def-interval]], [[def-cm01-units]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): polynomials in $t$ differentiate by the power rule; the Euclidean inner product is bilinear; the dimensions of $P=mx'$ are $\mu\ell\tau^{-1}$ and those of $K_{a}=a\cdot(tP-mx)$ are $\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-1}$.
1.1 Clause 1: differentiating $U(t,x)=-m\mathbf g\cdot x$ in the configuration slot gives $\nabla_{x}U(t,x)=-m\mathbf g$, hence $a\cdot\nabla_{x}U(t,x)=-m(a\cdot\mathbf g)$ for every $(t,x)$, which vanishes identically exactly when $a\cdot\mathbf g=0$; by [F3] this is precisely the differential translation condition. [F2, F3, F5, algebra]

      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.2 and Section 4.1, printed pages 13-16 and 80-87 (PDF pages 19-22 and 86-93): changing coordinate systems for Lagrange's equations and the Hamiltonian formulation of particle mechanics"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Sections 2.1-2.2 and 10.1, chapter pages 1-4 and 1-3: generalized coordinates for a particle and the Hamiltonian of a one-degree-of-freedom system"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"

  precheck: pass

## Example
Declare the one-label model of the force branch of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$ and potential
$U(t,x):=\tfrac12k\bigl(x_{1}-v_{0}t-c\bigr)^{2}$ with constants $k>0$,
$v_{0}\in\mathbb R$ and $c\in\mathbb R$, and no residual forces: the label is
attached by a spring of stiffness $k$ to a support whose position is the
prescribed function $v_{0}t+c$, so the force map is
$F=\bigl(-k(x_{1}-v_{0}t-c),0,0\bigr)$ and the mechanical Lagrangian is
$L=K-U$ with $K(v)=\tfrac12m\lvert v\rvert_{2}^{2}$
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]). Introduce the **co-moving
coordinates**
$X(t,y):=\bigl(y_{1}+v_{0}t+c,\ y_{2},\ y_{3}\bigr)$, a time-dependent
coordinate map with $X_{q}=I_{3}$, and the substituted Lagrangian
$L'(t,y,w):=L\bigl(t,X(t,y),\,X_{t}(t,y)+X_{q}(t,y)w\bigr)$. Then:
 
1. $L'(t,y,w)=\tfrac12m\bigl(w_{1}+v_{0}\bigr)^{2}+\tfrac12m\bigl(w_{2}^{2}+w_{3}^{2}\bigr)-\tfrac12ky_{1}^{2}$,
   its fibre derivative is
   $$p'=L'_{w}(t,y,w)=\bigl(m(w_{1}+v_{0}),\ mw_{2},\ mw_{3}\bigr),$$
   and the coordinate change reproduces the model's equation: a $C^{2}$ curve
   $y$ satisfies the Euler-Lagrange equation of $L'$ exactly when
   $x(t)=X(t,y(t))$ satisfies $m\ddot x_{1}=-k(x_{1}-v_{0}t-c)$, $m\ddot x_{2}=m\ddot x_{3}=0$
   ([[pthm-cm07-coordinate-covariance-of-the-equations]]).
2. $L'$ is hyperregular on its chart with inverse branch
   $w=\bigl(p'_{1}/m-v_{0},\ p'_{2}/m,\ p'_{3}/m\bigr)$ and Hamiltonian
   $$H'(t,y,p')=\frac{p'_{1}{}^{2}}{2m}-v_{0}p'_{1}+\frac{p'_{2}{}^{2}}{2m}+\frac{p'_{3}{}^{2}}{2m}+\frac{k}{2}y_{1}^{2} =\frac{\bigl(p'_{1}-mv_{0}\bigr)^{2}}{2m}+\frac{k}{2}y_{1}^{2}+\frac{p'_{2}{}^{2}+p'_{3}{}^{2}}{2m}-\frac{mv_{0}^{2}}{2},$$
   so that on the first coordinate the momentum $p'_{1}$ equals the inertial
   momentum $p_{1}=m\dot x_{1}$ of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]] while the
   branch velocity is shifted,
   $\dot y_{1}=p'_{1}/m-v_{0}$.
3. Hamilton's equations of $H'$ are
   $\dot y_{1}=p'_{1}/m-v_{0}$, $\dot p'_{1}=-ky_{1}$, $\dot y_{i}=p'_{i}/m$,
   $\dot p'_{i}=0$ for $i=2,3$; they imply $m\ddot y_{1}=-ky_{1}$, equivalently
   $m\ddot x_{1}=-k(x_{1}-v_{0}t-c)$, so the co-moving description is
   equivalent to the model's motion and the induced curve $x$ is a motion
   ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]).
4. along the solutions the Hamiltonian $H'$ is conserved — it has no explicit
   time dependence in the co-moving chart — while the model's mechanical
   energy $E=K+U$ is **not**: on the first axis
   $$H'=E-v_{0}p'_{1},\qquad E'(t)=-kv_{0}y_{1}(t),$$
   so a solution with $v_{0}y_{1}(t)\neq0$ has $H'$ constant and $E$ strictly
   monotone there; with data $y_{1}(0)=y_{0}$, $p'_{1}(0)=q$ and
   $\omega:=\sqrt{k/m}$ the explicit solution is
   $$y_{1}(t)=y_{0}\cos\omega t+\frac{q-mv_{0}}{m\omega}\sin\omega t,\qquad p'_{1}(t)=-m\omega y_{0}\sin\omega t+\bigl(q-mv_{0}\bigr)\cos\omega t+mv_{0}.$$
 
All quantities are model values of the declared idealization; the moving
support is a prescribed external mechanism, and no measurement is reported.
**Given:** The one-label model with the moving-support spring potential, the co-moving coordinate map $X$, the substituted Lagrangian $L'$ with its momentum $p'$ and Hamiltonian $H'$, and the initial data $y_{0},q$ of clause 4.
 
[F1] Model and Lagrangian data ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]): the model has $L=K-U$ with $K(v)=\tfrac12m\lvert v\rvert_{2}^{2}$, $U$ of class $C^{2}$, no residual forces, and motions solving $m\ddot x_{i}=F_{i}(t,x,\dot x)$; dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]].
 
[F2] Rheonomic coordinate covariance ([[pthm-cm07-coordinate-covariance-of-the-equations]], [[pthm-cm07-euler-lagrange-equations]]): for a $C^{3}$ coordinate map $X(t,q)$ with invertible $X_{q}$ and substituted Lagrangian $\widetilde L(t,q,v)=L(t,X,X_{t}+X_{q}v)$, one has $E_{\widetilde L}(q)=X_{q}^{\mathsf T}E_{L}(x)$ along induced curves, so the substituted Euler-Lagrange equation is equivalent to the model's potential-part equation.
 
[F3] Hyperregularity and branch Hamiltonian ([[prop-cm-mechanical-chart-lagrangian-is-hyperregular]], [[def-cm12-hyperregularity]], [[def-cm12-hamiltonian]], [[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]], [[thm-ck-euclidean-maps-closed-under-algebra-and-composition]], [[def-invertible-euclidean-linear-map]], [[def-linear-map]], [[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]]): a fibre-full $C^{2}$ chart of the form $\tfrac12v^{\mathsf T}Gv+A\cdot v-V$ with $G$ symmetric positive definite has fibre derivative $Gv+A$, is hyperregular with inverse $v=G^{-1}(p-A)$, and has branch Hamiltonian $\tfrac12(p-A)\cdot G^{-1}(p-A)+V$.
 
[F4] Energy data ([[pthm-cm12-energy-time]], [[pthm-cm03-energy-balance]], [[def-cm03-centre-of-mass-and-momenta]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]): in the inertial Cartesian chart the model's mechanical energy is $K+U$ along a curve; along a motion it satisfies $E'(t)=\partial_{t}U(t,x(t))+\sum_{i}\langle\mathfrak d_{i},x_{i}'\rangle$, here $\partial_{t}U=-kv_{0}(x_{1}-v_{0}t-c)$.
 
[F5] Calculus of the displayed data ([[thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives]], [[thm-algebra-of-derivatives]], [[def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral]], [[def-derivative]], [[def-interval]], [[def-euclidean-inner-product]], [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]], [[def-directional-and-partial-derivatives]], [[thm-sine-and-cosine-derivatives]], [[def-sine-and-cosine-by-power-series]]): the standard derivative rules for sums, products, composites and the trigonometric functions apply to the displayed curves.
- **Chart change, not a constraint.** The co-moving coordinates are a
  time-dependent coordinate map of the same configuration domain, covered by
  the covariance theorem for rheonomic coordinate changes; they are not a
  generalized-coordinate map [[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]
  of the constrained-configuration type, and no ideal reaction is involved.
  The support's motion is prescribed external data of the potential, so its
  energy exchange is not modeled here.
 
- **Two conserved quantities, one energy.** The co-moving Hamiltonian $H'$ is
  conserved because the substituted Lagrangian has no explicit time
  dependence in the moving chart, while the mechanical energy changes at the
  rate of the explicit time dependence of the inertial potential. The two
  quantities coincide only in the inertial chart, as [[pthm-cm12-energy-time]]
  states; the identity $H'=E-v_{0}p'_{1}$ here is the model instance of the
  momentum shift familiar from the moving-cart computation of Stewart's
  Section 1.2.

## Example
Declare the one-label model of the force branch of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$ and potential
$U(x,y,z)=mgy$ with constant $g>0$ and no residual forces, so that the force
map is the uniform field $F=(0,-mg,0)$
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]). Fix $l>0$ and constrain the
label to the circle of radius $l$ in the plane $z=0$ by the regular
holonomic-constraint datum of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]
is a parametrization of the constrained configurations in the sense of
[[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]] clause (d). Then, with the
substituted data of that definition:
[F1] Model, constraint and parametrization data ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]], [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]], [[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]): the model has masses $m_{i}>0$ and $L=K-U$ with $K(v)=\tfrac12v\cdot\mathsf Mv$; the constraint datum is regular and scleronomic; the parametrization $X$ is of class $C^{3}$ with injective derivative and $X(\Omega)=N\cap Q$, and the substituted Lagrangian is $\widetilde L(t,q,v)=L(t,X(q),DX(q)v)$ with kinetic metric $G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$.
[F2] Momentum, hyperregularity, Hamiltonian and energy ([[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]], [[def-cm12-hyperregularity]], [[def-cm12-hamiltonian]], [[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]], [[pthm-cm12-energy-time]]): the fibre derivative is $p=\widetilde L_{v}=G(q)v$, the chart is hyperregular with inverse branch $v=G(q)^{-1}p$, the Hamiltonian is $\widetilde H(t,q,p)=\tfrac12p\cdot G(q)^{-1}p+\widetilde U(t,q)$, and along a coordinate curve it equals the mechanical energy of the induced configuration curve.
[F5] Matrix and inner-product algebra of the kinetic metric ([[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]], [[def-directional-and-partial-derivatives]], [[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]], [[def-transpose-of-a-matrix]], [[def-linear-map]], [[def-euclidean-inner-product]], [[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]], [[def-euclidean-submersions-and-immersions]], [[def-cm01-units]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): the derivative $DX$ and the matrix product $DX^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX$ are computed componentwise, and inner products of vectors in $\mathbb R^{3}$ are the standard dot products.

## Example

[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$, a $C^{2}$ potential

[[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]). Fix a real parameter $\alpha$
and the $C^{2}$ function $F(t,x):=\alpha\,t\,x_{1}$, and define
[F1] Model and Lagrangian data ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $L=K-U$ with $K(v)=\tfrac12v\cdot\mathsf Mv$, $\mathsf Mv=mv$ for one label, $U$ of class $C^{2}$ and the standing regularity of the force branch; dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]].

## Example
Declare the one-label model of the force branch
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$, zero potential $U\equiv0$
and no residual forces (the **free particle instance**,
[[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]). By
[[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]] the branch Hamiltonian is
$H(x,p)=\lvert p\rvert^{2}/(2m)$ with Hamiltonian system
$\dot x=m^{-1}p$, $\dot p=0$
([[def-cm12-hamiltonian]], [[pthm-cm13-hamiltonian-field-integrates-the-model-equations]]).
Then:
[F1] Model and Hamiltonian ([[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]], [[def-cm12-hamiltonian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]], [[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]): $H=\lvert p\rvert^{2}/(2m)$ on $\mathbb R^{6}$ with Hamilton system $\dot x=m^{-1}p$, $\dot p=0$, whose solutions are $x(t)=x_{0}+(t/m)p_{0}$, $p(t)=p_{0}$.

## Example
Declare the one-label model of the force branch
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$, mass $m>0$, time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$, a $C^{2}$ potential
$U:\mathbb R\times Q\to\mathbb R$ and no residual forces
([[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]). By
[[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]] the presentation is hyperregular on
the Cartesian chart with $H(t,x,p)=\tfrac12m^{-1}\lvert p\rvert^{2}+U(t,x)$
([[def-cm12-hamiltonian]]), the two-form is
$\omega=\sum_{a=1}^{3}dx^{a}\wedge dp_{a}$
([[def-cm13-two-form]], [[pthm-cm13-two-form-is-symplectic]]) and the bracket
is [[def-cm13-poisson]]. Then:
[F1] Model and Hamiltonian ([[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]], [[def-cm12-hamiltonian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]]): $H(t,x,p)=\tfrac12m^{-1}\lvert p\rvert^{2}+U(t,x)$ with $H_{p}=m^{-1}p$ and $H_{x}=\nabla_{x}U$, and dimensions are read by the cited units item.

$A_{\mathrm{out}}=5\times10^{-5}\ \mathrm{m^2}$, outlet velocity along

FILE ex-cm15-impact-check
**Assertions to check.** For $e=1$ (elastic): $v_1^+=-3n$,
$v_2^+=+1n$, $\Delta K=0$. For $e=\frac12$: $v_1^+=-\frac32n$,
$v_2^+=+\frac12n$, impulse $J=-9n\ \mathrm{N\,s}$,
$\Delta K=-9\ \mathrm{J}$. For $e=0$ (sticking): $v_1^+=v_2^+=0$,
$\Delta K=-12\ \mathrm{J}$. In every case the total momentum $P=0$ is
continuous across the impact, as [[pthm-cm15-impact-balance]] clause 1
requires, and the energy changes are those of its clause 4.
2.1 Elastic case $e=1$: [L1] gives $v_1^+=V+\frac{m_2}{M}d^-=\frac68(-4)n=-3n$ and $v_2^+=V-\frac{m_1}{M}d^-=-\frac28(-4)n=+1n$, so the velocities are exchanged and the total momentum is $2(-3)+6(1)=0=P^-$; $\Delta K=-\frac{1-1}{2}\mu\lVert d^-\rVert_2^2=0$, and directly $K^+=\frac12\cdot2\cdot9+\frac12\cdot6\cdot1=12\ \mathrm{J}=K^-$. [step 1.1, L1, L2, algebra]
