FILE ex-cm16-pendulum-equilibrium-checks
---
id: ex-cm16-pendulum-equilibrium-checks
kind: example
title: "The ideal pendulum on declared data: strict minimum and Lyapunov stability at the downward equilibrium, negative Hessian and instability at the upward equilibrium, the exact linearisation frequencies, and failure of attraction"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 13
deps: [def-cm16-flow-equilibria-and-invariant-sets,
       def-cm16-stability-and-hyperbolicity-concepts,
       pthm-cm16-energy-stability-and-instability,
       def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart,
       thm-sine-and-cosine-derivatives,
       thm-derivative-of-exponential,
       def-cm01-units]
dependency_roles:
  def-cm16-flow-equilibria-and-invariant-sets: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm16-stability-and-hyperbolicity-concepts: formulation-prerequisite
  pthm-cm16-energy-stability-and-instability: physical-result
  def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart: mathematical-premise
  thm-sine-and-cosine-derivatives: mathematical-premise
  thm-derivative-of-exponential: mathematical-premise
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "A declared-data check of the page's energy-stability theorem on the ideal planar pendulum: with mass 1 kg, length 1 m and g=9.81 m s^-2 in the angle chart, the downward equilibrium is a strict minimum, hence Lyapunov stable but not attracting, with purely imaginary linearisation frequencies; the upward equilibrium has a strictly negative Hessian, hence is unstable, with the real linearisation rates. All data are declared model data; no measurement and no empirical comparison is made."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "Konstantin Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), §3.2 and §3.3: pendulum equilibrium types and the phase-plane discussion, with the linearised-stability caveats"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "David Tong, Classical Dynamics (University of Cambridge), §2.1 pp. 10-13: the pendulum Lagrangian and its equilibria"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "Iain Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309, Fall 2014), §7.3 pp. 164-172: the corrected stability definitions and the small-oscillation frequency about a stable equilibrium"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
---
**Declared model data.** Take one label on the real line as the angle chart of
the ideal planar pendulum: the chart domain is $\Omega:=\mathbb R$, the
configuration coordinate is the angle $q$ (radians, identified modulo $2\pi$
at the end), the kinetic metric is the constant matrix
$M:=m\ell^2$ with $m:=1\ \mathrm{kg}$, $\ell:=1\ \mathrm m$, and the potential
is
$$U(q):=mg\ell\,(1-\cos q),\qquad g:=9.81\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}},\qquad mg\ell=9.81\ \mathrm J .$$
This is the mechanical chart datum of
[[def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart]] with
$\Omega=\mathbb R$, $M$ constant and $U$ of class $C^\infty$. Its equations
are $m\ell^2\ddot q=-mg\ell\sin q$, equivalently
$\ddot q=-(g/\ell)\sin q$.
3.1 Failure of attraction: for every $0<\varepsilon<\pi$ the datum $z_0:=(\varepsilon,0)$ has energy $E(z_0)=mg\ell(1-\cos\varepsilon)>0=E(0,0)$, so letting $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ supplies states of different energy accumulating at the equilibrium; by [L1] fourth clause (and the strict-minimum clause of step 2.1) $(0,0)$ is Lyapunov stable but not asymptotically stable. In particular no datum of positive energy can converge to $(0,0)$, because the energy is conserved. [step 2.1, given, L1, algebra]
- **Periodic identification.** The chart coordinate $q$ is the lift of the
  circle angle; the potential is $2\pi$-periodic and the equation is invariant
  under $q\mapsto q+2\pi$, so the equilibria at $q=2\pi k$ (stable) and
  $q=\pi+2\pi k$ (unstable) are the same physical configurations as those
  checked. The two equilibria are the only mechanical equilibria, by step 1.1
  and the zero set of the sine.
- **What the check does not show.** The check verifies the hypotheses of
  [[pthm-cm16-energy-stability-and-instability]] on declared data; it does not
  produce a basin of attraction, a decay rate (none exists at the stable
  equilibrium) or any statement about the pendulum as an apparatus.

**Assertions to check.** (i) $R=13$ and the coding radicands
$a+x_{n-1}+x_{n+1}$ lie in $[74,126]=[a-2R,a+2R]$, with
$\sqrt{126}<13=R$; (ii) the contraction factor of the coding map is
$1/L=1/\sqrt{74}$ and $r_\pm=1\pm\sqrt{101}$; (iii) for the limiting pulse,
$K\circ R_0=F$ with $R_0(q,p)=(p,-q)$, $K(q,p)=(q,p+q^2-a)$ and
$F(x,y)=(y,y^2-a-x)$; (iv) every coded state satisfies
$\lvert Q\rvert\le13\ \mathrm m$ and $\lvert\Pi\rvert\le26\pi\
\mathrm{kg\,m\,s^{-1}}$, and the forcing period is
$T_p=(\pi/2+\delta)/(2\pi)\ \mathrm s$.
[L2] The coding contraction ([[lem-coded-quadratic-recurrence-is-a-contraction]]) and the limiting pulse ([[lem-rescaled-pulse-map-is-c-one-close-to-kick]]): the coding map $T_\sigma$ on the ball of bounded sequences with $\lvert x\rvert_\infty\le R$ is a contraction with factor $L^{-1}$ when the radicands stay positive and the derivative bound holds, and $K_\delta\to K$ in $C^1$ as $\delta\to0$ with $K(q,p)=(q,p+q^2-a)$.
2.1 Contraction and fixed points: the radicand bound of step 1.1 gives the derivative bound $\tfrac1{2\sqrt{74}}$ for the square root, hence the contraction factor $\bigl(\tfrac1{2\sqrt{74}}\bigr)\cdot2=\tfrac1{\sqrt{74}}=L^{-1}$ for the coding map, as in [L2] with $L=\sqrt{74}$. Constant codes satisfy $x_{n+1}+x_{n-1}=x_n^2-a$ with $x_n\equiv r$, that is $2r=r^2-a$ or $r^2-2r-a=0$, whose solutions are $r=1\pm\sqrt{1+a}=1\pm\sqrt{101}$. The branch conditions hold with exact inequalities: $\sqrt{101}>8$ because $101>64$, so $\lvert r_-\rvert=\sqrt{101}-1>7$; $\sqrt{101}>1+\sqrt{74}$ because $101>75+2\sqrt{74}$ reduces to $13>\sqrt{74}$, that is $169>74$; so $\lvert r_\pm\rvert>L$; and $r_+\le13$, $\lvert r_-\rvert\le13$ because $\sqrt{101}\le12$ and $\sqrt{101}-1\le13$, both from $101\le144$. [step 1.1, given, L2, L3, algebra]

1. the linear strain is $e_{11}(t,x)=-A\kappa\sin(\kappa x_1-\omega t)$ with

FILE lem-cm-mass-metric-positivity
---
id: lem-cm-mass-metric-positivity
kind: lemma
title: "The mass metric is symmetric, positive semidefinite, and positive definite exactly when the configuration differential is injective"
status: draft
origin: pipeline
domain: mathematics
dependency_level: 1
deps: [def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric, def-inner-product-space, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-definiteness-inertia-and-signature-data-over-the-reals, def-immersion-submersion-and-constant-rank-map]
dependency_roles:
  def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric: mathematical-premise
  def-inner-product-space: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-inner-product: mathematical-premise
  def-definiteness-inertia-and-signature-data-over-the-reals: mathematical-premise
  def-immersion-submersion-and-constant-rank-map: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, 2015), Section 2.1 and Section 3.1"
      url: "https://www.damtp.cam.ac.uk/user/tong/dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Section 3.9 (inertia of rigid motions)"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
Let $N\ge1$, let $Q$ be a smooth manifold, let $X:Q\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ be smooth, let $m_1,\dots,m_N>0$ and let $G_q$ be the mass metric of [[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]].
1. For every $q\in Q$ the map $G_q$ is a symmetric bilinear form on $T_qQ$.
2. $G_q(u,u)=\sum_{i=1}^N m_i\|dX_q(u)_i\|_2^2\ge 0$ for every $u\in T_qQ$, so $G_q$ is positive semidefinite.
3. $G_q$ is positive definite ([[def-definiteness-inertia-and-signature-data-over-the-reals]]) if and only if $dX_q$ is injective. In particular, if $X$ is an immersion ([[def-immersion-submersion-and-constant-rank-map]]) then $G_q$ is positive definite for every $q$.
4. If $b_1,\dots,b_n$ is an ordered basis of $T_qQ$ and $A$ is the matrix whose $j$-th column is the coordinate vector of $dX_q(b_j)$ in the standard basis of $\mathbb R^{3N}$, then the matrix of the bilinear form $G_q$ in this basis is $A^{\mathsf T}MA$, with $M$ the mass matrix.
**Given:** The data $(Q,X,m_1,\dots,m_N)$ of [[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]], a point $q\in Q$, and an ordered basis $b_1,\dots,b_n$ of $T_qQ$ for clause 4.
[L1] The mass metric is $G_q(u,v)=\langle dX_q(u),MdX_q(v)\rangle_{3N}$, the differential is linear, and $M(x_1,\dots,x_N)=(m_1x_1,\dots,m_Nx_N)$ with $m_i>0$ ([[def-cm-constrained-configuration-and-mass-metric]]).
1.1 Bilinearity of $G_q$ follows from linearity of $dX_q$ and of $M$ together with bilinearity of the inner product [L1, L2]: for reals $\alpha,\beta$ and $u,u',v\in T_qQ$, $G_q(\alpha u+\beta u',v)=\alpha G_q(u,v)+\beta G_q(u',v)$ and similarly in the second argument. Symmetry follows from symmetry of $M$: since $M$ acts by $x\mapsto(m_1x_1,\dots,m_Nx_N)$, $\langle a,Mb\rangle_{3N}=\sum_im_i\langle a_i,b_i\rangle_2=\langle Ma,b\rangle_{3N}$, so $G_q(u,v)=G_q(v,u)$. This proves clause 1. [L1, L2, algebra]
1.2 For $u\in T_qQ$ write $dX_q(u)=(w_1,\dots,w_N)$ with $w_i\in\mathbb R^3$; then [L1] and [L2] give $G_q(u,u)=\langle dX_q(u),MdX_q(u)\rangle_{3N}=\sum_{i=1}^Nm_i\langle w_i,w_i\rangle_2=\sum_{i=1}^Nm_i\|w_i\|_2^2\ge0$. This proves clause 2. [L1, L2, algebra]
3.1 In the basis $b_1,\dots,b_n$ the matrix of $G_q$ has entries $G_q(b_j,b_k)=\langle dX_q(b_j),MdX_q(b_k)\rangle_{3N}$. If $A$ is the matrix whose $j$-th column is the coordinate vector of $dX_q(b_j)$, then $M(dX_q(b_k))$ has coordinate vector $MAe_k$, and the inner product of coordinate vectors is the Euclidean product, so $G_q(b_j,b_k)=(A^{\mathsf T}MA)_{jk}$. This proves clause 4. [step 1.1, L1, L2, algebra] ∎

[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with one labeled particle of fixed mass
$m>0$ ([[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)) in a fixed inertial frame, with
time window $\mathbb R$ and configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3}$, and with

$v=\dot x$ ([[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]]). The particle moves through a
**medium** that fills the region of the motion and is at rest in the chosen
frame. The medium is not one of the model's particle labels, so its effect on
the label is an **external force** of the model in the sense of clause (i) of
[[post-cm02-interactions]]: the decomposition into the label's own dynamics
and the medium's effect is part of the model's declaration.

FILE post-cm02-force-superposition
---
id: post-cm02-force-superposition
kind: postulate
title: "Adopted force model: force maps, the equation of motion, additive composition of force contributions and frame covariance"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 8
deps: [post-cm01-spacetime, post-cm01-frames, post-cm01-worldlines, def-cm01-configuration, def-cm01-velocity-acceleration, def-cm01-units, def-locally-lipschitz-in-the-state-variable, pthm-cm01-galilean-kinematics]
dependency_roles:
  post-cm01-spacetime: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm01-frames: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm01-worldlines: formulation-prerequisite

  pthm-cm01-galilean-kinematics: formulation-prerequisite
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "Adoption of the framework's force branch: force maps as continuous, state-locally-Lipschitz vector-valued maps on a time window, an open configuration domain and velocity space, the equation of motion $m_i\\ddot x_i=F_i(t,x,\\dot x)$ in every inertial frame, pointwise additivity of declared force contributions, and the transformation rule that a force law must satisfy under a change of inertial frame. The postulate excludes impacts, dry and static friction, stochastic and distributional forcing, and constraint reactions; it asserts no measured value, no conservation law and no claim that any physical interaction is exactly of this form."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 1.2 'Newtonian Mechanics: A Single Particle', printed pages 2-5 (PDF pages 8-11): the equation of motion F = dp/dt = m a in an inertial frame, with F a function of the position and velocity (and possibly time)"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Section 1.2, chapter pages 3-4 (PDF pages 7-8): the force defined through the momentum derivative (Eq. 1.13), the explicit separate assumption that forces superpose as vectors, and the gravity law (1.15) introduced as an additional law"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Section 1.2, printed pages 1-2 (PDF pages 4-5): Newton's second law in its two equivalent forms, as N second-order equations F_i = d(m_i rdot_i)/dt with data r_i(0) and rdot_i(0), and as 2N first-order equations"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
    - title: "D. Tong, Dynamics and Relativity (Cambridge lecture notes, Lent 2013), Section 1.2.1 'Galilean Relativity', printed page 5 (PDF page 11): the boost x' = x + v t maps inertial frames to inertial frames and preserves d^2x/dt^2 = 0, so accelerations transform as spatial vectors and F = m rddot is the same law in both frames"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/dynamics-and-relativity/dynrel.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
**(i) Setting and configuration domain.** A model of the framework has
$N\ge1$ particle labels with fixed positive masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}$
([[post-cm01-worldlines]], clause (v)) and a configuration domain $Q$ in one
fixed inertial frame, in the unconstrained branch of
[[def-cm01-configuration]] clause (a): $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ is open,
possibly with the collision set $\Delta$ removed when the force law of the
model is singular at coincident positions. The constrained branch of that
definition, with its reaction forces, is **not** covered by this postulate; the
dynamics of constrained systems is a separate development with its own
assumptions. A **state** of the model is a pair $z=(x,v)\in Q\times\mathbb
R^{3N}$ of positions and coordinate velocities of the labels in the chosen
frame ([[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]]).
whose domain is the image of $D$ under the affine bijection
$(t',y,w)\mapsto\bigl(t'-b,\,R^{\mathsf T}(y-v(t'-b)-a_{0}),\,R^{\mathsf T}(w-v)\bigr)$;
that domain is open, and $F_{*}$ is continuous and locally Lipschitz in its
state variable because $F$ is and the transfer is an affine bijection with
bounded inverse on compacta. The framework formulates the equation of motion
in every inertial frame; a rule that does not transform by this law is a
description tied to the frame in which it was written, and the framework
attaches no kinematic covariance to it. When $Q=\mathbb R^{3N}$ — the case of
the models of the companion page — the transferred domain is again a product
$I_{0}'\times\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ with $I_{0}'=I_{0}+b$, and
$F_{*}$ is a force map in the sense of (iii); for a bounded $Q$ the
transferred admissible position set may depend on $t'$, and the equation is
then read on that transferred set.
**(vii) What this branch excludes.** Impulsive forcing (a finite momentum
transfer at an instant is not a force map); Coulomb sliding friction, whose
expression contains the factor $v/\lvert v\rvert$ and is undefined at $v=0$;
static friction, which requires an inequality or a set-valued reaction model;
stochastic forcing; and forces that exist only as distributions. Each of these
requires its own stated model and solution notion, and none is covered by
(iii)–(iv). A singular force law is admissible on a configuration domain with
the singular configurations removed — for instance an inverse-square law on
the collision-excluded $Q$ of clause (i) — and what the removal of those
configurations implies for existence in time is stated by the continuation
theorem of this page.
- **Open systems.** The configuration domain and the force map are declared for
  a stated set of labels: forces exerted by bodies outside that set are
  external data of the model, and the pair structure of interactions is a
  further restricted assumption
  ([[post-cm02-interactions]]). The scope warning for such model boundaries is
  [[rem-cm02-open-system-warning]].
- **No dynamics of constraints.** Reactions that maintain a constrained
  configuration are not force maps of clause (iii) in general; their ideal
  model and its hypotheses belong to the constraint development, not here.

---
Adopt, whenever a model of the force branch [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]
invokes them, the following **restricted interaction assumptions**. Throughout,
the data $I_{0}$, $Q$, $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}$ and the force map $F$ are those of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]], and a state writes $z=(x,v)$ with
$x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})\in Q$ and $v=(v_{1},\dots,v_{N})\in\mathbb R^{3N}$.
on the collision-excluded configuration domain ($x_{i}\ne x_{j}$ for all
$i\ne j$), where $G_{\mathrm N}>0$ is a constant of dimension
$\ell^{3}\mu^{-1}\tau^{-2}$ ([[def-cm01-units]]) and the numbers
$m_{1}^{g},\dots,m_{N}^{g}>0$ are **gravitational coupling parameters** of the
labels, primitive model data distinct from the inertial masses $m_{i}$ of the
equation of motion. By clause (iii) this potential supplies the attractive
inverse-square pair force
$F_{ij}=-G_{\mathrm N}m_{i}^{g}m_{j}^{g}\,(x_{i}-x_{j})/\lvert x_{i}-x_{j}\rvert^{3}$,
the content of the elementary differentiation
$\nabla_{x}\bigl(1/\lvert x-y\rvert\bigr)=-(x-y)/\lvert x-y\rvert^{3}$; a
concrete instance of that differentiation is verified on the companion page.
The adopted model satisfies the antisymmetry and centrality options of (ii)
where they are invoked.

FILE post-cm04-harmonic-model

id: post-cm04-harmonic-model
kind: postulate
title: "The harmonic (linear restoring) model: a declared linear force law with parameter $k>0$"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 13
deps: [def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria, post-cm02-force-superposition, def-cm01-units, def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy, def-locally-lipschitz-in-the-state-variable, lem-derivative-of-a-power, def-derivative]

  post-cm02-force-superposition: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite

  lem-derivative-of-a-power: mathematical-premise

physical_scope: "Adoption of the harmonic oscillator as a restricted one-dimensional model: configuration domain the whole line, fixed mass m>0, force law F(x)=-k x with declared spring constant k>0 of dimension mass per time squared, and potential U(x)=k x^2/2. The model is a linear idealization of a restoring force, adopted for a stated regime (small displacements from a stable equilibrium, in which the quadratic term of the potential is the adequate approximation, as in the sources' linearization procedure); the spring constant is model data whose value, if taken from measurement, is an empirical premise with its own conditions and uncertainty. The item asserts no value of k, no claim that the model applies to a given spring or oscillation, and no measured result."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Section 5.1, Eqs. (5.1)-(5.2), chapter pages 1-2 (PDF pages 87-88): the harmonic oscillator Lagrangian L = m qdot^2/2 - kappa q^2/2 and its equation m qddot + kappa q = 0 with omega_0 = (kappa/m)^{1/2}; Section 3.2, Eqs. (3.10)-(3.12), chapter pages 2-3 (PDF pages 34-35): the quadratic Taylor expansion of the effective potential about a fixed point and the linearised equation with effective spring constant d^2 U_ef/dq^2"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 1.2.4, Example 1 (the simple harmonic oscillator), printed page 5 (PDF page 11): the force F(x) = -k x arising from the potential V = k x^2/2, with energy conserved"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Chapter 3, Section 3.1, Eqs. (3.1)-(3.4), printed pages 52-53 (PDF pages 55-56): expansion of the potential about its minimum, the quadratic Lagrangian and the linearised equations of motion with the stiffness constants V_ij"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"

  precheck: n/a
---
**(i) Setting.** Work in the one-dimensional model class of
[[def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria]] with time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R$, a fixed mass $m>0$
and a **spring constant** $k>0$ of dimension $\mu\tau^{-2}$, read in newtons
per metre ([[def-cm01-units]]). The **harmonic model** is the model whose
force law is the **linear restoring force**
$$F(x,v):=-k\,x .$$
Its equation of motion is $m\,x''=-k\,x$, equivalently $m\,x''+k\,x=0$; the
model is autonomous and velocity-independent, and its potential is
$$U(x):=\tfrac12 k\,x^{2},$$
of dimension $\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-2}$ (joules), which presents the force law in
the sense of clause (d) of [[def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria]]:
$U$ is of class $C^{2}$ and $-U'(x)=-k\,x=F(x)$ ([[lem-derivative-of-a-power]],
[[def-derivative]]). The harmonic model is therefore a **conservative**
model of the class, with the potential representative $U$ declared above and
no residual force.

FILE post-cm04-linear-damping-model
**(i) Setting.** Work in the one-dimensional model class of
[[def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria]] with time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R$, a fixed mass $m>0$,
the spring constant $k>0$ of [[post-cm04-harmonic-model]], and a **drag
coefficient** $c\ge0$ of dimension $\mu\tau^{-1}$, read in kilograms per
second ([[def-cm01-units]]). The model's force law is declared through the
additive composition of force contributions, in the sense of clause (v) of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]], read in one coordinate:
$$F(x,v)=-k\,x-c\,v .$$

FILE post-cm04-sinusoidal-drive-model
**(i) Setting.** Work in the one-dimensional model class of
[[def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria]] with time window
$I_{0}=\mathbb R$, configuration domain $Q=\mathbb R$, mass $m>0$, spring
constant $k>0$ ([[post-cm04-harmonic-model]]) and drag coefficient $c\ge0$
([[post-cm04-linear-damping-model]]). Let a real **drive amplitude**
$f_{0}\in\mathbb R$ of dimension $\mu\ell\tau^{-2}$ (newtons) and an
**angular drive frequency** $\Omega>0$ of dimension $\tau^{-1}$ (radians per
second; the radian is dimensionless in the framework's angle convention,
[[def-cm01-units]]) be declared. Adopt, through the additive composition of
force contributions of clause (v) of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] and
the external-contribution reading of [[post-cm02-interactions]], the force
law
$$F(t,x,v):=f_{0}\cos(\Omega t)-k\,x-c\,v .$$
The **sinusoidally driven oscillator model** is the one-dimensional model
with this force law; its equation of motion is
$$m\,x''+c\,x'+k\,x=f_{0}\cos(\Omega t),$$
and its data $(m,c,k,f)$ with $f(t)=f_{0}\cos(\Omega t)$ are oscillator-type
data in the sense of clause (f) of
[[def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria]]. The term
$f_{0}\cos(\Omega t)$ is the **drive** or **driving force**; the model is
undamped when $c=0$ and strictly damped when $c>0$.

FILE post-cm05-central-model
---
id: post-cm05-central-model
kind: postulate
title: "Adopted central-force model class: static central pair potentials, the reduced one-label problem, the fixed-centre idealization, reading conventions and exclusions"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 14
deps: [post-cm02-force-superposition, post-cm02-interactions, post-cm01-worldlines, def-cm02-newtonian-flow, def-cm01-configuration, def-cm01-units, def-cm-central-force-trajectory, lem-cm05-radial-potential-gradient, pthm-cm05-relative-reduction]
dependency_roles:
  post-cm02-force-superposition: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm02-interactions: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm01-worldlines: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm02-newtonian-flow: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm01-configuration: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm-central-force-trajectory: mathematical-premise
  lem-cm05-radial-potential-gradient: mathematical-premise
  pthm-cm05-relative-reduction: formulation-prerequisite
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "Adoption of the page's central-force model class inside the framework's force and interaction branches: two-label models presented by a static $C^2$ central pair potential on a collision-excluded annulus and free of external forces; the reduced one-label problem of mass $\\mu$; the separate one-label fixed-centre idealization; the reading conventions for force centre, orbit and body labels; and the explicit exclusions (velocity- or time-dependent, non-central, non-potential, constrained, colliding, external-force and many-body models) together with the status of the model data. The item adopts a modelling class; it asserts no law of nature, no measured value and no conservation law."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "David Tong, Classical Dynamics (University of Cambridge), Section 2.5.4, printed pp. 31-33 (PDF pp. 37-39): two bodies interacting through a central force, the separation $r_{12}$ and the reduced mass $\\mu$, the planarity of the orbit under the conserved angular momentum, and the neglect of the centre-of-mass piece"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "Konstantin Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Section 3.4, Eqs. (3.30)-(3.38), chapter pages 7-9 (PDF pages 40-42): two particles interacting via a conservative central force, the transfer to the relative vector and centre of mass, and the statement that the relative dynamics is identical to that of a single effective particle of mass $\\mu$ in the central potential $U(r)$"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "Iain Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Section 4.5, printed page 79 (PDF page 82): the Kepler problem of two masses in a central potential, treated with the centre-of-mass coordinate at the origin and the reduced mass"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
---
**(i) The two-label central model.** A model of class (i) is a force model of
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=2$ labels, fixed masses
$m_1,m_2>0$ ([[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)), a time window $I_0$, the
collision-excluded configuration domain
$$Q:=\{\,x=(x_1,x_2)\in(\mathbb R^3)^2:\lvert x_1-x_2\rvert\in J_0\,\}$$
for an open interval $J_0\subseteq(0,\infty)$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), and the force map presented through the
time-independent **static central pair potential**
$$U(x_1,x_2):=u(\lvert x_1-x_2\rvert),\qquad u:J_0\to\mathbb R\ \text{of class }C^2,$$
so that $F_i(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x_i}U(x)$ on the state domain (clauses (iii)
and (iv) of [[post-cm02-interactions]]; $U$ is of class $C^2$ on the open
$Q$ by [[lem-cm05-radial-potential-gradient]]). In the external-plus-pair
presentation of clause (i) of that postulate the model has
$F_i^{\mathrm{ext}}\equiv0$: no external forces act on the labels of a model
of class (i).
**(iii) The one-label fixed-centre idealization.** A **one-label central
model** is a force model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=1$,
mass $\mu>0$, time window $I_0$, a fixed **centre** $c\in\mathbb R^3$, the
configuration domain $Q_c=\{x\in\mathbb R^3:\lvert x-c\rvert\in J_0\}$, and
the external force map
$$F(t,x,v)=-\frac{u'(\lvert x-c\rvert)}{\lvert x-c\rvert}\,(x-c),$$
presented through the potential $u(\lvert x-c\rvert)$ with the same function
$u\in C^2(J_0)$. This model is the **fixed-centre idealization** of the
reduced problem: it drops the centre-of-mass degree of freedom and holds the
force centre fixed. It is a separate adopted model class, not a consequence
of class (i), and every use of it must state that the idealization is
intended.
**(v) Exclusions and status.** The class excludes: velocity-dependent and
explicitly time-dependent central forces; non-central and non-potential
forces; constraints and reaction forces; colliding configurations
$x_1=x_2$ (equivalently $r=0$); external forces on the labels; and models
with more than two labels. Finite-size, rotational, deformational, dissipative
and relativistic effects are outside the class. The masses, the interval
$J_0$ and the function $u$ (including any parameters it carries) are model
data, not measured values ([[def-cm01-units]]); quantitative empirical use
requires separately stated measured inputs with their uncertainties, and no
statement of this page asserts that any physical system is exactly a model
of this class.

FILE post-cm05-gravity
**(i) The two-label gravitational model.** A model of the gravitational
branch is a model of class (i) of [[post-cm05-central-model]] with
$J_0=(0,\infty)$, $Q=\{x=(x_1,x_2):x_1\ne x_2\}$, masses $m_1,m_2>0$ in the
equation of motion, and the **gravitational pair potential**
$$U(x_1,x_2)=-\frac{G_{\mathrm N}\,m_1^{g}m_2^{g}}{\lvert x_1-x_2\rvert},$$
where $G_{\mathrm N}>0$ is the **Newtonian constant of gravitation**, a
constant of dimension $\ell^{3}\mu^{-1}\tau^{-2}$ read in
$\mathrm{m^3\,kg^{-1}\,s^{-2}}$, and $m_1^{g},m_2^{g}>0$ are the
**gravitational coupling parameters** of the labels, primitive model data
distinct from the inertial masses $m_1,m_2$ ([[post-cm02-interactions]]
clause (v), [[def-cm01-units]]). The corresponding central pair potential of
class (i) is $u(\rho)=-\kappa/\rho$ with
$$\kappa:=G_{\mathrm N}\,m_1^{g}m_2^{g}>0,\qquad\text{dimension } \mu\ell^{3}\tau^{-2};$$
it is of class $C^\infty$ on $(0,\infty)$, the model has no external forces,
and coincident configurations are excluded. The force is attractive, and its
inverse-square form is an **adopted law** of this branch, not a consequence
of the equation of motion.
**(iv) Scope and exclusions.** The model is non-relativistic and weak-field;
it treats the two labels as point particles with fixed masses, ignores
external bodies, rotation, deformation, mass loss, radiation and any
non-gravitational force, and excludes coincident configurations. It is not
asserted that any physical system is exactly of this form; in particular,
the identification of clause (ii) is not a framework-wide identification,
and the inverse-square law is not derived from the balance laws of the
framework.

FILE post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law
---
id: post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law
kind: postulate
title: "Adopted ideal-reaction law for holonomic constraints: the d'Alembert condition, its scope and its limits"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 10
deps: [def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model, post-cm02-force-superposition, def-cm01-units, def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions, lem-cm-constraint-multipliers-and-accelerations, def-euclidean-inner-product]

  def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions: formulation-prerequisite

The item records the modelled mechanisms and the exclusions (sliding friction along admissible directions, unilateral and contact-change mechanisms, rank loss, active drives, mechanical capability of the reaction) and separates the virtual-work condition from statements about actual work and energy. No measured value and no conservation claim is asserted."

  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.3.1, Eqs.       url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Sections 1.4-1.5, printed pages 19-25 (PDF pages 22-28): an ideal constraint reaction annihilates all admissible virtual displacements at fixed time (d'Alembert's virtual-work hypothesis); time-dependent constraints can do actual work despite zero virtual work; sliding and static friction are separate models"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Sections 2.1-2.2, chapter pages 1-4: the ideal constraint reaction as an adopted frictionless restriction, not an automatic consequence of imposing a constraint"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "G. J. Sussman and J. Wisdom, Structure and Interpretation of Classical Mechanics, second edition (MIT Press, 2014/2015), Section 1.10.3: d'Alembert's ideal constraint forces and the explicit distinction from stationary augmented-action (vakonomic) formulations"
      url: "https://mitp-content-server.mit.edu/books/content/sectbyfn/books_pres_0/9579/sicm_edition_2.zip"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
Adopt as a physical assumption of the framework, in the setting of
[[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]:
**(i) The ideal-reaction law.** Along every constrained motion $x:J\to
\mathbb R^{3N}$ of a holonomic-constraint model, with reaction field
$R:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$, the reaction annihilates every virtual displacement:
the pairing being the Euclidean inner product of $\mathbb R^{3N}$
([[def-euclidean-inner-product]]). This is the **d'Alembert condition** (the
condition of zero virtual work of the reaction) in the sense of
[[def-cm-holonomic-constraint-and-ideal-reactions]]. By the multiplier lemma
[[lem-cm-constraint-multipliers-and-accelerations]], applied at each time with
the surjective Jacobian $D_{x}G(t,x(t))$, the condition holds at time $t$ if
and only if there is a unique $\lambda(t)\in\mathbb R^{k}$ with
$$R(t)=D_{x}G\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)^{\mathsf T}\lambda(t);$$
this representation is a mathematical consequence of the condition and the
rank hypothesis, not a separate assumption, and the multiplier has dimension
$\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-2}/\gamma_{\alpha}$ in component $\alpha$
([[def-cm01-units]]). The condition is imposed only along the motions of the
model, and it does not by itself determine $R$: it constrains $R$ at each time
to the $k$-dimensional conormal space, and which element occurs is fixed by
the twice-differentiated constraint through the balance theorem of this page.
**(iii) Intended model identifications.** The framework intends clause (i) to
model, subject to the qualifications of clause (iv): frictionless hinges and
joints; smooth bilateral contacts and tracks; a taut light rod, and a taut
string while it stays taut; and no-slip rolling in the planar holonomic case,
provided the contact can supply the required tangential reaction. Each of
these identifications is an application-level statement made and recorded with
the apparatus; the framework asserts no measurement, no material parameter and
no numerical value here.
- **No conservation claimed.** The ideality says nothing about energy; energy
  statements require the potential presentation of the ambient force map and
  the rheonomic analysis of this page.

physical_scope: "Adoption of the heavy symmetric top branch of the rigid-material model: a reference mass distribution whose body inertia matrix is diag(A_c,A_c,C_c) about a declared symmetry axis, a material point held fixed at the origin of the inertial frame (the pivot, on the symmetry axis at distance d from the centre of mass, so that the centre of mass is constrained to the circle of radius d about the pivot), an ideal pivot mechanism contributing no torque about the pivot in the framework's sense of a frictionless hinge, a uniform gravitational field of magnitude g directed along $-e_3$, and the adopted balance of angular momentum about the pivot. The branch excludes friction and driving torques at the pivot, rolling and sliding contacts, non-axisymmetric bodies and non-uniform fields, and asserts no measured value and no claim that a concrete top or gyroscope realises it."
sources:
  references:

      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"

      url: "https://mitp-content-server.mit.edu/books/content/sectbyfn/books_pres_0/9579/sicm_edition_2.zip"
    - title: "I. W. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09 Advanced Classical Mechanics, Fall 2014 course text), Chapter 2 Section 2.5, printed pages 48-51 (PDF pages 51-54): the symmetric top about a fixed point and its reduction to a one-dimensional problem"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
**(i) Setting.** A model of the branch carries the data of
[[post-cm10-rigid-material]]: a reference mass distribution $\nu$ with total
mass $m>0$ and centred reference coordinates, a time window $I_0$, an
inertial frame with adapted coordinates $(t,x)$ ([[post-cm01-frames]]),
whose origin is chosen at the pivot point of clause (iii), and a motion
$x(t,\rho)=c(t)+R(t)\rho$ with configuration path $(c,R)$ of class $C^{2}$
([[def-cm10-rigid-body-configuration]]). Assume the Axiom of Countable
Choice $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ ([[def-countable-choice]]), used only through the
integrals of the model's inertial quantities. Fix once and for all an
orthonormal basis $(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3})$ of the reference coordinates.
**(iii) The pivot constraint.** The material point of reference coordinate
$\rho_{p}:=-d\,e_{3}$ is the **pivot material point**; it lies on the
symmetry axis at distance $d$ from the centre of mass. The motions of the
branch are those satisfying
$$c(t)+R(t)\rho_{p}=0\qquad(t\in I_{0}),$$
that is $c(t)=d\,R(t)e_{3}$: the pivot material point is held fixed at the
origin of the inertial frame for the whole time window, and the centre of
mass is constrained to the circle of radius $d$ about the origin. The
constraint is maintained by a mechanism (a bearing, a hinge or a fine
support) whose force system is **ideal** in the sense adopted for
frictionless hinges in [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]] clause (iii) together
with the virtual-work law of its clause (i): the mechanism exerts on the body
a force system equivalent to a single force applied at the pivot point, so
that its total torque about the origin **vanishes**. The holonomic-constraint
vocabulary of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]] — constrained curve
families, reaction fields and the declaration that the constraint is
maintained by an additional force contribution — is the framework's account
for finitely many particle labels; the present model is a continuum, and this
clause adopts the analogous idealisation for it. No friction, damping or
driving torque at the pivot is asserted; such torques are excluded and would
have to be declared as part of a torque datum with their own model.

**(i) Setting.** A model of the branch carries the data of
[[post-cm10-rigid-material]]: a reference mass distribution $\nu$ with total
mass $m>0$ and centred reference coordinates, a time window $I_0$, an
inertial frame with adapted coordinates $(t,x)$ ([[post-cm01-frames]]), and a
motion $x(t,\rho)=c(t)+R(t)\rho$ with configuration path $(c,R)$ of class
$C^{2}$ ([[def-cm10-rigid-body-configuration]]). Assume the Axiom of
Countable Choice $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$ ([[def-countable-choice]]), used only
through [[pthm-cm10-kinetic-splitting]] to make the defining integrals of the
model's inertial quantities finite. Along the motion the body angular
velocity $\omega$ and the body inertia matrix $I$ are those of
[[def-cm10-angular-velocity]] and [[def-cm10-inertia-tensor]], and the
**angular momentum about the centre of mass** is
$$L_c(t):=\int\bigl(x(t,\rho)-c(t)\bigr)\times\bigl(\partial_tx(t,\rho)-c'(t)\bigr)\,d\nu(\rho),$$
the finite vector of clause 1 of [[pthm-cm10-kinetic-splitting]], which by
clause 4 of that item satisfies the identity
$$L_c(t)=R(t)\,I\,\omega(t)\qquad(t\in I_0).$$
**(iii) The adopted balance.** Along every motion of a model of the branch
with external torque datum $\tau$, adopt the **external-torque balance**
$$L_c'(t)=\tau(t)\qquad(t\in I_0).$$
This is an adopted dynamical law of the model. Through the identity
$L_c=RI\omega$ of clause (i) it is one vector equation relating the
configuration path, its first two time derivatives and the datum; it does
not by itself determine $(c,R)$, and it is not a consequence of the kinematic
definitions of [[post-cm10-rigid-material]].

FILE post-cm17-contact-force-model
---
id: post-cm17-contact-force-model
kind: postulate
title: "Adopted force model of the continuum branch: continuous body forces and Cauchy contact tractions"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 11
deps: [def-cm17-traction-and-stress, def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units, post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model, def-cm01-units, def-cm-continuum-motion, def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]
dependency_roles:
  def-cm17-traction-and-stress: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm-continuum-motion: mathematical-premise
  def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "Adoption of the force description of the continuum branch: a continuous body-force field per unit mass on the swept region and a continuous traction field, force per unit area, depending on a contact surface only through its unit normal at the point (Cauchy's hypothesis). The postulate excludes couple stresses, nonlocal contact interactions, curvature-dependent contact forces, and electromagnetic, thermal or chemical forces; it asserts no balance law, no constitutive relation and no measured value."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Section 4.3 (body forces, contact forces and Cauchy's hypothesis), Section 4.4 (the traction on a surface)"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
    - title: "K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Classical Mechanics (September 1, 2026 complete volume), Section 7.2 (bulk and surface forces and the stress tensor)"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
**(ii) Contact forces and Cauchy's hypothesis.** There is a traction field
$t:U\times S^2\to\mathbb R^3$ of class $C^1$, hence continuous, in the sense of
[[def-cm17-traction-and-stress]], force per unit area, with dimension
$[t]=\mu\ell^{-1}\tau^{-2}$. The definition of a traction field already
encodes **Cauchy's hypothesis**: the contact force exerted across a surface at
a point depends on the surface only through the value of its unit normal at
that point, so two surfaces through the same point with the same unit normal
experience the same traction. The contact force exerted across the boundary of
an admissible region $D$ is $\int_{\partial D}t(y,\nu_D(y))\,dS(y)$, with the
outward unit normal $\nu_D$ and the surface integral of the current-region
class. No dependence of $t$ on the curvature of the surface, on higher
derivatives of the surface, or on the distant shape of the body is introduced;
no surface couples, no traction singularities and no contact forces on curves
are introduced.
**(iii) Exclusions.** This force description is **not** adopted, and no
statement of this page covers: couple stresses or distributions of surface
couples; contact interactions that depend on the geometry of the surface
beyond its tangent plane at the point (thin boundary layers, capillarity at
contact lines, adhesion laws with curvature dependence); nonlocal contact
interactions; electromagnetic, thermal, chemical and radiative forces; and
body couples. A model with such features requires an extended force
description and its own balance laws.
**(iv) Status.** These are adopted assumptions about how forces are
represented in the model. No balance of forces is asserted by this postulate;
the balance principles are the subject of the next postulate, and the
existence of a representing stress matrix is a conditional conclusion of the
theorem that applies Cauchy's tetrahedron argument to those balances. The
fields $b$ and $t$ are model quantities; no measured force is asserted, and
the frame-covariance requirement of the kinematic branch applies to them.

Adopt the following constitutive branches for models of the continuum
framework ([[post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model]],

on the current regions ([[def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus]]) together with
the **Newtonian stress field**
**(b) Barotropic inviscid fluid.** Model data are a $C^1$ **pressure law**
$p:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb R$ and a $C^1$ potential $V$ with the conservative
body force $b=-\nabla V$. The branch's stress field is

  def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "Adoption of the small-strain isotropic linear elastic constitutive branch: displacement field, linear strain, stress matrix lambda (tr e) I + 2 mu_s e with positive-definite moduli, and the elastic strain-energy density with its positivity. The postulate states the small-strain kinematic idealization, the constancy of the reference density and the validity limits (small strains relative to a stress-free reference state, isothermal, no plasticity, viscosity, damage or coupling); it asserts no measured modulus, no boundary condition and no dynamical claim."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Section 11.2 and Problem 11.5 (linearized elasticity, isotropic stress-strain relations and the positive definite strain energy)"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
    - title: "K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Classical Mechanics (September 1, 2026 complete volume), Section 7.3 (Hooke's law and the two elastic moduli of an isotropic medium)"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
**(i) Small-displacement description.** There is a reference placement in
which the body is stress free and homogeneous, and the motion of the model is
described by a **displacement field** $\xi:I\times O\to\mathbb R^3$ of class
$C^2$ through $x(t,X)=X+\xi(t,X)$ ([[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]).
The model retains only the first-order term in the displacement gradient in
its strain measure: the **linear strain** is
$e=\tfrac12(D\xi+D\xi^{\mathsf T})$, equivalently
$e_{ij}=\tfrac12(\partial_j\xi_i+\partial_i\xi_j)$
([[def-cm-linear-elastic-constitutive-law]]). The displacement gradients are
assumed small in the sense of the model: the linear constitutive relation
below is used as the first-order approximation of the material response about
the stress-free reference state, and no quantitative strain threshold is
asserted by this postulate.
**(ii) Constitutive law.** The stress matrix of the branch is
with **Lamé parameters** $\lambda,\mu_s\in\mathbb R$ satisfying the positivity
hypotheses $\mu_s>0$ and $\lambda+\tfrac{2\mu_s}{3}>0$
([[def-cm-linear-elastic-constitutive-law]]). Both parameters are constant in
space and time in this branch. Their dimension is that of stress,
$[\lambda]=[\mu_s]=\mu\ell^{-1}\tau^{-2}$ ([[def-cm01-units]]); in SI they are
measured in $\mathrm{N\,m^{-2}}$.

FILE post-cm17-momentum-balance

id: post-cm17-momentum-balance
kind: postulate
title: "Adopted balance principles of the continuum model: force and couple-free torque balance on every admissible region"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 12
deps: [post-cm17-contact-force-model, post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model, def-cm17-density-and-mass, def-cm17-traction-and-stress, def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units, def-bounded-piecewise-c-one-euclidean-domain, def-surface-integral-on-a-compact-c-one-hypersurface, def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus, def-cm-continuum-motion, def-cm01-units, def-cross-product-in-r3]
dependency_roles:
  post-cm17-contact-force-model: formulation-prerequisite
  post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm17-density-and-mass: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm17-traction-and-stress: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units: formulation-prerequisite
  def-bounded-piecewise-c-one-euclidean-domain: mathematical-premise
  def-surface-integral-on-a-compact-c-one-hypersurface: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-continuum-motion: mathematical-premise
  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cross-product-in-r3: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: not-applicable
physical_scope: "Adoption of the balance principles of the continuum branch in force form: on every admissible bounded piecewise C-one region inside the current region, the surface traction resultant balances minus the volume integral of the body force minus the inertial term rho a, and the corresponding torque about any fixed origin balances likewise with no body or surface couples. The postulate excludes couple stresses, electromagnetic momentum exchange, mass sources and non-inertial frames; it asserts no constitutive law, no existence of a stress matrix and no measured value."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "R. Abeyaratne, Continuum Mechanics, Volume II of Lecture Notes on the Mechanics of Solids (MIT, 1988, revised 2020), Sections 4.4-4.6 (balance of linear and angular momentum and the field equations)"
      url: "https://web.mit.edu/abeyaratne/Volumes/RCA_Vol_II.pdf"
    - title: "K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Classical Mechanics (September 1, 2026 complete volume), Section 7.2 (Eq. (7.25) and the bulk-plus-surface force balance of a continuum element)"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: n/a
---
Adopt for every model of the continuum branch, together with the force
description of [[post-cm17-contact-force-model]] and the fields of
[[def-cm17-density-and-mass]] and [[def-cm17-traction-and-stress]], the
following balance principles.
**(i) Setting.** Let $U\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^3$ be an open set
containing the swept region of the motion over its time window. The density
$\rho$, the spatial velocity $u$ and the material acceleration $a=D_tu$ are
continuous on $U$ ([[def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus]],
[[def-cm-continuum-motion]]), and $b$ and $t$ are the body-force and traction
fields of [[post-cm17-contact-force-model]]. For $s$ in the time window the
current region is $\Omega_s=\chi_s(B)$.
**(v) Exclusions and status.** These principles are **not** adopted for: bodies
with couple stresses or surface couples; systems exchanging momentum with
electromagnetic or thermal fields (the momentum of those fields would have to
be accounted for separately); bodies with mass sources; and descriptions in
non-inertial frames (where additional inertial terms appear and are excluded
by the choice of the fixed inertial frame of
[[def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units]]). The principles are adopted model
assumptions, not derived here; comparisons of predicted motions with
observations can support them under stated apparatus assumptions, and no
measurement is asserted or used by this postulate. In particular, the
existence of a representing stress matrix, its continuity and its symmetry
are **not** assumed here; they are conditional conclusions of the theorem
items of this page, whose arguments apply the balance principles exactly as
stated.


FILE pthm-cm02-continuation
---
id: pthm-cm02-continuation
kind: physics-theorem
title: "Maximal Newtonian motions and the compact-continuation criterion: finite-time breakdown requires escape from every compact subset of the state domain"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 11
deps: [post-cm02-force-superposition, def-cm02-newtonian-flow, pthm-cm02-ivp, def-cm01-configuration, def-first-order-ode-initial-value-problem-and-solution, def-locally-lipschitz-in-the-state-variable, def-maximal-ode-solution, thm-cm-compact-continuation-for-locally-lipschitz-odes, cor-cm-finite-time-breakdown-requires-escape, thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-a-maximal-ode-solution, thm-heine-borel-rn]
dependency_roles:
  post-cm02-force-superposition: physical-assumption
  def-cm02-newtonian-flow: formulation-prerequisite
  pthm-cm02-ivp: physical-result
  def-cm01-configuration: formulation-prerequisite
  def-first-order-ode-initial-value-problem-and-solution: mathematical-premise
  def-locally-lipschitz-in-the-state-variable: mathematical-premise
  def-maximal-ode-solution: mathematical-premise
  thm-cm-compact-continuation-for-locally-lipschitz-odes: mathematical-premise
  cor-cm-finite-time-breakdown-requires-escape: mathematical-premise
  thm-existence-and-uniqueness-of-a-maximal-ode-solution: mathematical-premise
  thm-heine-borel-rn: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "The continuation and breakdown criterion for the adopted Newtonian force model: the maximal motion of an initial datum is unique; at a finite right endpoint of its maximal interval its state graph leaves every compact subset of the open state domain, so a finite-time breakdown is caused by escape of the state (unbounded positions or velocities, or positions approaching the excluded configurations of the domain) or by the declared end of a finite time window; an autonomous force whose state is trapped in a compact subset of the state domain extends past the putative endpoint. The result is a conditional deduction from the adopted force postulate and the cited mathematical continuation theorems; it asserts no global existence for particular force laws, no empirical statement and no claim that any physical system realizes the model."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "G. Teschl, Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical Systems (AMS Graduate Studies in Mathematics 140, online edition), Section 2.6 'Extensibility of solutions', Corollary 2.15 (compact continuation) and Corollary 2.16 (escape of every compact set), printed pages 50-53"
      url: "https://schecter.math.ncsu.edu/ma_732_sp13/teschl_ode.pdf"
    - title: "Julien Arino, MATH 8430 Fundamental Theory of Ordinary Differential Equations (University of Manitoba), Section 1.3 'Continuation of solutions', Lemma 1.3.1, Theorem 1.3.3 and Corollary 1.3.4, printed pages 24-28: a maximal solution whose right endpoint is finite approaches the boundary of the domain"
      url: "https://julien-arino.github.io/assets/pdf/courses/math8430_lecture_notes.pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Section 7.1.1, printed page 143 (PDF page 146): deterministic evolution from the local existence and uniqueness theorem, with no global-existence claim"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
Adopt the force model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with time window
$I_{0}$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$, masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$ and force map $F$ continuous and locally Lipschitz in the
state variable, locally uniformly in time; let $D=I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb
R^{3})^{N}$, $\mathcal F(t,x,v)=\bigl(v,\mathsf M^{-1}F(t,x,v)\bigr)$,
$U:=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, and let motions, maximal motions and the state
curve $z=(x,\dot x)$ be as in [[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]. Fix an initial datum
$(t_{0},x_{0},v_{0})\in I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$, write
$z_{0}=(x_{0},v_{0})$, and let $x_{\max}:J_{\max}\to Q$ be the maximal motion
with this datum, with state curve $z=(x_{\max},\dot x_{\max}):J_{\max}\to U$
and $J_{\max}=(a,b)$ its maximal interval, $-\infty\le a<b\le+\infty$.
1. **Existence and uniqueness of the maximal motion.** $J_{\max}$ is an open
   interval containing $t_{0}$ and contained in $I_{0}$, and every motion with
   the datum is a restriction of $x_{\max}$.
2. **Breakdown criterion.** If $b<+\infty$, then for every compact
   $K\subseteq D$ and every $\eta>0$ with $(b-\eta,b)\cap J_{\max}\neq\varnothing$
   there is $t\in(b-\eta,b)\cap J_{\max}$ with $(t,z(t))\notin K$. Equivalently:
   if there are a compact $K\subseteq D$ and $\delta>0$ with
   $(t,z(t))\in K$ for all $t\in(b-\delta,b)\cap J_{\max}$, then
   $x_{\max}$ extends to a motion of the model on a strictly larger interval
   than $J_{\max}$ — so $J_{\max}$ cannot have right endpoint $b$.
3. **Autonomous case.** Suppose $F$ does not depend on its time argument:
   $F(t,x,v)=F_{0}(x,v)$ for a map $F_{0}:U\to(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}$ satisfying
   the standing regularity of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] clause (iii).
   Then the associated field is autonomous,
   $\mathcal F(t,x,v)=(v,\mathsf M^{-1}F_{0}(x,v))$, the equations do not use
   the time coordinate, and **the model is read on the extended time window
   $\mathbb R$** (the standing hypotheses are unchanged by the extension).
   With this reading, if there are a compact $K_{0}\subseteq U$ and
   $\delta>0$ with $z(t)\in K_{0}$ for all $t\in(b-\delta,b)\cap J_{\max}$,
   then $x_{\max}$ extends past $b$; consequently $b=+\infty$, and the motion
   is defined for all forward times $t\ge t_{0}$.
4. **Reading.** Suppose the time window is all of $\mathbb R$ (the
   time-dependent case included) and $b<+\infty$. Then for every compact
   $K_{0}\subseteq U$ and every $\delta>0$ there is $t\in(b-\delta,b)$ with
   $z(t)\notin K_{0}$: near a finite breakdown the state leaves every compact
   subset of the open state domain $U=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$. Consequently a
   finite-time breakdown is caused either by unboundedness of the state or by
   approach of the positions to the complement of $Q$ (the configurations
   excluded from the model); the mere finiteness of $F$ at every point of $Q$
   prevents neither.
[L1] The associated first-order system, its motions and their equivalence with
the second-order motions, the state domain $D=I_{0}\times U$, and maximal
motions are [[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]; the state is $z=(x,v)$ and
$U=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$.
- **No uniform lower bound on the maximal interval.** Clause 2 is a criterion,
  not a quantitative estimate; the length of existence depends on the local
  Lipschitz data of $F$ and on the distance of the datum from the complement of
  the domain, and it can shrink to zero along a sequence of data approaching
  the excluded configurations.
- **The excluded configurations matter.** For a singular force law read on the
  collision-excluded domain $Q=\mathbb R^{3N}\setminus\Delta$, a motion that
  reaches a collision configuration in finite time has no extension beyond it;
  the companion counterexample on the examples page computes such a motion for
  the inverse-square external model, where the state leaves every compact
  subset by unbounded velocity while the position approaches the excluded
  point.

FILE pthm-cm04-equilibrium-stability-at-a-strict-minimum
---
id: pthm-cm04-equilibrium-stability-at-a-strict-minimum
kind: physics-theorem
title: "A strict local minimum of the potential is a Lyapunov stable equilibrium of the conservative one-dimensional model, and its linearisation is an oscillator-type model"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 15
deps: [def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria, def-cm04-phase-plane-and-energy-level-sets, pthm-cm04-conservative-motion-and-energy, post-cm04-harmonic-model, def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart, thm-cm-energy-stability-of-a-strict-minimum, prop-cm-linearization-of-the-mechanical-system-at-an-equilibrium, def-cm-equilibrium-and-lyapunov-stability, def-cm-oscillator-model-and-phase-system, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-metric-ball, def-interval, def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]
dependency_roles:
  def-cm04-one-dimensional-models-and-equilibria: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm04-phase-plane-and-energy-level-sets: formulation-prerequisite
  pthm-cm04-conservative-motion-and-energy: physical-result
  post-cm04-harmonic-model: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart: mathematical-premise
  thm-cm-energy-stability-of-a-strict-minimum: mathematical-premise
  prop-cm-linearization-of-the-mechanical-system-at-an-equilibrium: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-equilibrium-and-lyapunov-stability: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-oscillator-model-and-phase-system: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-inner-product: mathematical-premise
  def-metric-ball: mathematical-premise
  def-interval: mathematical-premise
  def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "The equilibrium theory of a conservative one-dimensional model near a strict local minimum of its potential: the rest point is Lyapunov stable, with the Euclidean phase-plane norm, and its linearisation is the oscillator-type model with data (m,0,U''(x_*),0), which is the harmonic model with spring constant U''(x_*) when that number is positive. The statement is conditional on the declared potential and its strict local minimum; it asserts no asymptotic stability, no attraction and no instability in any case, and no measured value."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Section 3.2, Eqs. (3.10)-(3.18), chapter pages 2-4 (PDF pages 34-36): the quadratic expansion of the effective potential about a fixed point, the effective spring constant d^2 U_ef/dq^2, the characteristic equation, harmonic motion with frequency (kappa_ef/m_ef)^{1/2} at a minimum and the exponentially growing solution at a maximum"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "G. Teschl, Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical Systems (AMS Graduate Studies in Mathematics 140, online edition), Section 6.7, Theorem 6.16, printed pages 214-216: a fixed point of Newton's equation is stable if the potential has a local minimum there"
      url: "https://schecter.math.ncsu.edu/ma_732_sp13/teschl_ode.pdf"
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.6, printed pages 37-39 (PDF pages 43-45): stability of equilibria read from the potential, small oscillations about a minimum and the positive-definite kinetic matrix"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
1. **Lyapunov stability.** $(x_{*},0)$ is a Lyapunov stable rest point of
   the phase system in the sense of [[def-cm-equilibrium-and-lyapunov-stability]]:
   for every $\varepsilon>0$ with $\overline B((x_{*},0),\varepsilon)\subseteq
   Q\times\mathbb R$ there is $\delta>0$ such that the unique maximal solution of the autonomous phase equation through the datum of every motion $x$ of the
   model with $\lVert(x(t_{0}),x'(t_{0}))-(x_{*},0)\rVert_{2}<\delta$ for some
   $t_{0}\in I_{0}$ is defined for all $t\ge t_{0}$ and satisfies
   $$\bigl\lVert\bigl(x(t)-x_{*},\,x'(t)\bigr)\bigr\rVert_{2}<\varepsilon \qquad(t\ge t_{0}).$$
   Here $x$ in the displayed all-time bound denotes that maximal extension; on the original motion interval it agrees with the given motion. The autonomous equation is read on $\mathbb R\times Q\times\mathbb R$, even when the declared time window $I_0$ is smaller; no enlargement of that declared window is asserted. The norm is the Euclidean norm on $\mathbb R^{2}$ of
   [[def-euclidean-inner-product]]; the balls are those of
   [[def-metric-ball]]. No asymptotic stability and no attraction are
   asserted.
- **Stability is not attraction.** Clause 1 asserts only Lyapunov stability:
  nearby motions stay nearby, with no claim that they approach the rest
  point. For the conservative model the energy is conserved, and the
  companion results do not make the rest point attracting; the dissipative
  model is where attraction is proved.

Adopt the force model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with $N=2$
labels, masses $m_1,m_2>0$ ([[post-cm01-worldlines]] clause (v)), a time
window $I_0$, and the collision-excluded configuration domain
$$Q:=\{\,x=(x_1,x_2)\in(\mathbb R^3)^2:\lvert x_1-x_2\rvert\in J_0\,\}$$
for an open interval $J_0\subseteq(0,\infty)$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]). Suppose the model presents its force map
through the time-independent potential
$$U(x_1,x_2):=u(\lvert x_1-x_2\rvert),$$
where $u:J_0\to\mathbb R$ is of class $C^2$
([[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]), so that
$F_i(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x_i}U(x)$ on the state domain ([[post-cm02-interactions]],
clauses (iii) and (iv), [[lem-cm05-radial-potential-gradient]]). Let
$x:J\to Q$ be a **motion** of the model on an interval $J$ with nonempty
interior ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]) and write
$$M:=m_1+m_2,\quad \mu:=\frac{m_1m_2}{M},\quad X:=\frac{m_1x_1+m_2x_2}{M},\quad V:=X',\quad P:=m_1x_1'+m_2x_2',\quad r:=x_1-x_2,$$
with $\lvert\cdot\rvert=\lVert\cdot\rVert_2$ the Euclidean norm
([[def-euclidean-inner-product]]) and dimensions as in [[def-cm01-units]]
($[M]=[\mu]=\mu$, $[X]=[r]=\ell$, $[P]=\mu\ell\tau^{-1}$). Then:
[L6] Vector FTC ([[cor-vector-valued-ftc-and-lipschitz-bound]]): if $f:[a,b]\to\mathbb R^3$ is differentiable with integrable derivative, then $\int_a^bf'=f(b)-f(a)$; the interval $J$ has nonempty interior, hence at least two points, and the open interval $A\subseteq\mathbb R^3$ and the configuration domain $Q$ are those of the hypotheses ([[def-interval]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]).
- **Scope of the reconstruction.** The bijection is stated on a fixed interval $J$ with nonempty interior; it does not assert global existence of either side, and the collision set $x_1=x_2$ is excluded by the configuration domain, so $\lvert r\rvert>0$ throughout.

FILE pthm-cm06-inertial-forces
Adopt the force branch of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: $N\ge1$ labels
with masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, time window $I_{0}$, open configuration
domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ and force map $F$ with the standing
regularity; fix one inertial frame $F$ with adapted coordinates $(t,x)$
([[post-cm01-frames]], [[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]],
[[def-cm01-units]]). Let a moving spatial chart of $F$ be given by data
$(I,c,R)$ with $I\subseteq I_{0}$ ([[def-cm06-moving-frame]]), with angular
velocity $\omega$ and inertial-terms field
$$\tau(t,y,\dot y):=-\Bigl(2\,\omega(t)\times\dot y+\omega'(t)\times y+\omega(t)\times\bigl(\omega(t)\times y\bigr)+R(t)^{\mathsf T}c''(t)\Bigr).$$

Adopt a holonomic-constraint model of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]
with datum $(O,k,G)$, mass operator $\mathsf M$, force map $F$, and the
ideal-reaction law of [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]]. Suppose, in addition
to the hypotheses of those items:
2. **determined dynamics.** With
   $a(t,x,v):=\mathsf M^{-1}\bigl(F(t,x,v)+D_{x}G(t,x)^{\mathsf T}\Lambda(t,x,v)\bigr)$,
   the tuple $(x,w)$ solves the first-order system
   $$x'=v,\qquad v'=a(t,x,v)$$
   on $J$; conversely every solution $(x,v)$ of this system on an interval
   whose initial datum is consistent, with
   $R(t):=D_{x}G(t,x(t))^{\mathsf T}\Lambda(t,x(t),v(t))$, is a constrained
   motion of the model satisfying the ideal-reaction law; and through every
   consistent datum $(t_{0},x_{0},v_{0})$ there is a unique maximal solution
   of the system. Hence the holonomic-constraint model with ideal reactions
   has determined dynamics on its constraint locus.
**Given:** The holonomic-constraint model and ideal-reaction law of the statement, with datum $(O,k,G)$, mass operator $\mathsf M$, force map $F$, and a constrained motion $x:J\to Q$ with ideal reaction $R$ and velocity $w=x'$.
[F1] The model ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]): $\mathsf M$ is symmetric positive definite; $\Sigma\subseteq I_{0}\times Q$ is the nonempty constraint locus with $D_{x}G$ of rank $k$ on $\Sigma$; the virtual displacements are $V(t,x)=\ker D_{x}G(t,x)$; a constrained motion satisfies $\mathsf Mx''=F(t,x,w)+R$; and a constrained curve family has $G(t,x(t))=0$.
[F3] The chart proposition ([[prop-cm-ideal-holonomic-system-in-a-time-dependent-chart]]): let $O'\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{n}$ be open and nonempty, $G:O'\to\mathbb R^{k}$ of class $C^{3}$ with $G_{q}$ of rank $k$ on $O'$, $M$ symmetric positive definite and $f:O'\times\mathbb R^{n}\to\mathbb R^{n}$ of class $C^{1}$; with $C=G_{q}M^{-1}G_{q}^{\mathsf T}$ and $\Lambda,a$ as in that item, $C$ is symmetric positive definite, $\Lambda$ and $a$ are $C^{1}$, every initial value problem for $q'=v$, $v'=a(t,q,v)$ has a unique maximal solution, solutions with consistent data satisfy $G(t,q(t))=0$ and $G_{t}+G_{q}q'=0$ and are ideal-constraint motions with multiplier $\Lambda$, and conversely every $C^{2}$ ideal-constraint motion of $(G,M,f)$ with permitted velocity solves the system.
[F4] Identification of data: put $O':=O\cap(I_{0}\times Q)$, an open nonempty subset of $\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ (it contains the nonempty $\Sigma$), $n:=3N$, $M:=\mathsf M$, $f:=$ the restriction of $F$ to $O'\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, and $q:=x$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]] for $\mathsf M$ and $Q$, [[def-interval]] for $J$).
1.1 The hypotheses of [F3] hold for the data of [F4]: $O'$ is open and nonempty, $G$ is $C^{3}$ with $G_{q}=D_{x}G$ of rank $k$ on $O'\subseteq O$ by (h1), $\mathsf M$ is symmetric positive definite by [F1], and $f$ is $C^{1}$ on $O'\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ because $F$ is $C^{1}$ on the open set $I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3N})$ by (h2). [F1, F3, F4, given]
3.1 Clauses 1 and 2, regularity and forward direction: by [F3], $C$ is symmetric positive definite and invertible and $\Lambda,a$ are $C^{1}$ on $O'\times\mathbb R^{3N}$; composing with the $C^{1}$ curve $t\mapsto(t,x(t),w(t))$ shows that $\lambda$ is $C^{1}$ as well, completing clause 1. Moreover $x'=w$ and $w'=x''=\mathsf M^{-1}(F+D_{x}G^{\mathsf T}\lambda)=\mathsf M^{-1}(F+D_{x}G^{\mathsf T}\Lambda(t,x,w))=a(t,x,w)$ by step 2.1, so $(x,w)$ solves the system of clause 2. [F3, step 1.2, step 2.1]

Adopt a holonomic-constraint model of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]]
with datum $(O,k,G)$ and the ideal-reaction law of
[[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]], and suppose that $G$ is of class $C^{2}$
with $D_{x}G$ of rank $k$ on the constraint locus $\Sigma$. Let
$x:J\to Q$ be a constrained motion with ideal reaction $R$ on an interval
$J\subseteq I_{0}$ with nonempty interior, with velocity $w=x'$ and multiplier
$\lambda:J\to\mathbb R^{k}$ as in [[pthm-cm06-multiplier-balance]] clause 1.
Suppose further that the model's force map is presented through a potential
with residual forces in the sense of clause (a) of
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]: a $C^{2}$ function
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ of dimension $\mu\ell^{2}\tau^{-2}$ and maps
$d_{i}:I_{0}\times Q\times(\mathbb R^{3})^{N}\to\mathbb R^{3}$ with the
standing regularity of clause (iii) of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], such
that
$$F_{i}(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U(t,x)+d_{i}(t,x,v)\qquad(i=1,\dots,N)$$
on the state domain ([[def-cm01-units]] for the dimensions). Write
$K$ and $E(t):=K(t)+U(t,x(t))$ for the kinetic and mechanical energy of
[[def-cm03-centre-of-mass-and-momenta]] clauses (b),(d) and of clause (b) of
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], and
$\mathfrak d_{i}(t):=d_{i}(t,x(t),w(t))$ for the evaluated residual forces.
Then:
**Given:** The holonomic-constraint model, ideal-reaction law, constrained motion $x:J\to Q$ with reaction $R$, velocity $w=x'$ and multiplier $\lambda$, and the potential presentation $(U,d)$ of the statement.
[L1] The model and the ideal law ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]], [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]], [[pthm-cm06-multiplier-balance]] clause 1): $\mathsf Mx''=F(t,x,w)+R$; $R(t)=D_{x}G(t,x(t))^{\mathsf T}\lambda(t)$; and $G(t,x(t))=0$, whence $G_{t}(t,x(t))+D_{x}G(t,x(t))w(t)=0$ by the chain rule ([[thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives]]).
[L6] The scleronomic case: the datum is scleronomic when its constraint locus is time-independent, and then $G_{t}(t,x)=0$ for every $(t,x)\in\Sigma$ for the declared presentation ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]] clause (d)); a constrained motion has $(t,x(t))\in\Sigma$ for every $t\in J$.

  def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix: mathematical-premise

  def-interval: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "Covariance of the model's equation of motion under a time-dependent coordinate map of the configuration domain: along a curve described by a C^3 map X with everywhere invertible configuration Jacobian, the Euler-Lagrange expression of the substituted Lagrangian is the transpose-Jacobian multiple of the inertial-chart expression, so the model's potential-part equation holds along the Cartesian description if and only if the substituted Euler-Lagrange equation holds along the coordinate description; when the chart condition holds, this is equivalent to stationarity of the substituted action. The statement is conditional on the adopted force branch, the potential presentation and the smooth invertibility of the coordinate map; it asserts nothing about constraints with fewer coordinates, which are treated separately, and no empirical claim."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.2 'Changing Coordinate Systems', printed pages 13-16 (PDF pages 19-22): Lagrange's equations take the same form in any coordinate system, with the rotating-coordinate example"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "D. Cline, Variational Principles in Classical Mechanics (2nd edition, University of Rochester River Campus Libraries, open textbook, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0), Sections 6.3-6.4 and 5.8: Lagrange equations from d'Alembert's principle in generalized coordinates and generalized coordinates in variational calculus"
      url: "https://downloads.libretexts.org/api/v1/download/phys-9557/pdf"
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Section 2.1, chapter pages 1-2: transformation of the Lagrangian description to arbitrary generalized coordinates"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"

  precheck: pass

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]).
Let $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ be open and nonempty and let
$X:\Omega\to Q$ be of class $C^{3}$ with $X_{q}(t,q)$ invertible for every
$(t,q)\in\Omega$ ([[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]],
[[def-invertible-euclidean-linear-map]]). Put
$$\widetilde O:=\bigl\{(t,q,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb R^{3N}:\ \bigl(t,X(t,q),X_{t}(t,q)+X_{q}(t,q)v\bigr)\in I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\bigr\},$$
Let $a<b$ and let $q:[a,b]\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ be a $C^{2}$ curve with
$(t,q(t),q'(t))\in\widetilde O$ for all $t\in[a,b]$; put $x(t):=X(t,q(t))$.
Then:
2. If in addition there is an open set $U\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ with
   $q([a,b])\subseteq U$ and $[a,b]\times U\times\mathbb R^{3N}\subseteq\widetilde O$,
   then $(\widetilde O,\widetilde L)$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over
   $[a,b]\times U$ and: $q$ is stationary for the substituted action
   $\widetilde S(q)=\int_{a}^{b}\widetilde L(t,q,q')\,dt$ if and only if $x$ is
   stationary for $S$.
[F3] The chart dictionary ([[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]], [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[thm-cm-stationarity-iff-euler-lagrange]]): a pair $(O',\mathcal L)$ with $O'\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ open and $[a,b]\times U\times\mathbb R^{3N}\subseteq O'$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over $[a,b]\times U$; stationarity means the vanishing of the first variation $\delta S(q)[\eta]$ for all variations $\eta$ with $\eta(a)=\eta(b)=0$, and it is equivalent to the vanishing of the Euler-Lagrange expression on $[a,b]$.
1.1 Clause 1, structure: the Cartesian Lagrangian $L$ is $C^{2}$ on the open set $O_{x}:=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]), the map $X$ and the set $\Omega$ satisfy the hypotheses of [F1] with $n:=3N$, and the displayed $\widetilde O$, $\widetilde L$ are exactly the supplier's constructions; hence $\widetilde O$ is open, $\widetilde L$ is $C^{2}$, and the transport identity holds along $q$. [F1, given]
- **Fewer coordinates are a different construction.** Here $X$ is a local
  diffeomorphism of the configuration domain ($X_{q}$ invertible), so the
  number of coordinates equals the number of Cartesian degrees of freedom.
  The reduction to a parametrisation of a holonomic constraint locus, where
  $X$ has fewer coordinates than the ambient configuration space, is treated
  separately on this page.
