## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and the mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]).
Let $a<b$ with $[a,b]\subseteq I_{0}$, and let $x:[a,b]\to Q$ be a $C^{2}$
admissible curve ([[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]]). Then the
following three conditions are equivalent:
[F1] The chart instance and first variation ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[lem-cm-first-variation-formula]]): $(O,L)$ with $O=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over $[a,b]\times Q$, and the notions of admissible curve, variation, action, first variation and stationarity are those of [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]] instantiated at it.
[F4] The model's equation of motion ([[post-cm02-force-superposition]] clause (iv), [[def-cm01-velocity-acceleration]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): a motion of the model with force map $F$ satisfies $m_{i}x_{i}''(t)=F_{i}(t,x(t),x'(t))$ on its interval, equivalently $\mathsf M x''(t)=F(t,x(t),x'(t))$; for the presentation $(U,d)$ of the statement the potential part of $F$ is $-\nabla_{x}U$ ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]] clause (a)).

The result is a conditional deduction from the adopted force branch, the declared potential presentation and the cited mathematical first-variation formula; it asserts no equation of motion, which is the subject of the following items."

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and the mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$, time
window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and a potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ of class $C^{2}$
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]).
Let $a<b$ with $[a,b]\subseteq I_{0}$, let $x:[a,b]\to Q$ be a $C^{2}$
admissible curve and let $\eta$ be a variation of $x$
([[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]]). Then:
   where $\nabla_{x}U=(\nabla_{x_{1}}U,\dots,\nabla_{x_{N}}U)$ is the Euclidean
   gradient in the configuration slot and the dot is the Euclidean inner
   product of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ ([[def-euclidean-inner-product]]);
[F1] The chart instance ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]]): $(O,L)$ with $O=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over $[a,b]\times Q$, and admissible curves, variations, action, first variation and Euler-Lagrange expression are those of [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]] instantiated at it; thus the Euler-Lagrange expression is $E_{L}(x)(t)=L_{x}(t,x(t),x'(t))-\frac{d}{dt}\bigl(L_{v}(t,x(t),x'(t))\bigr)$ with $L_{x}$, $L_{v}$ the Euclidean gradients in the second and third slots ([[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]).
[F3] Slots of the mechanical Lagrangian ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $L(t,x,v)=K(v)-U(t,x)$ with $K(v)=\frac12 v\cdot\mathsf M v$; the mass operator $\mathsf M$ is linear and symmetric positive definite; $U$ does not depend on $v$; $K$ does not depend on $x$.
1.3 Gradient in the configuration slot: because $K$ does not depend on $x$ by [F3], $L_{x}(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x}U(t,x)$ for every $(t,x,v)$; hence along $x$ the configuration gradient is $-\nabla_{x}U(t,x(t))$. [F3, algebra]

id: pthm-cm07-lagrangian-reduction-of-ideal-constraints
kind: physics-theorem
title: "Ideal scleronomic holonomic constraints: the substituted Lagrange equations are equivalent to the constrained model equation"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 14
deps: [def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces, def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian, lem-cm07-immersion-euler-lagrange-transport, pthm-cm07-first-variation-and-euler-lagrange-expression, def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model, post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law, lem-cm-constraint-multipliers-and-accelerations, def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy, post-cm02-force-superposition, def-cm01-configuration, def-cm01-velocity-acceleration, def-cm01-units, def-euclidean-submersions-and-immersions, cor-matrix-rank-nullity, def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient, def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms, thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives, def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix, def-transpose-of-a-matrix, def-linear-map, def-euclidean-inner-product, def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral, def-interval]

physical_scope: "The elimination of ideal scleronomic holonomic constraints by parametrization: for a global C^3 parametrization of the constrained configurations of a scleronomic regular holonomic datum, a C^2 coordinate curve describes an ideal constrained motion of the force model exactly when it solves the declared Lagrange equations of the substituted Lagrangian with the residual generalized force; the reaction is then uniquely determined, annihilates the virtual displacements and admits the unique continuous multiplier. The statement is conditional on the adopted force branch, the potential presentation, the constraint datum with its rank hypothesis and the ideal-reaction law; it asserts no solution existence, no uniqueness of the coordinate curve for given data and no empirical claim."

      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"

## Statement
Adopt the force-branch data of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]]: $N\ge1$
labelled particles with masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, an open time window
$I_{0}$, an open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ and a force map
$F$ with the standing regularity; adopt a regular holonomic-constraint datum
$(O,k,G)$ of [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]] and the ideal-reaction law
of [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]]. Assume the datum is **scleronomic with
time-independent locus** $N\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$:
Let $(U,d)$ be a potential presentation of $F$ in the sense of clause (a) of
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], so that
$F_{i}(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U(t,x)+d_{i}(t,x,v)$. Let $r:=3N-k$,
let $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb R^{r}$ be open and nonempty, and let
$X:\Omega\to Q$ be a **parametrization of the constrained configurations** in
the sense of clause (d) of [[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]:
$X$ is of class $C^{3}$, $DX(q)$ is injective for every $q\in\Omega$,
$X(\Omega)=N\cap Q$ and $(t,X(q))\in O$ for all $(t,q)\in I_{0}\times\Omega$.
Define the kinetic metric, substituted potential, substituted Lagrangian and
residual generalized force
1. $x$ is a constrained motion of the model with an ideal reaction field: there
   is a continuous $R:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ with
   $\mathsf M x''(t)=F\bigl(t,x(t),x'(t)\bigr)+R(t)$ and
   $R(t)\cdot\delta=0$ for every $t\in J$ and every virtual displacement
   $\delta\in\ker D_{x}G(x(t))$;
2. $\Lambda(\widetilde L)(q)(t)=Q^{\mathrm{res}}\bigl(t,q(t),q'(t)\bigr)$ for
   every $t\in J$, i.e. the declared Lagrange equations of
   [[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]] hold along $q$.
[F1] The holonomic-constraint model ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]], [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]]): a constrained curve family is a $C^{2}$ curve $x:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ with $(t,x(t))\in O$ and $G(t,x(t))=0$; for a scleronomic datum the locus membership is a condition on $x$ alone and $G_{t}=0$ on $\Sigma$ for the declared presentation; a constrained motion satisfies $\mathsf M x''=F(t,x,x')+R$ with a continuous reaction field $R$; the ideal-reaction law imposes $R(t)\cdot\delta=0$ for every $\delta\in\ker D_{x}G(x(t))$; the virtual displacements are that kernel.
[F2] Virtual displacements and the parametrization ([[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]] clause (a), [[cor-matrix-rank-nullity]], [[def-euclidean-submersions-and-immersions]], [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]], [[thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives]], [[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]]): $D_{x}G(x)$ is surjective with $k$ rows, so $\dim\ker D_{x}G(x)=3N-k=r$; since $G(t,X(q))=0$ for all $(t,q)\in I_{0}\times\Omega$, the chain rule gives $D_{x}G(t,X(q))\,DX(q)=0$, so $DX(q)(\mathbb R^{r})\subseteq\ker D_{x}G(t,X(q))$; the left side has dimension $r$ because $DX(q)$ is injective; hence the two spaces are equal.
[F5] The multiplier lemma ([[lem-cm-constraint-multipliers-and-accelerations]]): for a surjective Jacobian $A=D_{x}G(x(t))$, a vector $R(t)$ annihilating $\ker A$ has a unique representation $R(t)=A^{\mathsf T}\lambda(t)$; the pairings are the Euclidean inner products of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ ([[def-euclidean-inner-product]], [[def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix]], [[def-transpose-of-a-matrix]], [[def-linear-map]]).
- **Why the parametrization must be global.** The equivalence uses
  $X(\Omega)=N\cap Q$, so every constrained configuration occurs as $X(q)$.
  A merely local parametrization gives the equivalence on the parametrized
  piece; assembling pieces across a covering requires a partition argument
  that is not asserted here.

  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "The stationary-action principle for the global potential branch of the force model: every motion of the model restricts to a stationary curve of the mechanical action on each closed subinterval, and conversely every C^2 stationary curve on a closed interval is the restriction of the unique maximal motion of the model through its initial datum, so that the stationary curves are exactly the restrictions of motions. The equivalence is conditional on the adopted force branch, on the declared potential presentation with no residual forces, and on the cited existence, uniqueness and continuation theorems; it asserts no global existence, no minimisation property and no empirical statement."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.1, printed pages 10-13 (PDF pages 16-19): Lagrange's equations as the stationarity condition of the action, equivalent to Newton's equations for a particle in a potential"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "D. Cline, Variational Principles in Classical Mechanics (2nd edition, University of Rochester River Campus Libraries, open textbook, CC BY-NC-SA 4.0), Section 6.2: the Newtonian plausibility argument identifying the stationary curves of the action T - U with the Newtonian trajectories"
      url: "https://downloads.libretexts.org/api/v1/download/phys-9557/pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Section 1.2 and Chapter 2: uniqueness of Newtonian evolution from initial data and the equivalence of the Lagrangian and Newtonian descriptions"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
verification:
  precheck: pass

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]).
Assume the presentation has **no residual forces**, $d\equiv0$, so that the
force map itself is $F_{i}(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U(t,x)$. Then:
[F1] The chart instance ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]]): $(O,L)$ with $O=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart, and stationarity of admissible curves is the stationarity of that chart.
[F3] Motions and the maximal motion ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] clause (iv), [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]] clause (a), [[pthm-cm02-ivp]]): a motion on an interval $J$ with nonempty interior is a $C^{2}$ curve $x:J\to Q$ with $J\subseteq I_{0}$ satisfying $\mathsf M x''(t)=F(t,x(t),x'(t))$ for all $t\in J$, i.e. with $F_{i}=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U$ the equation $\mathsf M x''=-\nabla_{x}U$; and through every datum $(t_{0},x_{0},v_{0})\in I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ there is exactly one maximal motion, whose maximal interval $J_{\max}$ is an open interval containing $t_{0}$ and contained in $I_{0}$, and every motion with that datum is a restriction of it. In particular two motions with the same datum agree on the intersection of their intervals.
[F5] Compactness of a $C^{2}$ graph in the state domain ([[thm-heine-borel-rn]], [[thm-continuous-image-of-a-compact-space-is-compact]], [[def-continuity-real]], [[def-vector-valued-derivative-and-integral]], [[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]], [[def-interval]]): if $x:[c,d]\to Q$ is $C^{2}$ then the graph $K:=\{(t,x(t),x'(t)):t\in[c,d]\}$ is the image of the compact interval $[c,d]$ under the continuous map $t\mapsto(t,x(t),x'(t))$, hence compact, and it is contained in the open state domain $D=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ because $[c,d]\subseteq I_{0}$ and $x$ takes values in $Q$.
1.2 Clause 2, setup: let $x:[a,b]\to Q$ be stationary. By [F2] the curve satisfies $\mathsf M x''(t)=-\nabla_{x}U(t,x(t))$ for every $t\in[a,b]$; the datum $(a,x(a),x'(a))$ lies in $I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ because $[a,b]\subseteq I_{0}$ and $x$ takes values in $Q$. Let $x_{\max}:J_{\max}\to Q$ be the maximal motion with this datum and $J_{\max}=(\alpha,\beta)$ its maximal interval; by [F3], $\alpha<a<\beta$. The restriction $x|_{[a,b]}$ is a motion on $[a,b]$ with the same datum, so by the uniqueness clause of [F3], $x_{\max}$ and $x$ agree on $J_{\max}\cap[a,b]$. [F2, F3, given]

M x) satisfies the model's Noether identity exactly when the potential is invariant under the simultaneous translation of all labels by the vector a; in the global potential branch the boost charge a . (t P - M X) is then constant, the component a of the centre of mass moves uniformly, and the boosted curves t -> x(t) + s t a of motions are again motions; for an arbitrary presentation the exact rate of the charge is the time times the component a of the total force, and a nonzero translation derivative of the potential at a nonzero time produces a motion along which the charge is not constant. Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and $U$ of
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]. Fix a nonzero vector
$a\in\mathbb R^{3}$ and consider the maps
$$\xi^{a}(t,x):=t\,\bigl(a,\dots,a\bigr)\in\mathbb R^{3N},\qquad B^{a}(t,x):=\bigl\langle a,\mathsf M x\bigr\rangle=\sum_{i}m_{i}\,a\cdot x_{i},$$
[F1] Model data and chart ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $O=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is open and $(O,L)$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over every $[a',b']\times Q$ with $[a',b']\subseteq I_{0}$; $L=K-U$ with $L_{x}=-\nabla_{x}U$, $L_{v}=\mathsf M v$; $F_{i}=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U+d_{i}$; and $U$ is $C^{2}$ so that the map $(t,x)\mapsto\sum_{i}a\cdot\nabla_{x_{i}}U(t,x)$ is continuous ([[def-continuity-real]], [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]]).
- **Kinematics versus model symmetry.** Every model of the framework is
  described in every inertial frame, and the Galilean transformation of the
  description is available whatever the force law
  ([[post-cm02-force-superposition]] clause (vi)); the boost charge, by
  contrast, is a conserved quantity only when the declared potential is
  invariant under the simultaneous translation of all labels. For a confining
  potential such as $U=\tfrac{k}{2}\sum_{i}\lvert x_{i}\rvert^{2}$ the condition
  fails at every nonzero configuration, the datum of clause 1 does not satisfy
  the model identity, and the centre of mass oscillates rather than moving
  uniformly. This is the precise boundary of the boost statement: it is a
  property of the model's Lagrangian, not of Galilean kinematics.

([[def-cm08-symmetry-of-the-mechanical-lagrangian]] clause (a)), with charge
[F1] Model data and chart ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $O=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is open and $(O,L)$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over every $[a,b]\times Q$ with $[a,b]\subseteq I_{0}$; $L=K-U$ with $K(v)=\frac12v\cdot\mathsf M v$, $L_{v}=\mathsf M v$, $L_{x}=-\nabla_{x}U$, $L_{t}=-U_{t}$; the force map is $F_{i}=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U+d_{i}$; and $U'=U+c$ presents the same force map with the same residual forces, with $L'=L-c$.
[F7] Regularity and dimensions ([[def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms]], [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]], [[def-directional-and-partial-derivatives]], [[def-cm01-units]]): all maps occurring in the proof are of the stated differentiability class, and $\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle$ is the Euclidean inner product of $\mathbb R^{3N}$.
- **Sign convention.** The convention is the one fixed by
  [[def-cm08-symmetry-of-the-mechanical-lagrangian]]: $\xi$ generates the
  configuration change $x\mapsto x+s\xi$ at fixed $t$ up to the time shift
  $s\tau$. With the inverse-family convention of the SICM source the same
  identity produces the negative of the charge; the identity itself and the
  conservation statement are convention independent.

Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and $U$ of
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]. Let $\Omega\subseteq\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^{3N}$
be open and nonempty and let $X:\Omega\to Q$ be of class $C^{3}$ with
$X_{q}(t,q)$ invertible for every $(t,q)\in\Omega$; let
$$\widetilde O:=\bigl\{(t,q,v)\in\Omega\times\mathbb R^{3N}:\bigl(t,X(t,q),X_{t}(t,q)+X_{q}(t,q)v\bigr)\in O\bigr\},\qquad \widetilde L(t,q,v):=L\bigl(t,X(t,q),\,X_{t}(t,q)+X_{q}(t,q)v\bigr)$$
be the substituted data of [[pthm-cm07-coordinate-covariance-of-the-equations]].
Let $q:J\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ be a $C^{2}$ curve with
$(t,q(t),q'(t))\in\widetilde O$ for every $t\in J$, and put $x(t):=X(t,q(t))$.
Define the **chart energy function** and the **inertial mechanical energy**
along the curve by
[F2] The mechanical Lagrangian ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $L(t,x,v)=K(v)-U(t,x)$ with $K(v)=\tfrac12\langle v,\mathsf M v\rangle$, $\mathsf M$ symmetric positive definite, $U$ of class $C^{2}$, $L_{v}=\mathsf M v$, $L_{x}=-\nabla_{x}U$; the force map is $F_{i}=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U+d_{i}$, and for $d\equiv0$ the motions satisfy $\mathsf M x''=-\nabla_{x}U$.

Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], with
masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$ and $U$ of
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], and let $(\tau,\xi,B)$ be a
symmetry datum of the model's mechanical Lagrangian with $\tau\equiv0$
([[def-cm08-symmetry-of-the-mechanical-lagrangian]] clause (a)); write
   where the pairing is the Euclidean inner product of $\mathbb R^{3N}$;
   consequently $C$ is constant on $J$ if and only if this pairing vanishes
   for every $t\in J$.
[F1] Model data and chart ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm-time-dependent-lagrangian-and-action]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $O=I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is open and $(O,L)$ is a time-dependent Lagrangian chart over every $[a,b]\times Q$ with $[a,b]\subseteq I_{0}$; $L(t,x,v)=K(v)-U(t,x)$ with $K(v)=\frac12v\cdot\mathsf M v$, $L_{v}(t,x,v)=\mathsf M v$, $L_{x}(t,x,v)=-\nabla_{x}U(t,x)$ and $L_{t}(t,x,v)=-U_{t}(t,x)$; the force map is $F_{i}=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U+d_{i}$.
[F2] The symmetry datum and its charge ([[def-cm08-symmetry-of-the-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm-infinitesimal-point-symmetry]]): $\xi:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R^{3N}$ and $B:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ are of class $C^{2}$ and satisfy the strict identity $L_{x}(t,x,v)\cdot\xi(t,x)+L_{v}(t,x,v)\cdot D_{t}\xi(t,x,v)=D_{t}B(t,x,v)$ on $O$; the charge is the displayed function $C$; when $\xi$ is independent of $t$ the pair $(\xi,B)$ is an infinitesimal point symmetry of the chart $(O,L)$ in the sense of the cited definition.

FILE pthm-cm09-linearization-at-a-strict-minimum
---
id: pthm-cm09-linearization-at-a-strict-minimum
kind: physics-theorem
title: "Small oscillations at a strict minimum: the linearized model, its normal modes and the Lyapunov stability of the nonlinear equilibrium"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 16
deps: [def-cm09-coupled-quadratic-model, pthm-cm09-free-modes-and-degeneracy, thm-cm-quadratic-mode-decomposition, prop-cm-linearization-of-the-mechanical-system-at-an-equilibrium, thm-cm-energy-stability-of-a-strict-minimum, lem-cm-strict-minimum-has-positive-semidefinite-hessian, def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart, def-cm-equilibrium-and-lyapunov-stability, cor-hessian-is-symmetric, def-hessian-and-euclidean-critical-point, def-matrix-product-and-identity-matrix, def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms, def-interval]

provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "A mechanical system in a chart at a strict local minimum of its potential: the assigned small-oscillation quadratic model with mass metric M(q_0) and stiffness the Hessian D^2 U(q_0) (symmetric and non-negative), the identification of that model with the derivative linearization of the mechanical field, the complete normal-mode classification of the linearized motions with frequencies sqrt(lambda_i) and no exponential directions, and the Lyapunov stability of the nonlinear equilibrium by exact energy conservation. The stability conclusion is a statement about the declared chart model; it asserts no asymptotic attraction, no measured frequency, no particular physical system and no empirical claim, and the accuracy of the linear model as an approximation of the nonlinear motion is a separate finite-time statement on this page."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 2.6, Eqs. (2.113)-(2.129), printed pages 38-41 (PDF pages 44-47): expansion about an equilibrium, the quadratic matrices and the stability reading of the normal modes"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "I. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309 Fall 2014 course text), Chapter 3, Sections 3.1-3.2, printed pages 52-59 (PDF pages 55-62): the quadratic natural Lagrangian about a minimum and the generalized symmetric eigenproblem"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
    - title: "G. Teschl, Ordinary Differential Equations and Dynamical Systems (AMS Graduate Studies in Mathematics 140, online edition), Section 6.7, Theorem 6.16: a fixed point of Newton's equation is stable if the potential has a local minimum there"
      url: "https://schecter.math.ncsu.edu/ma_732_sp13/teschl_ode.pdf"

  precheck: pass
---
1. $K$ is symmetric and non-negative, and the small-oscillation model at
$q_{0}$ of clause (d) of [[def-cm09-coupled-quadratic-model]] — the coupled
quadratic model with mass metric matrix $M_{0}$, stiffness $K$ and zero
generalized force, in the displacement coordinates $\xi=q-q_{0}$ — is an
oscillatory model in the sense of clause (e) of that definition;
2. the linearization of $G$ at $(q_{0},0)$ is
$$DG(q_{0},0)(\delta q,\delta v)=\bigl(\delta v,\,-M_{0}^{-1}K\,\delta q\bigr)\qquad(\delta q,\delta v\in\mathbb R^{n}),$$
and for a curve $\xi:J\to\mathbb R^{n}$ of class $C^{2}$ on an interval $J$
with nonempty interior the following are equivalent: $\xi$ is a motion of the
small-oscillation model, $M_{0}\xi''+K\xi=0$ on $J$, and $t\mapsto(\xi(t),\xi'(t))$
solves the linear system $y'=DG(q_{0},0)y$ on $J$;
3. the motions of the small-oscillation model are exactly the modal
superpositions of clause 2 of [[pthm-cm09-free-modes-and-degeneracy]] with
every modal stiffness $\lambda_{i}\ge0$: there are no exponential
pure-$\lambda$ families, every oscillatory frequency is
$\omega_{i}=\sqrt{\lambda_{i}}$, and the pure-$\lambda$ motions of a
degenerate stiffness are the solutions of $q''+\lambda q=0$ with values in
the intrinsic subspace $N_{\lambda}=M_{0}^{-1/2}\{u:M_{0}^{-1/2}KM_{0}^{-1/2}u=\lambda u\}$;
4. $(q_{0},0)$ is a Lyapunov stable equilibrium of the nonlinear mechanical
system: for every $\varepsilon>0$ with
$\overline B((q_{0},0),\varepsilon)\subseteq\Omega\times\mathbb R^{n}$ there
is $\delta>0$ such that every maximal solution of $z'=G(z)$ with
$\lVert z(0)-(q_{0},0)\rVert_{2}<\delta$ exists for all $t\ge0$ and stays
within distance $\varepsilon$ of $(q_{0},0)$.
No asymptotic attraction, no all-time validity of the linear model as an
approximation of the nonlinear motion, and no measured frequency is asserted.
[F3] Model dictionary ([[def-cm09-coupled-quadratic-model]] clauses (b), (d),
(e)): the small-oscillation model at $q_{0}$ is the coupled quadratic model
with mass metric $M_{0}$, stiffness $K$ and $J\equiv0$ in the coordinates
$\xi$; its motions are the $C^{2}$ curves with $M_{0}\xi''+K\xi=0$, and its
phase system is $y'=Ay$ with $A=\begin{pmatrix}0&I_{n}\\-M_{0}^{-1}K&0\end{pmatrix}$;
it is oscillatory when $K$ is non-negative, and its modal vocabulary is that
of the definition's clause (e) with $M=M_{0}$.
3.1 Clauses 1-4 are proved. Clause 4 is a nonlinear conclusion obtained from exact energy conservation, not from the linearization; clauses 2 and 3 describe the linearized model only, and no accuracy of that model as an approximation of the nonlinear motion, and no attraction, is claimed here. [step 1.1, step 1.2, step 2.1, step 1.3] ∎

FILE pthm-cm09-linearization-error-bound
[F6] Model dictionary ([[def-cm09-coupled-quadratic-model]] clauses (b), (d)):
the small-oscillation model at $q_{0}$ is the coupled quadratic model with
mass metric matrix $M(q_{0})$ and stiffness $D^{2}U(q_{0})$, whose phase
system is $y'=Ay$ with the displayed block matrix
$A=\begin{pmatrix}0&I_{n}\\-M(q_{0})^{-1}D^{2}U(q_{0})&0\end{pmatrix}$; a
$C^{2}$ curve $\xi$ is a motion of it if and only if
$M(q_{0})\xi''+D^{2}U(q_{0})\xi=0$, with the datum read off $(\xi(0),\xi'(0))$.

Assume the Axiom of Countable Choice $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$
([[def-countable-choice]]), used only through the integrals of
[[pthm-cm10-kinetic-splitting]]; the theorems below neither use nor imply any
other choice principle. Adopt a model of the rigid-material branch
[[post-cm10-rigid-material]] with the external-torque branch
[[post-cm11-torque-balance]]: a reference mass distribution $\nu$ with total
mass $m$ and centred reference coordinates, a time window $I_0$
([[def-interval]]), an inertial frame, a motion $x(t,\rho)=c(t)+R(t)\rho$ with
configuration path $(c,R)$ of class $C^{2}$
([[def-cm10-rigid-body-configuration]]), body angular velocity $\omega$
([[def-cm10-angular-velocity]]) and body inertia matrix $I$
([[def-cm10-inertia-tensor]]), and a continuous external torque datum
$\tau:I_0\to\mathbb R^{3}$ with respect to which the adopted balance of
[[post-cm11-torque-balance]] clause (iii) holds along the motion. Then:
**Given:** The model data and motion of the statement, the balance hypothesis $L_c'=\tau$ of [[post-cm11-torque-balance]] clause (iii), and the derivative of the configuration path.
[L7] Frame changes: the description of the motion in the second frame has configuration path $(\tilde c,\tilde R)$ with $\tilde R(t')=QR(t'-b)$, body angular velocity $\tilde\omega(t')=\omega(t'-b)$, and inertial quantities $\tilde L_c(t')=Q L_c(t'-b)$ ([[pthm-cm10-frame-covariance]] clauses 1, 2 and 5); differentiation of a composition with the affine reparametrisation $t\mapsto t'-b$ and the constant rotation $Q$ follows the chain rule ([[thm-chain-rule-for-total-derivatives]]).
4.1 Applying step 2.1 in the second frame (whose configuration path is $(\tilde c,\tilde R)$ of class $C^{2}$ by [L7]) gives $I\tilde\omega'+\tilde\omega\times I\tilde\omega=\tilde R^{\mathsf T}\tilde\tau$ on $I_0+b$; and $\tilde R(t')^{\mathsf T}\tilde\tau(t')=R(t'-b)^{\mathsf T}Q^{\mathsf T}Q\tau(t'-b)=R(t'-b)^{\mathsf T}\tau(t'-b)$ because $Q^{\mathsf T}Q=I_{3}$ [L3], so the body-frame torque is unchanged; this is clause 5. [step 1.2, step 2.1, given, L3, L7, algebra] ∎

  def-cm01-units: formulation-prerequisite
  def-interval: mathematical-premise
  def-metric-ball: mathematical-premise
provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "Conditional statements about the torque-free branch of a nondegenerate rigid-material model of [[post-cm10-rigid-material]]: for every principal axis $e_j$ and every real rate $\\Omega$ there is a motion whose orientation path is a uniform rotation with constant body angular velocity $\\Omega e_j$; for $\\Omega\\neq0$ the material axis $R(t)e_j$ is fixed in the inertial frame and the motion is a rotation about a fixed spatial direction; the invariants take the values $C=\\Omega^{2}I_j^{2}$, $E=\\tfrac12\\Omega^{2}I_j$; and conversely, a torque-free motion with constant body angular velocity must spin about a principal axis. The statements carry the countable-choice assumption used only to select the initial unit quaternion, assert no measurement, no empirical law and no claim that any apparatus has been observed."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "D. Tong, Classical Dynamics (Cambridge lecture notes, January 2015 printing), Section 3.4.1, printed pages 55-57 (PDF pages 61-63): the free symmetric top rotating about its symmetry axis and the motion of the body axis under a constant angular velocity"
      url: "https://www.davidtong.org/pdfs/teaching/classical-dynamics/clas.pdf"
    - title: "K. K. Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics, Part CM: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), Chapter 4 Section 4.4, Eqs. (4.63)-(4.71), chapter pages 9-12 (PDF pages 63-66): free symmetric-top precession about the fixed angular-momentum direction and the uniform-rotation special case"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "I. W. Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09 Advanced Classical Mechanics, Fall 2014 course text), Chapter 2 Sections 2.4-2.5, printed pages 41-51 (PDF pages 44-54): Euler equations, principal-axis spins and the linear treatment of their stability"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
    - title: "G. J. Sussman and J. Wisdom, Structure and Interpretation of Classical Mechanics, 2nd ed. (MIT Press, 2014), Sections 2.8.2-2.9: uniform spins about principal axes and the evolution of the free rigid body"
      url: "https://mitp-content-server.mit.edu/books/content/sectbyfn/books_pres_0/9579/sicm_edition_2.zip"
verification:
  precheck: pass
---
1. **Existence of the uniform rotation.** There is a path
   $R:I\to SO(3)$ of class $C^{1}$ with $R(t_{0})=R_{0}$ and
   $$R(t)^{\mathsf T}R'(t)=[\Omega e_{j}]_\times\qquad(t\in I),$$
   that is, with constant body angular velocity $\omega\equiv\Omega e_{j}$
   ([[def-cm10-angular-velocity]] clause (a)).
2. **It is a torque-free motion.** For every such path $R$ and every curve
   $c:I\to\mathbb R^{3}$ of class $C^{2}$, the map
   $x(t,\rho):=c(t)+R(t)\rho$ is a motion of the torque-free model of the
   branch: its angular momentum about the centre of mass is
   $$L_c(t)=\Omega I_{j}\,R(t)e_{j},$$
   it is constant on $I$, and the adopted balance $L_c'=0$ holds. (Here $R$
   is automatically of class $C^{2}$, because $R'=R[\Omega e_{j}]_\times$ is
   the product of the $C^{1}$ curve $R$ with a constant matrix, so the
   configuration path $(c,R)$ has the regularity required of a motion.)
3. **Geometry and invariants.** In the motions of clause 2 one has
   $l=I\omega=\Omega I_{j}e_{j}$ constant,
   $$C=\lVert L_c\rVert_2^{2}=\Omega^{2}I_{j}^{2},\qquad E=\tfrac12\omega^{\mathsf T}I\omega=\tfrac12\Omega^{2}I_{j};$$
   if $\Omega\neq0$, the material axis $R(t)e_{j}$ is **fixed in the inertial
   frame**, $R(t)e_{j}=L_c/(\Omega I_{j})$ for every $t$, so the motion is a
   uniform rotation about the fixed spatial direction $L_c/|L_c|$; if
   $\Omega=0$, then $R$ is constant on $I$.
4. **Converse classification.** Conversely, let a motion of a nondegenerate
   torque-free model have **constant** body angular velocity
   $\omega\equiv\omega_{0}$ on its time window. Then
   $I\omega_{0}=\lambda\omega_{0}$ for some $\lambda\in\mathbb R$: either
   $\omega_{0}=0$ and the orientation path is constant, or $\omega_{0}\neq0$
   and $\omega_{0}$ lies along a principal axis; in the latter case an orthonormal principal basis can be chosen with one vector $e_j=\omega_0/\|\omega_0\|_2$ and $\Omega=\|\omega_0\|_2\ne0$, and the conclusions of clauses 2 and 3 hold in that adapted basis. It can be made positively oriented by negating a different basis vector. No alignment with the initially fixed basis is asserted when eigenvalues repeat.
2.1 Let $R$ be a path as in clause 1 and let $c:I\to\mathbb R^{3}$ be of class $C^{2}$. Its body angular velocity is the constant $\omega\equiv\Omega e_{j}$ by step 1.1, so $l=I\omega=\Omega I_{j}e_{j}$ is constant, and by [L1] $L_c=Rl$; with [L4], $(Rl)'=R'l+Rl'=R[\omega]_\times l+0=R(\omega\times l)=R(\Omega^{2}I_{j}\,e_{j}\times e_{j})=0$ by [L5]. Moreover $R'=R[\omega]_\times$ is of class $C^{1}$ because $R$ is and $[\omega]_\times$ is constant, so $R$ is of class $C^{2}$ by definition of second differentiability and $(c,R)$ is a configuration path of class $C^{2}$; hence $x(t,\rho)=c(t)+R(t)\rho$ is a motion of the model. Since $L_c$ is constant, the zero-torque balance $L_c'=0$ holds and the motion belongs to the torque-free branch; this is clause 2. [step 1.1, given, L1, L4, L5, algebra]
4.1 For the converse, let a torque-free motion have constant body angular velocity $\omega\equiv\omega_{0}$. By [L6] its body angular velocity satisfies $I\omega'+\omega\times I\omega=0$; with $\omega'=0$ this reads $\omega_{0}\times I\omega_{0}=0$, so $I\omega_{0}$ is a real multiple of $\omega_{0}$ by alternation [L5]: if $\omega_{0}\neq0$ there is $\lambda\in\mathbb R$ with $I\omega_{0}=\lambda\omega_{0}$, so $\omega_{0}$ is an eigenvector of $I$. Within its eigenspace choose the unit vector $e_j=\omega_0/\|\omega_0\|_2$. Starting with the original principal vectors in that eigenspace, subtract their projection onto $e_j$, and normalize a nonzero remainder if the eigenspace has dimension greater than one; subtract the projections onto both selected vectors and normalize a nonzero remainder if its dimension is three. Such remainders exist because the original vectors span the eigenspace; linear combinations remain eigenvectors for the same eigenvalue, and the projection subtractions give orthonormality by [L5]. Retain the original orthonormal vectors in the other eigenspaces. This supplies an adapted orthonormal principal basis directly in dimension three. Negating one of the other two basis vectors if necessary makes it positively oriented. In this adapted basis $\omega_0=\Omega e_j$, $\Omega=\|\omega_0\|_2\ne0$, without a simple-spectrum hypothesis; if $\omega_{0}=0$, then $R'=R[0]=0$ and $R$ is constant by [L4]. In the nonzero case the path satisfies the identity of clause 1 with rate $\Omega$, so clauses 2 and 3 apply to it. This is clause 4. [step 2.1, given, L5, L6, algebra] ∎

The statement assumes the Euler-angle description as a hypothesis on the
interval: no claim is made that every motion of the branch admits such a
description, that a description extends across the poles $\theta\in\{0,\pi\}$
(where the sleeping-top and hanging configurations lie), or that the
description is unique. On every subinterval on which a description with
$\theta\in(0,\pi)$ exists, the conclusions hold there. The statement asserts
no friction or driving torque at the pivot, no measurement and no empirical
value.
[L1] Branch kinematics: the pivot material point $\rho_{p}=-d e_{3}$ satisfies $c(t)+R(t)\rho_{p}=0$, so $c(t)=d\,R(t)e_{3}$; the pivot reaction contributes no torque about the origin; the gravitational torque about the origin is $\tau_{p}=mc\times(-ge_{3})$; and the adopted balance is $J_{p}'=\tau_{p}$ with $J_{p}=\int x\times\partial_{t}x\,d\nu$ ([[post-cm11-heavy-top]] clauses (iii)-(v), [[def-cm10-rigid-body-configuration]], [[def-cm10-angular-velocity]]).
- **The role of the chart.** The Euler-angle description is a hypothesis;
  the chart is singular at the poles $\theta=0,\pi$, where the sleeping top
  and the hanging configuration lie, and the statement says nothing there.
  The definitions of $a$, $b$, $E$ and $U$ depend on the chosen description
  (the angles are determined only up to the chart's ambiguities), while the
  physical conclusions of clause 3 are invariants of the motion.

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing
regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Let $H$ be the Cartesian Hamiltonian and
let, for a generalized-coordinate map $X:\Omega\to Q$ with substituted
Lagrangian $\widetilde L$, kinetic metric
$G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$ and substituted potential
$\widetilde U(t,q)=U(t,X(q))$, the symbol $\widetilde H$ denote the
Hamiltonian in the coordinates $X$ ([[def-cm12-hamiltonian]],
[[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]). Let $J\subseteq I_{0}$ be a
nonempty open interval and let $x:J\to Q$ be a $C^{2}$ curve with
$p(t):=\mathsf Mx'(t)$; write $K(t)=\tfrac12\,x'(t)\cdot\mathsf Mx'(t)$ for
the kinetic energy and $E(t):=K(t)+U\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)$ for the mechanical
energy along $x$ ([[def-cm03-centre-of-mass-and-momenta]],
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]]). Then:
1. **(the Hamiltonian is the energy)** For every $(t,x,p)\in I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$,
   $H(t,x,p)=\tfrac12\,p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p+U(t,x)$; consequently along every
   $C^{2}$ curve $x:J\to Q$ one has
   $H\bigl(t,x(t),p(t)\bigr)=\tfrac12\,x'(t)\cdot\mathsf Mx'(t)+U\bigl(t,x(t)\bigr)=E(t)$
   for every $t\in J$: on the Cartesian chart the Hamiltonian of the
   presentation is the model's mechanical energy.
   the substituted-chart Hamiltonian of the induced configuration curve is
   the same mechanical energy of the model.
[F1] Model, Lagrangian and energy data ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[def-cm03-centre-of-mass-and-momenta]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $L=K-U$ with $\mathsf M$ symmetric positive definite, $U$ of class $C^{2}$ of energy dimension, the residual forces of the standing regularity, and $K(t)=\tfrac12\sum_{i}m_{i}\lvert x_{i}'(t)\rvert_{2}^{2}$ along a curve family; dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]].

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing
regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Let $H$ be the Cartesian Hamiltonian of
[[def-cm12-hamiltonian]], let $X:\Omega\to Q$ be a generalized-coordinate map
with substituted Lagrangian $\widetilde L$, kinetic metric
$G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$, substituted potential
$\widetilde U(t,q)=U(t,X(q))$ and residual generalized force
$Q^{\mathrm{res}}(t,q,v)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}d(t,X(q),DX(q)v)$, and let
$\widetilde H$ be the Hamiltonian in the coordinates $X$
([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]],
[[def-cm12-hamiltonian]]). Let $J\subseteq I_{0}$ be a nonempty open interval
and let $x:J\to Q$ be a $C^{2}$ curve, with $p(t):=\mathsf Mx'(t)$
([[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]). Then:
4. **(constraint elimination)** Assume in addition that $d\equiv0$ and that
   $X$ is a parametrization of a scleronomic regular holonomic-constraint
   locus in the sense of [[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]
   clause (d), with the ideal-reaction law of
   [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]]. Then the equivalent conditions of
   clause 3 with $Q^{\mathrm{res}}\equiv0$ hold for $q$ if and only if
   $x=X\circ q$ is a constrained motion of the model with an ideal reaction
   ([[pthm-cm07-lagrangian-reduction-of-ideal-constraints]]).
[F1] Model and Lagrangian data ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]]): the force map has the standing regularity, the presentation has $F_{i}=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U+d_{i}$ with $U$ of class $C^{2}$, and the mechanical Lagrangian is $L=K-U$ with $\mathsf M$ symmetric positive definite; a motion of the model is defined by $m_{i}\ddot x_{i}=F_{i}(t,x,\dot x)$ ([[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]); dimensions are read by the cited units item.
[F7] Constraint elimination ([[pthm-cm07-lagrangian-reduction-of-ideal-constraints]], [[def-cm06-holonomic-constraint-model]], [[post-cm06-ideal-reaction-law]]): for a scleronomic regular holonomic-constraint datum and a parametrization of the constrained configurations, a curve $x=X\circ q$ is a constrained motion with ideal reaction if and only if $\Lambda(\widetilde L)(q)=Q^{\mathrm{res}}(t,q,q')$ along $q$.

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing
regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Let $X:\Omega\to Q$ be a
generalized-coordinate map with substituted Lagrangian $\widetilde L$ and
kinetic metric $G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$
([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]). Then:
1. **(velocity Hessian)** For every $(t,x,v)\in I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$
   one has $L_{v}(t,x,v)=\mathsf Mv$ and $L_{vv}(t,x,v)=\mathsf M$, and for
   every $(t,q,v)\in I_{0}\times\Omega\times\mathbb R^{r}$ one has
   $\widetilde L_{v}(t,q,v)=G(q)v$ and $\widetilde L_{vv}(t,q,v)=G(q)$. Both
   matrices are symmetric and positive definite; consequently the Cartesian
   presentation and the presentation in the coordinates $X$ are
   velocity-regular at every state in the sense of [[def-cm12-regularity]].
[F1] Model and Lagrangian data ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $L=K-U$ is of class $C^{2}$ on the open set $I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, the velocity gradient and Hessian are formed with the Euclidean partial derivatives of [[def-jacobian-matrix-and-gradient]] and [[def-directional-and-partial-derivatives]], $K'(v)=\mathsf Mv$, the mass operator $\mathsf M$ is a symmetric positive definite linear map of $\mathbb R^{3N}$, and the presentation $(U,d)$ has $U$ of class $C^{2}$ with the standing regularity of the force branch; dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]].
1.1 The set $I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is open, and $L$ is a $C^{2}$ map on it by [F1]; hence $(I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N},L)$ is a fibre-full time-dependent Lagrangian chart in the sense of [F5]. By [F1] one has $L(t,x,v)=\tfrac12v\cdot\mathsf Mv-U(t,x)$ with $\mathsf M$ symmetric positive definite and $U$ of class $C^{2}$, so $L$ is the mechanical chart Lagrangian of [F2] with the data $G:=\mathsf M$, $A:=0$, $V:=U$. [F1, F5, algebra]

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data and conventions of [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]: masses
$m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$, open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$,
time window $I_{0}$, mass operator $\mathsf M$
([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential
$U:I_{0}\times Q\to\mathbb R$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing
regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]). Let $X:\Omega\to Q$ be a
generalized-coordinate map with substituted Lagrangian $\widetilde L$,
kinetic metric $G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$ and substituted
potential $\widetilde U(t,q)=U(t,X(q))$
([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]]), and let $H$ and
$\widetilde H$ be the Hamiltonians of [[def-cm12-hamiltonian]]. Then:
1. **(Cartesian chart)** The fibre momentum map
   $\mathbb F_{t,x}(v)=\mathsf Mv$ is a bijection of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ onto
   $\mathbb R^{3N}$ for every $(t,x)\in I_{0}\times Q$, and the inverse
   Legendre branch is the $C^{2}$ map
   $v(t,x,p)=\mathsf M^{-1}p$ on the whole phase chart
   $I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$. Hence the presentation is
   hyperregular on the Cartesian chart in the sense of
   [[def-cm12-hyperregularity]], and the Hamiltonian of the branch is
[F1] Model and Lagrangian data ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]]): $L(t,x,v)=\tfrac12v\cdot\mathsf Mv-U(t,x)$ with $\mathsf M$ a symmetric positive definite linear map of $\mathbb R^{3N}$ and $U$ of class $C^{2}$; the standing regularity of the force branch holds; dimensions are read by [[def-cm01-units]].
1.1 By [F1] one has $L(t,x,v)=\tfrac12v\cdot\mathsf Mv-U(t,x)$ with $\mathsf M$ symmetric positive definite and $U$ of class $C^{2}$, so $L$ is the mechanical chart Lagrangian of [F2] with the $C^{2}$ data $G:=\mathsf M$, $A:=0$, $V:=U$; [F2] therefore gives that $(t,x,v)\mapsto(t,x,\mathsf Mv)$ is a $C^{2}$ bijection of $I_{0}\times Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ onto itself with $C^{2}$ inverse $v(t,x,p)=\mathsf M^{-1}p$, and that the Hamiltonian of the branch is $H(t,x,p)=\tfrac12p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p+U(t,x)$, of energy dimension by [F1]. [F1, F2, algebra]

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data, charts and Hamiltonian field of [[def-cm13-two-form]],
[[def-cm13-hamiltonian-field]]: masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$ and open
configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ with mass operator
$\mathsf M$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]]), time window $I_{0}$, $C^{2}$
potential $U$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing regularity
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-units]]), the Cartesian
Hamiltonian $H(t,x,p)=\tfrac12p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p+U(t,x)$ and, for a
generalized-coordinate map $X:\Omega\to Q$ with kinetic metric
$G=DX^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX$, the chart Hamiltonian
$\widetilde H(t,q,P)=\tfrac12P\cdot G(q)^{-1}P+\widetilde U(t,q)$
([[def-cm12-hamiltonian]], [[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]],
[[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]],
[[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]]). Let $J\subseteq I_{0}$ be a nonempty open
interval. Then:
[F1] Model and motions ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]], [[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]): the force branch has the standing regularity, a motion is a curve with $m_{i}\ddot x_{i}=F_{i}(t,x,\dot x)$, and on the potential branch $F_{i}=-\nabla_{x_{i}}U+d_{i}$ with the residual forces of the standing regularity.
2.2 Clause 4, necessity: suppose the forced field equals $X_{f}$ for a $C^{2}$ function $f$ on a phase domain containing the chart; comparing first components gives $f_{p}(t,x,p)=\mathsf M^{-1}p$, so $\nabla_{p}\bigl(f-\tfrac12p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p\bigr)=0$; for each fixed $(t,x)$ the map $p\mapsto f(t,x,p)-\tfrac12p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p$ has vanishing total derivative on the convex open set $\mathbb R^{3N}$, so by [F4] it is constant, and evaluating at $p=0$ gives $f(t,x,p)=\tfrac12p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p+g(t,x)$ with $g(t,x):=f(t,x,0)$ of class $C^{2}$ by [F4]; comparing second components gives $-\nabla_xU+d=-\nabla_xg$, hence $d(t,x,v)=-\nabla_x(g-U)(t,x)$ for every state, so the asserted velocity-independent potential is $V:=g-U$. For any fixed admissible $V$, two phase functions producing this same field have difference with both spatial and momentum gradients zero; by the componentwise constancy argument of [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]] clause (c), that difference is a $C^2$ function of $t$ on each connected component of $Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$, namely each $C\times\mathbb R^{3N}$; the model's forced equation is then $\mathsf Mx''=-\nabla_{x}(U+V)(t,x)$, the equation of the potential presentation with potential $U+V$ and vanishing residual forces. [step 1.5, F1, F2, F4, algebra]

---

physical_scope: "Phase-volume conservation for the declared potential presentation: the top form $\\omega^{3N}/(3N)!$ is nowhere zero and is preserved by every time slice of the $C^{2}$ branch Hamiltonian evolution, and the Lebesgue measure of every canonical chart is preserved on bounded Borel sets of the actual flow domain, also when the Hamiltonian depends on time. The statement is a conditional deduction from the adopted force branch, the declared presentation and the cited mathematics, and in its measure-theoretic part it uses the Axiom of Countable Choice exactly where the cited change-of-variables theorem requires it; no empirical content is asserted."

## Statement
Assume the Axiom of Countable Choice, $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$
([[def-countable-choice]]), where the change-of-variables theorem below
requires it. Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and
mechanical Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of
[[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the momentum charts and
two-form datum of [[def-cm13-two-form]], the canonical coordinates of
[[def-cm13-canonical-form]], the canonical maps of
[[def-cm13-canonical-map]] and the flow of [[pthm-cm13-flow-preservation]]:
$H:I_{0}\times W\to\mathbb R$ of class $C^{2}$ with
$H(t,x,p)=\tfrac12p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p+U(t,x)$ on the Cartesian phase domain
$W=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ ([[def-cm12-hamiltonian]],
[[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]],
[[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]],
[[def-cm01-units]]). Write $n:=3N$ and let
- **Volume form versus coordinate measure.** $\Omega$ and the coordinate top
  form $dx^{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge dp_{3N}$ are both nowhere-zero top forms on
  a connected canonical chart, hence differ by a nowhere-zero continuous
  factor; clause 1 says the flow preserves the former and clause 2 that it
  preserves the latter, so the invariant phase volume does not depend on the
  chosen density representative.

id: pthm-cm13-two-form-is-symplectic

title: "The two-form of a potential presentation is closed and nondegenerate, and each generalized-coordinate momentum chart carries the same symplectic structure: $\\Phi_X^*\\lambda=\\sum P_a\\,dq^a$ and $\\Phi_X^*\\omega=\\sum dq^a\\wedge dP_a$"

## Statement
Adopt the force model with potential presentation $(U,d)$ and mechanical
Lagrangian $L=K-U$ of [[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]] with the
data, charts and forms of [[def-cm13-two-form]]: masses $m_{1},\dots,m_{N}>0$,
open configuration domain $Q\subseteq\mathbb R^{3N}$ and mass operator
$\mathsf M$ ([[def-cm01-configuration]]), time window $I_{0}$, $C^{2}$
potential $U$ and residual forces $d$ of the standing regularity
([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-units]]), the Cartesian phase
domain $W=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ with $n=3N$, its canonical one-form
$\lambda$ and two-form $\omega=-d\lambda$, and for each generalized-coordinate
map $X:\Omega\to Q$ with $r$ coordinates and kinetic metric $G$ the chart map
$\Phi_{X}(q,P)=(X(q),\mathsf M\,DX(q)G(q)^{-1}P)$ with pullbacks
$\lambda_{X}=\Phi_{X}^{*}\lambda$ and $\omega_{X}=\Phi_{X}^{*}\omega$
([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]],
[[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]], [[def-cm12-hyperregularity]]). Then:
[F1] Model, presentation and charts ([[def-cm07-action-and-mechanical-lagrangian]], [[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]], [[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]], [[post-cm02-force-superposition]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm01-units]], [[def-cm12-cotangent-momentum]], [[def-cm12-hyperregularity]]): the force branch has the standing regularity, $Q$ and $\Omega$ are nonempty open sets, the mass operator $\mathsf M$ is symmetric positive definite, $U$ is of class $C^{2}$ and $d$ is a force field of the standing regularity; $X$ is a $C^{3}$ immersion with $G(q)=DX(q)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf M\,DX(q)$ symmetric positive definite, and the momentum datum in the coordinates $X$ is $P=G(q)v$, so that the chart map $\Phi_{X}$ of [[def-cm13-two-form]] is well defined.
1.1 The set $W=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$ is a nonempty open subset of $\mathbb R^{2n}$ with $n=3N\ge1$, hence a phase domain, and by [F2] its two-form is $\omega=\sum_{a}dx^{a}\wedge dp_{a}$, exactly the standard two-form of $W$ in the sense of [F3]; therefore [F3] gives $d\omega=0$, nondegeneracy of $\omega$ at every point of $W$, and the symplectic-manifold property of $(W,\omega)$: clause 1 holds. [F2, F3, given]
3.1 The image $\Omega\times\mathbb R^{r}$ of the coordinates $(q,P)$ is a phase domain: $\Omega$ is nonempty and open by [F1] and $r\ge1$, so $\Omega\times\mathbb R^{r}$ is a nonempty open subset of $\mathbb R^{2r}$. By step 2.1 the form $\omega_{X}$ equals the standard two-form of that phase domain, which by [F3] is closed and nondegenerate; therefore $\Phi_{X}^{*}\omega=\omega_{X}$ is closed and nondegenerate, and $\Phi_{X}$ preserves the two-form wherever it is defined, which proves clause 2. When $r=3N$ the derivative of $\Phi_{X}$ is invertible by [F1] and [F5], and the identities describe a change of canonical coordinates on the overlap of two open charts. [step 2.1, F1, F3, F5]
4.1 For two generalized-coordinate charts $X_{1},X_{2}$ whose chart maps $\Phi_{X_{1}},\Phi_{X_{2}}$ are local diffeomorphisms (the case $r=3N$) and whose images overlap, the transition $\Theta:=\Phi_{X_{2}}^{-1}\circ\Phi_{X_{1}}$ is defined on a nonempty open set and, by functoriality of pullback [F4] and the identities of steps 1.2 and 2.1 applied to each chart, $\Theta^{*}\omega_{X_{2}}=\omega_{X_{1}}$: the transition preserves the standard two-form. Consequently the difference $\lambda_{X_{1}}-\Theta^{*}\lambda_{X_{2}}$ has exterior derivative $\omega_{X_{1}}-\Theta^{*}\omega_{X_{2}}=0$ and is closed, so the one-form datum is determined up to the closed gauge $\lambda\mapsto\lambda+dF$; this proves clause 3. [step 2.1, step 3.1, F4] ∎

physical_scope: "Action-angle coordinates for a completely integrable datum of a declared hyperregular potential presentation of the framework's force branch: near a compact connected regular fibre and over a ball of regular values on which the integral map is a proper submersion with connected fibres, there are period-one angle and action coordinates of the presentation's two-form with $\\omega=\\sum_i d\\theta_i\\wedge dI_i$; fibrewise-constant functions, in particular the datum and the branch Hamiltonian, depend on the actions alone; and the model's motions become the constant-action, linear-angle flows. The conclusion is conditional on the Axiom of Countable Choice, on the complete integrability of the datum and on all the fibre hypotheses; it is local over the ball of regular values, asserts no global coordinates on the phase domain, and carries no empirical content. The identification of the coordinates with classical period integrals is a normalization convention, not part of the theorem."

([[def-cm01-configuration]]), $C^{2}$ potential $U$ and residual forces $d$
of the standing regularity ([[def-cm03-potential-and-mechanical-energy]],
[[post-cm02-force-superposition]]), the presentation being hyperregular on
its charts ([[pthm-cm12-mechanical-hyperregularity]],
[[def-cm12-hamiltonian]]). Fix one momentum chart of the presentation
(Cartesian coordinates $(t,x,p)$, or the chart $(t,q,p)$ of a
generalized-coordinate map $X:\Omega\to Q$
([[def-cm07-generalized-coordinates-and-forces]])), with $m$ pairs of
coordinates and Hamiltonian $H$ of class $C^{2}$ on
$J_{0}\times V\times\mathbb R^{m}$; let $S:J\times U\times A\to\mathbb R$
be a $C^{3}$ complete integral of the model's Hamilton-Jacobi equation at
$(t_{0},q_{0},\alpha_{0})\in J\times U\times A$ in the sense of

[L1] Model and reduction ([[post-cm05-central-model]] clauses (i)-(ii), [[pthm-cm05-relative-reduction]] clauses 2-4, [[def-cm-central-force-trajectory]], [[def-cm01-configuration]], [[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]): the model has no external forces, its relative coordinate is a $C^2$ central-force trajectory for $(\mu,u)$ with $|r(t)|\in J_0=(0,\infty)$, and the map $x\mapsto(X,r)$ is a bijection from motions of the model onto pairs (affine $X$ with $X''=0$, central-force trajectory $r$ with values in $J_0$), with $x_1=X+\frac{m_2}{M}r$, $x_2=X-\frac{m_1}{M}r$, $M=m_1+m_2$.
2.1 Clause 1: [L2] gives that $r$ extends uniquely to a central-force trajectory $\bar r:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R^3$ with $\rho\ge r_*$, $\rho(t)>r_*$ for $t\ne t_*$, $\rho$ strictly decreasing on $(-\infty,t_*]$ and strictly increasing on $[t_*,\infty)$, and the quadrature identity. For the model statement, let $X$ be the centre of mass of the original motion, affine on $J_0$ with $X''=0$ by [L1] clause 2, and let $\bar X$ be its affine extension to $\mathbb R$; then by [L1] clause 4 the pair $(\bar X,\bar r)$ reconstructs a motion of the model on all of $\mathbb R$ with relative coordinate $\bar r$ and centre of mass $\bar X$, because $|\bar r(t)|\in J_0$ for all $t$ (so the configuration remains admissible) and the force presentation is the same. This is clause 1. [step 1.1, L1, L2, L5, algebra]

([[def-countable-choice]]), as used by the transport and divergence theorems.
**Adopt the smooth open-flow model** with the following model data
([[def-cm-continuum-motion]], [[def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus]],
[[post-cm01-worldlines]], [[def-cm01-units]]): a nonempty open interval $I$;

a motion $\chi$ of $B$ on $I$ of class $C^2$ whose time slices are
diffeomorphisms with positive Jacobian onto their images; a density field
$\rho>0$ of class $C^1$ and a spatial velocity field $u$ (the velocity of
$\chi$); a stress field $\sigma$ of class $C^1$
([[def-cm-traction-and-stress]]); and a continuous body-force density $b$;
all fields defined on a neighbourhood of the swept region over a compact
subinterval $J_0$ with $\overline{J_0}\subseteq I$. Adopt as the model's
**material balances**: for every material subregion $B'\subseteq B$ (a
nonempty bounded open subset with $\overline{B'}\subseteq B$ whose images
$\chi_t(B')$ are bounded $C^1$ domains,
[[def-bounded-c-one-domain-boundary-charts-and-outward-normal]]) and every
$t\in J_0$,
[L1] Localization of the material balances ([[thm-cm-local-mass-balance]] clause (i), [[thm-cm-local-momentum-balance]], [[def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus]], [[def-cm-traction-and-stress]]): if the material mass balance holds for the inverse images of all sufficiently small current balls at each $t\in J_0$, as included in the adopted smooth admissible subregion class, then $\partial_t\rho+\operatorname{div}(\rho u)=0$ on $J_0\times\chi_t(B)$; and if moreover the material momentum balance holds for each admissible pair of a time and a material subregion whose current image is a bounded $C^1$ domain, then $\rho D_tu=\rho b+g$ there, where $g$ is the row divergence of $\sigma$. Both require the stated regularity and the countable-choice assumption.

FILE pthm-cm16-energy-stability-and-instability
---
id: pthm-cm16-energy-stability-and-instability
kind: physics-theorem
title: "Energy stability and instability of the mechanical model at an equilibrium: the strict minimum is Lyapunov stable, a negative Hessian eigenvalue gives linear instability, a strictly concave potential gives nonlinear instability, and exact energy conservation rules out attraction"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 12
deps: [def-cm16-stability-and-hyperbolicity-concepts,
       def-cm16-flow-equilibria-and-invariant-sets,
       thm-cm-energy-stability-of-a-strict-minimum,

  def-cm16-flow-equilibria-and-invariant-sets: formulation-prerequisite
  thm-cm-energy-stability-of-a-strict-minimum: mathematical-premise
  prop-cm-linearization-of-the-mechanical-system-at-an-equilibrium: mathematical-premise
  thm-cm-instability-of-a-hyperbolic-saddle: mathematical-premise
  lem-cm-energy-conservation-for-the-mechanical-system: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-mechanical-vector-field-and-energy-in-a-chart: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-equilibrium-and-lyapunov-stability: mathematical-premise
  def-metric-ball: mathematical-premise
  def-euclidean-inner-product: mathematical-premise
  def-hessian-and-euclidean-critical-point: mathematical-premise
  cor-hessian-is-symmetric: mathematical-premise

provenance:
  statement: ai-altered
  proof: ai-altered
proof_strategy: direct
physical_scope: "The stability consequences of the mechanical chart data at a mechanical equilibrium q0: a strict local minimum of the potential gives a Lyapunov stable equilibrium; a negative Hessian eigenvalue gives an unbounded solution of the linearised variational equation (linear instability, with no nonlinear conclusion); a negative definite Hessian (strict local maximum) makes the equilibrium unstable in the nonlinear sense through the hyperbolic instability theorem; and exact energy conservation along the model's motions prevents asymptotic stability whenever states of different energy accumulate at the equilibrium, so a strict minimum is stable but not attracting. No global stability, no asymptotic stability and no statement about degenerate (zero-eigenvalue) Hessians is claimed."
sources:
  references:
    - title: "Iain Stewart, Advanced Classical Mechanics (MIT 8.09/8.309, Fall 2014), Chapter 5 pp. 60-103 and §7.3 pp. 164-172: linearised stability about an equilibrium, the corrected epsilon-delta definitions, and the energy argument for a strict minimum"
      url: "https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/8-09-classical-mechanics-iii-fall-2014/d9bac33f6c60b304dc0398e99b327102_MIT8_09F14_full.pdf"
    - title: "Konstantin Likharev, Essential Graduate Physics: Classical Mechanics (title page last edited September 1, 2026), §§3.2 and 5.6: equilibrium types and the warning that precise Lyapunov, asymptotic and linear notions are distinct"
      url: "https://essentialgraduatephysics.org/Files/CM%20merged.pdf"
    - title: "Gerald Sussman and Jack Wisdom, Structure and Interpretation of Classical Mechanics, 2nd ed., §4.3: fixed points, stable and unstable sets, and the warning that a saddle Hessian requires a nonlinear theorem before an instability claim"
      url: "https://mitp-content-server.mit.edu/books/content/sectbyfn/books_pres_0/9579/sicm_edition_2.zip"
---
1. **(Strict minimum.)** If $q_0$ is a strict local minimum of $U$, then
   $(q_0,0)$ is a Lyapunov stable equilibrium of $G$
   ([[def-cm-equilibrium-and-lyapunov-stability]],
   [[def-cm16-stability-and-hyperbolicity-concepts]] clause (i)).
2. **(Linear instability from a negative eigenvalue.)** If $K$ has a negative
   eigenvalue ([[def-eigenvalue-eigenvector-eigenspace-and-spectrum]]), then
   there are $\mu>0$ and $w\ne0$ in $\mathbb R^n$ such that
   $t\mapsto e^{\mu t}(w,\mu w)$ is a solution of the variational equation
   $Y'=DG(q_0,0)Y$ along the constant solution $z\equiv(q_0,0)$
   ([[def-variational-equation-along-an-ode-solution]]); in particular
   $(q_0,0)$ is linearly unstable in the sense of
   [[def-cm16-stability-and-hyperbolicity-concepts]] clause (iii).
3. **(Nonlinear instability from a strictly concave potential.)** If $K$ is
   negative definite, then every complex eigenvalue of $DG(q_0,0)$ is real
   and nonzero, at least one is positive, and $(q_0,0)$ is unstable: it is
   not Lyapunov stable.
4. **(No attraction under exact energy conservation.)** The energy $E$ is
   constant along every maximal solution of $G$. If there are states
   $z=(q,v)$ with $\lVert z-(q_0,0)\rVert_2$ arbitrarily small and
   $E(z)\ne E(q_0,0)$, then $(q_0,0)$ is not asymptotically stable. In
   particular, if $q_0$ is a strict local minimum of $U$, then $(q_0,0)$ is
   Lyapunov stable but not asymptotically stable.
5. **(Scope.)** No statement is made about a degenerate Hessian $K$ (some
   eigenvalue $0$), and clause 2 gives no nonlinear conclusion when $K$ has
   both a negative and a positive eigenvalue: the linearisation then also has
   purely imaginary eigenvalues and is not hyperbolic, so the instability
   theorem used in clause 3 does not apply. No global stability, no basin of
   attraction and no statement about the size of any neighbourhood is
   claimed.

FILE pthm-cm16-hyperbolic-linearization
1. **(Exponential dichotomy.)** If no complex eigenvalue of $A$
   ([[def-eigenvalue-eigenvector-eigenspace-and-spectrum]]) has real part
   $0$ --- that is, if $z_*$ is hyperbolic in the sense of
   [[def-cm16-stability-and-hyperbolicity-concepts]] clause (iv) --- then
   there are $A$-invariant subspaces $E_s,E_u\subseteq\mathbb R^{6N}$ with
   $\mathbb R^{6N}=E_s\oplus E_u$ and real constants $C\ge1$, $c>0$ such
   that, with the Euclidean norm of [[def-euclidean-inner-product]] and the
   exponential series $e^{tA}$:
   $$\lVert e^{tA}v\rVert_2\le Ce^{-ct}\lVert v\rVert_2\ (v\in E_s,\ t\ge0), \qquad \lVert e^{tA}v\rVert_2\ge C^{-1}e^{ct}\lVert v\rVert_2\ (v\in E_u,\ t\ge0),$$
   $$\lVert e^{tA}v\rVert_2\le Ce^{ct}\lVert v\rVert_2\ (v\in E_u,\ t\le0),$$
   and $E_s=\{v:e^{tA}v\to0\ (t\to+\infty)\}$,
   $E_u=\{v:e^{tA}v\to0\ (t\to-\infty)\}$. The spaces $E_s,E_u$ are the
   **stable and unstable subspaces** of the linearised flow.
2. **(Stable case.)** If every complex eigenvalue of $A$ has negative real
   part, then $z_*$ is locally exponentially asymptotically stable: there are
   $\delta>0$, $\beta\ge1$, $\gamma>0$ such that every maximal solution $z$
   of the model with $\lVert z(0)-z_*\rVert_2<\delta$ is defined for all
   $t\ge0$ and satisfies
   $$\lVert z(t)-z_*\rVert_2\le\beta e^{-\gamma t}\lVert z(0)-z_*\rVert_2\qquad(t\ge0).$$
   In particular $z_*$ is Lyapunov stable and asymptotically stable
   ([[def-cm-equilibrium-and-lyapunov-stability]]).
3. **(Saddle case.)** If no complex eigenvalue of $A$ has real part $0$ and
   at least one has positive real part, then $z_*$ is unstable: there is
   $\varepsilon_0>0$ such that for every $\delta>0$ there are a state $z_0$
   with $\lVert z_0-z_*\rVert_2<\delta$ and a time $t_1>0$ such that the
   maximal solution through $z_0$ is defined on $[0,t_1]$ and
   $\lVert z(t_1)-z_*\rVert_2=\varepsilon_0$.
4. **(Rates, charts and scope.)** The constants $C,c,\beta,\gamma$ and the
   subspaces depend on $A$ and on the chart scale; the rates $c,\gamma$ have
   dimension $\tau^{-1}$ and the chart dependence of the eigenvalues is that
   recorded in [[def-cm16-stability-and-hyperbolicity-concepts]] clause
   (vi)(b). No conclusion is asserted when some eigenvalue has zero real part
   (the centre case), and no statement is made about the size of the stable
   set, the basin of attraction, uniformity in a family of models or any
   empirical realization.
- **Reading the dichotomy physically.** The subspace $E_s$ consists of the tangent directions whose linearised deviations from the equilibrium decay exponentially, and $E_u$ of those that grow exponentially; the dichotomy quantifies the separation of nearby initial states only at the linear level. For the nonlinear model, clause 2 upgrades the stable case to local exponential attraction and clause 3 upgrades the saddle case to genuine nonlinear instability.

FILE pthm-cm16-recurrence-for-finite-volume-models
Assume the Axiom of Countable Choice $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$
([[def-countable-choice]]), inherited from the phase-volume preservation
theorem [[pthm-cm13-liouville]] (its measure-preservation clause uses the
change-of-variables theorem under that assumption). Adopt the force model with
potential presentation and the Hamiltonian data of [[pthm-cm13-liouville]]:
time-independent potential $U:Q\to\mathbb R$ with residual forces
$d\equiv0$, Cartesian phase domain $W=Q\times\mathbb R^{3N}$
([[def-cm13-two-form]]), Hamiltonian
$$H:I_0\times W\to\mathbb R,\qquad H(t,x,p)=\tfrac12p\cdot\mathsf M^{-1}p+U(x),$$
phase-volume form $\Omega=\omega^{\wedge3N}/(3N)!$, and local flow
$\Phi_t=\Phi(t,\cdot)$ on the neighbourhood $U_0$ of
[[pthm-cm13-flow-preservation]]. Let $A\subseteq U_0$ be a bounded Borel set
([[def-borel-sigma-algebra]]) with $\overline A\subseteq U_0$, and suppose
that the flow is defined on $A$ for all real times and maps $A$ onto itself:
$$\Phi_t(A)=A\qquad(t\in\mathbb R),$$
so that $A$ is invariant in the sense of
[[def-cm16-flow-equilibria-and-invariant-sets]] clause (iv). Then
$\lambda_{6N}(A)<\infty$, and for every Borel set $E\subseteq A$ almost every
$x\in E$ with respect to the Lebesgue measure $\lambda_{6N}$ of the chart
([[def-lebesgue-measure-and-the-lebesgue-sigma-algebra]]) has
$$\Phi_n(x)\in E\qquad\text{for infinitely many }n\ge1.$$
The time unit is the frame time $[t]=\tau$ ([[def-cm01-units]]), so
"infinitely many $n$" means infinitely many returns at unit time steps.

FILE pthm-cm16-separatrix-splitting-and-horseshoe
**(i) The model.** Fix a length scale $\ell_0>0$, a mass $m>0$, a frequency
scale $\omega_0>0$, a dimensionless amplitude $a\ge100$, and a pulse width
$\delta>0$. Let $w$ be the normalized smooth bump and $\beta_\delta$ the
smooth $P$-periodic pulse of [[def-smooth-periodic-oscillator-pulse-model]]
with $P:=\pi/2+\delta$, $\beta_\delta=0$ on $[0,\pi/2]$ and
$\beta_\delta(t)=\delta^{-1}w((t-\pi/2)/\delta)$ on $[\pi/2,P]$. The
**physical pulsed oscillator model** is the one-label model on the
configuration domain $Q:=\mathbb R$ with fixed mass $m$ and the
$T_p$-periodic force map
$$F(T,Q):=-m\omega_0^2\Bigl[Q+\ell_0\,\beta_\delta(\omega_0T)\Bigl(a-\frac{Q^2}{\ell_0^2}\Bigr)\Bigr], \qquad T_p:=\frac{P}{\omega_0},$$
that is, a model of [[post-cm02-force-superposition]] with
$m\ddot Q=F(T,Q)$ and $F(T+T_p,Q)=F(T,Q)$; its motions are the $C^2$ curves of
[[def-cm02-newtonian-flow]]. Its canonical momentum is
$\Pi=m\dot Q$ and its stroboscopic map at period $T_p$ is the map $\Psi_\delta$
of [[def-cm16-stroboscopic-and-poincare-maps]] clause (i).
- **Why this is a chaos proof and not a picture.** The conjugacy to the shift, the mixing, the density of periodic points, the sensitivity and the transverse homoclinic points are all inferred from the supplier theorem's explicit constructions (a contraction on a complete sequence space, invariant cones with expansion, and weighted graph-transform curves), not from a plotted tangle; the physical transfer uses only a linear change of scale. See [[def-cm16-chaos-list-for-model-maps]] clause (d).

FILE pthm-cm16-variational-equation-and-linearization


FILE pthm-cm17-elastic-plane-wave-speeds
[F2] Plane-wave theorem ([[thm-cm-plane-elastic-waves]], [[def-cm-linear-elastic-constitutive-law]]): for $\xi(t,x)=a\cos(\langle k,x\rangle-\omega t)$ with $\omega\ne0$, the identity $\rho_0\partial_t^2\xi_i=\sum_j\partial_j\sigma_{ij}$ holds on $\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^3$ for the stress $\sigma=\lambda(\operatorname{tr}e)I_3+2\mu_s e$ if and only if (D) holds; and for $a\ne0$ parallel to $k$ one has $\rho_0\omega^2=(\lambda+2\mu_s)|k|_2^2$, while for $a$ orthogonal to $k$ one has $\rho_0\omega^2=\mu_s|k|_2^2$.
**Proof technique:** direct; identify the branch's displacement equation with the stress-divergence identity of the cited plane-wave theorem, then read off the two polarization branches and their positivity.
1.1 The field $\xi$ is $C^\infty$ on $\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^3$ and the branch's displacement equation (E) of [F1] with $b\equiv0$ has right-hand side $\mu_s\Delta\xi+(\lambda+\mu_s)\nabla(\operatorname{div}\xi)$, which is exactly the divergence of the branch's stress $\sigma=\lambda(\operatorname{tr}e)I_3+2\mu_se$ as computed by [F1]; the cited plane-wave theorem [F2] therefore gives that (E) holds on all of $\mathbb R\times\mathbb R^3$ if and only if (D) holds. [given, F1, F2]

Assume the Axiom of Countable Choice $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$
([[def-countable-choice]]). Adopt the incompressible Newtonian fluid branch
and the periodic-cell idealization of [[post-cm17-fluid-constitutive]]
(clauses (a) and (c)) with constant density $\rho>0$, dynamic viscosity
$\eta>0$ and the stress field $\sigma=-pI_3+\eta(Du+Du^{\mathsf T})$
([[def-cm-fluid-constitutive-models]]), and let $\nu:=\eta/\rho$. Let $s\ge4$
be an integer and let $u_0$ be a smooth $\mathbb T^3$-periodic divergence-free
velocity field with $Y_0:=\|u_0\|_{H^s}<+\infty$ in the conventions of
[[def-periodic-vector-sobolev-space-and-leray-projection]], and let
$T>0$ be the time of [[thm-local-smooth-periodic-euler-viscous-solution]],
$T=(2C_sY_0)^{-1}$ if $Y_0>0$ and $T=1$ if $Y_0=0$. Then:
**(i) Reduction.** With the body force absent ($b\equiv0$), the field
equations of the branch — the continuity equation (C) of
[[pthm-cm17-continuity-equation]] with $\rho$ constant,
$\operatorname{div}u=0$, and the momentum equation (M) of
[[pthm-cm17-momentum-equation]] with this stress field — are equivalent, for
smooth periodic fields with mean-zero pressure, to the projected equation
hold. Equivalently, the pair $(u,P_{\mathrm m})$ with model pressure
$P_{\mathrm m}:=\rho p$ satisfies the momentum equation (M) with $b\equiv0$
and stress $\sigma=-P_{\mathrm m}I_3+\eta(Du+Du^{\mathsf T})$.

The theorem is a conditional identity of the model; it asserts no solution existence, no error bound for the small-displacement replacement and no measured modulus."

Assume the Axiom of Countable Choice $\mathrm{AC}_\omega$
([[def-countable-choice]]) and the model of [[pthm-cm17-momentum-equation]]:
mass balance and the balance principles (F) and (T) of
[[post-cm17-momentum-balance]], with the stress matrix of
[[pthm-cm17-stress-matrix-and-symmetry]], so that the momentum equation
holds on the current regions. Suppose the model lies in the small-displacement
isotropic linear elastic branch of
[[post-cm17-linear-elastic-constitutive]]: the stress is
$\sigma=\lambda(\operatorname{tr}e)I_3+2\mu_s e$ with
$e=\tfrac12(D\xi+D\xi^{\mathsf T})$, the density is the constant reference
density $\rho_0>0$, the spatial velocity is $u=\partial_t\xi$ and the material
acceleration is $D_tu=\partial_t^2\xi$, where $\xi:I\times O\to\mathbb R^3$ is
of class $C^2$ with continuous second spatial derivatives and the material and
spatial variables are identified to first order as stated in that postulate.
Let $b$ be the body-force field of that model.
**Proof technique:** direct; write the momentum equation in the branch's material description and compute the divergence of the linear elastic stress with the commuting mixed partials.
1.2 Compute the divergence of the stress: from $\sigma_{ij}=\lambda(\operatorname{tr}e)\delta_{ij}+2\mu_s e_{ij}$ of [F2], the product rule of [F4] gives $\sum_j\partial_j\sigma_{ij}=\lambda\,\partial_i(\operatorname{tr}e)+2\mu_s\sum_j\partial_j e_{ij}$. Since $\operatorname{tr}e=\sum_j\partial_j\xi_j=\operatorname{div}\xi$ by [F4], the first term is $\lambda\,\partial_i(\operatorname{div}\xi)$. For the second, $2\sum_j\partial_j e_{ij}=\sum_j\partial_j(\partial_j\xi_i+\partial_i\xi_j)=\Delta\xi_i+\partial_i(\operatorname{div}\xi)$, where the interchange of $\partial_j$ and $\partial_i$ on $\partial_i\xi_j$ uses the commuting mixed partials of [F3]. Hence $g_i=\mu_s\Delta\xi_i+(\lambda+\mu_s)\partial_i(\operatorname{div}\xi)$. [given, F2, F3, F4, algebra]
- **Divergence of a symmetric matrix.** The row divergence used in (M)
  coincides with the column divergence because $\sigma$ is symmetric (by
  [[pthm-cm17-stress-matrix-and-symmetry]], or directly by construction in
  this branch), so writing $\operatorname{div}\sigma$ in (E) is unambiguous.

FILE pthm-cm17-stress-matrix-and-symmetry
---
id: pthm-cm17-stress-matrix-and-symmetry
kind: physics-theorem
title: "Existence, uniqueness and symmetry of the stress matrix from the adopted balance principles"
status: draft
origin: session
domain: physics
dependency_level: 13
deps: [post-cm17-momentum-balance, post-cm17-contact-force-model, post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model, def-cm17-traction-and-stress, def-cm17-density-and-mass, def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units, thm-cm-traction-is-given-by-a-stress-matrix, thm-cm-stress-matrix-is-symmetric, def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus, def-bounded-piecewise-c-one-euclidean-domain, def-bounded-c-one-domain-boundary-charts-and-outward-normal, def-cm-continuum-motion, def-ck-euclidean-maps-and-diffeomorphisms, def-countable-choice]

  post-cm17-contact-force-model: physical-assumption
  post-cm17-continuum-kinematic-model: physical-assumption
  def-cm17-traction-and-stress: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm17-density-and-mass: formulation-prerequisite
  def-cm17-continuum-body-and-units: formulation-prerequisite
  thm-cm-traction-is-given-by-a-stress-matrix: mathematical-premise
  thm-cm-stress-matrix-is-symmetric: mathematical-premise
  def-cm-euclidean-spatial-calculus: mathematical-premise
